### MINI-119 follow-up chain — SuperMoto shipped end-to-end, then NPC ragdoll, Koss purchase, world population, and zone/tuning fixes (2026-08-28)

- Date: 2026-08-28 (long single-session continuation, following directly on from the "SuperMoto rider mounting confirmed working" entry above it in `Docs/CURRENT.md`)
- Owner: released
- Full detail: `git log --grep=MINI-119` on this repo — every item below has its own commit with the user's verbatim request, root-cause reasoning, and what was/wasn't verified. This entry is a compact index, not a substitute.
- **NPC ragdoll-on-hit** (`adbbf6e`): user, "if i hit the npc they will behave like ragdoll... yes they can get back up but if there health is 0 then they fade after lying down for 3 seconds. if not then they walk... you can do it will the police, villagers, gang members" (explicitly excluding shopkeepers/dealers/mission NPCs). New `NpcRagdoll.cs` wraps the existing generic `SacatRagdollBuilder`; `NpcCombatHealth.Hit()`/`Update()`/`ResetForRespawn()` branch on whether a ragdoll is present — fatal hits lie 3s then scale-fade and deactivate, non-fatal hits lie 2s then recover and resume walking; NPCs with no ragdoll keep the original animation-only reaction unchanged. Wired onto all three existing `NpcCombatHealth` sites (Police, Not Ah Word roster, Dog Life) plus newly extended to Villager-role patrol NPCs (previously had no combat health at all). Verified via Unity batch mode (`Mini119NpcRagdollCheck.cs`) simulating both the fatal and non-fatal `Hit()` paths directly.
- **Koss bike purchase** (`a71742f`): user, "make the bike like you can buy it from the car dealer, it will be the cheapest bike... it will be called a Koss", price confirmed at $2,500. The ~360-line SuperMotoWRagdoll wiring block inside `SpawnStockDemoBikeAndDisableOurCharacter` was extracted verbatim into a new shared `VehicleSpawnController.WireSuperMotoInstance(instance, player, spawnPos, autoMount)` so a real Car Dealer purchase gets the exact same proven setup (VehicleSeat/VehicleRider/pillion/hand-foot-lock/wheelie assist) as the dev spawn, not a second drifting copy. `SpawnPurchasedVehicle` gets a new `"koss"` branch (parked, not auto-mounted, walk up + press F like every other vehicle). Verified via batch mode: purchase produces a fully-wired `PlayerKoss`, `HasRider=false` immediately after purchase, a second purchase attempt returns the already-own message with no duplicate spawned.
- **More villagers + Lalay/Highland rezone, three corrections in a row** (`a674d1b`, `59c1b5e`, `8d89f92`, `a791c0a`): user asked for more villagers on both Lalay and Highland, then iteratively corrected the zone boundary three times as they clarified their own intent — "highland starts after the bridge, lalay is the entire road" → "lalay ends by the car dealer" (discovered the Car Dealer sits on a different road branch than the farm cluster, so `AreaNameDisplay`'s nearest-circle-wins logic needed care to avoid the farm misreading as Lalay) → final: "dog life block should be where the lalay road starts". Each iteration was verified by calling `AreaNameDisplay.ResolveArea` directly via reflection against ~9 real landmark coordinates (`Mini119ZoneResolveCheck.cs`), not worked out by hand — this caught one real miss (LalayHouse resolving to no zone at 70m when the circle needed 85m). Final state: single Lalay circle from the Dog Life block to the Car Dealer; Highland stays a tight circle on the farm/safehouse cluster only, starting at the bridge (`Bridge_03_user_highland_lalay_inroad`). 7 new Villager NPCs added along both stretches via a new additive tool (`Mini119AddMoreVillagers.cs`) — ground-snapped by raycast, not `Terrain.SampleHeight`, since this scene has no Unity Terrain component at all (confirmed by survey, ground is collider-based).
- **Farm hedge containment fix** (`30580af`): user, "i dont want people through the farm are where the hedge is inside." One of the villagers just added (`NPC_Villager_HighlandFarm`) and one of its own patrol waypoints were confirmed sitting INSIDE the Highland farm's hedge enclosure via a new geometric containment check (`Mini119FarmHedgeCheck.cs`, reconstructs the hedge rectangle from the rear hedge's own transform). Moved outside to the farm's entrance gap; re-ran the check afterward and confirmed zero NPCs/waypoints inside anywhere in the scene.
- **Wheelie back-clip damping** (`ec4b8bf`): user, "the back goes through the handlebar so the back should stay outward while wheelieing." Measured first (`Mini119WheelieBackClipCheck.cs`, simulates `VehicleRider`'s seated-vs-wheelie pose math and estimates chest-to-handlebar distance for both) before touching anything — the position/pitch numbers turned out NOT to be the cause (chest moves AWAY from the handlebar during a wheelie by that math, 0.62m -> 0.99m); the real cause is `BikeRiderAnimation`'s authored wheelie-overlay clip, tuned against the TMAX's handlebar geometry, reshaping the torso independently of the position blend. Per the user's own choice between two offered options, dialed `BikeRiderAnimation.WheelieClipWeight` down from its default 0.45 to 0.22 while mounted on the SuperMoto specifically (that component lives on the shared player object, also used by the TMAX, so the original value is cached on `Mount()` and restored on `Dismount()` rather than changed permanently). Verified the mount/dismount swap via batch mode; the exact right weight still needs the user's own visual confirmation.
- **Auto-mount disabled for handoff** (`b4d5f15`): user, "dont automount the character on the bike because we are dont with this but you can leave the bike spawning for future testing." `AutoMountSuperMotoOnSpawn` flipped `true` -> `false`; the dev bike spawn itself (`StockDemoBikeTestMode`) is untouched and still spawns the parked, walk-up-and-F-mountable SuperMoto every Play session.
- Files changed (new): `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Combat/NpcRagdoll.cs`; roughly a dozen new one-off `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini119*.cs` diagnostic/patch tools (kept per this project's established convention of not deleting them after use — see any of them for the exact reasoning/measurements behind each fix). Files changed (modified): `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Combat/NpcCombatHealth.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/{VehicleSpawnController,SuperMotoVehicleInteractable}.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs` (Koss shop entry, Villager ragdoll wiring, final zone numbers — kept in sync with the live-scene patch tools so a future full rebuild doesn't regress any of this), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity`.
- Verification: every item above was checked via a dedicated Unity batch-mode tool against the real `GrandBayProof.unity` scene (not just compiled) before being committed; a fresh Windows build was produced and handed to the user after each meaningfully-testable change. **Honest limitation, same class as every earlier entry in this task**: none of this round has been hands-on played by the user yet except via their own live direction mid-session (e.g. the three zone-boundary corrections, the wheelie-damping option choice) — the ragdoll fade/recover timing, the Koss's actual ride feel, the new villagers' patrol routes, and whether 0.22 is the right wheelie-overlay weight all still need a real playtest.
- Also asked mid-session, NOT yet started (deliberately, pending scoping): combat mechanics — user wants "fighting first, then shooting after"; follow-up narrowed the first target to fixing the existing warped-punch-pose/over-generous-hit-detection bugs (not new combos) per the user's own choice. User also asked whether to buy a paid or free fighting animation pack; recommended paid (a small, real-mocap unarmed/boxing pack, e.g. "Punch Perfect: Boxing Animations & Tools" — 123 mocap animations + Animator Controller + character controller — over a free pack, since free packs are the single most common source of exactly the "warped pose" symptom already on record, and this project has already paid a real cost in debugging time for animation/rig problems elsewhere, e.g. the MINI-107 LOD failures). No purchase has been made; this needs the user's own approval before spending money, per standing project convention.
- Next action: user hands-on playtests this build (ragdoll fade/recover, Koss purchase and ride, new villagers, zone labels, wheelie feel with the damped overlay, auto-mount now off) and reports back; only then should the combat bug-fix pass (MINI-120, unclaimed) begin, following the same reproduce-first discipline as every fix in this entry — do not start it from the vague "fighting mechanics" framing alone.

### MINI-119 follow-up — Motorbike Physics Tool integration: full revert, then a scoped re-integration (SuperMoto riding + ported TMAX wheelie/trike + a new continuous roll-corrector)

- Date: 2026-08-23/24
- Owner: released
- Request (verbatim, across the session): integrate the purchased Motorbike Physics Tool asset's SuperMoto bike; get its wheelie working with the front wheel genuinely leaving the ground; stop it crashing at spawn and leaning/toppling during normal riding or after hitting a ledge/hill; when integration attempts kept failing in real play despite passing automated tests, the user ordered a **full revert** ("put my game back how it was before the motor bike asset"); after review, the user asked to retry with a narrower, explicit design: SuperMoto's own riding/physics for normal control, but TMAX's own proven kinematic `ApplyWheelie`/`ApplyTrikeStabilizers` ported over just for the wheelie, plus a new continuous "return to 0 degree lean" counter-force for normal riding, explicitly asked to be kept separate from the wheelie system.
- **Revert**: `git reset --hard` to `268431c` (the last commit before the asset was ever touched), full state tagged `mini119-motorbike-asset-attempt` for recovery. Confirmed clean: `Assets/MotorbikePhysicsTool/` (213 files) gone, `TmaxBikeController`/`TmaxBikeControllerCustom` back to their pre-asset state, working tree clean.
- **Re-integration** (`e72f625`): restored the tagged SuperMoto assets/scripts, then re-applied the one fix that was proven correct from real evidence (the user's own `Player.log`) but had never been committed before the revert: `RB_Controller` has `[RequireComponent(typeof(Input_Manager))]`; the old spawn code destroyed the stock (LeftCtrl/LeftShift) `Input_Manager` while it was the ONLY one present, so Unity silently blocked the destroy (`"Can't remove Input_Manager (Script) because RB_Controller (Script) depends on it"`, logged not thrown) - the stock manager therefore survived and was what every `GetComponent<Input_Manager>()` actually resolved to, so E/Q never reached the wheelie. Fixed by adding the E/Q remap FIRST, then destroying the stock one (now safe, since the remap satisfies the requirement). `Mini119RealSceneWheelieTest` now asserts on this directly (`Input_Manager` count == 1, and it's the remap) so this exact failure mode can't silently regress again.
- **Ledge-lean bug** (`1f8c7e5`): user reported the bike getting visually stuck leaned after a steep-ledge hit. Found the same `&&`-binds-tighter-than-`||` operator-precedence bug already fixed once in `AutoLeveling.AutoLevel` (MINI-119 batch, above) present again, unfixed, in the vendor's own `RB_Controller.AddTorque`'s lean-input gate - past `maxLeanAngle` (45deg default) on one side, player steering input silently stopped applying any correcting torque at all, on the ground or not, until roll drifted back under 45deg by itself. Fixed the parenthesization.
- **Continuous roll-corrector** (`1cdba6b`, `5004058`) - the user's explicit, repeated ask ("a counter force seperate for the superassist to bring it bck to 0 degrees lean that it constantly checks for... strictly the normal riding system"), scoped away from the wheelie entirely: new `SuperMotoUprightAssist`. Two torque-based attempts (copying `TmaxBikeController`'s own proven spring-damper-via-`AddTorque` upright-assist) were built and **rejected after real testing**: a gain strong enough to win against a sustained lean (e.g. resting against a slope) resonated into a violent tumble (40+ rad/s from a dead stop, zero input); a gain gentle enough to stay stable at rest was too weak and the lean crept upward anyway while stuck. A controlled A/B test (identical ledge-hit scenario, component removed entirely) proved the torque approach was ITSELF the source of both failure modes - the base bike alone already settles cleanly to true upright. Replaced with the same technique already proven reliable for the kinematic wheelie: every physics step, directly steer the Rigidbody's rotation toward a zero-roll target via `MoveRotation` at a fixed max degrees/second, preserving current pitch/yaw - no torque, no gain to tune, cannot resonate. `AutoLeveling` (the vendor's own competing roll-correction) is disabled so nothing fights this for the same axis.
- **Self-reinforcing stuck-pitch bug, found and fixed same session** (`5004058`): the new corrector measured pitch each frame via `Mathf.Asin(forward.y)` (fold-back-prone past 90deg) and then baked that measured value directly into the bike's real rotation via `MoveRotation` every frame - so one bad reading during a hard sustained turn didn't stay a glitch, it became the new "current pitch" the same flawed formula would faithfully re-measure and re-apply forever, turning a measurement error into a genuine, persistent, self-reinforcing stuck pitch (constant 73deg for 6+ seconds in testing). Fixed by replacing the pitch measurement with a signed angle around the flattened-forward-derived right axis (same principle already used for roll/yaw elsewhere in the file) - confirmed the stuck plateau is gone; pitch now moves naturally and settles near 0.
- Files changed: `Assets/MotorbikePhysicsTool/**` (restored from tag), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/{VehicleSpawnController,SuperMotoWheelieAssist,SuperMotoWheelieKeyRemap,SuperMotoTrikeStabilizer,SuperMotoWheelieHud,SuperMotoUprightAssist}.cs` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/{Mini119RealSceneWheelieTest,Mini119FrontWheelLiftTest,Mini119LedgeHillLeanTest,Mini119RampClimbLeanTest,Mini119UprightAssistIsolationTest}.cs`.
- Verification commands: `Mini119RealSceneWheelieTest.Run` (real `GrandBayProof.unity` scene, real spawn method via reflection), `Mini119FrontWheelLiftTest.Run` (real raycast-measured front-wheel ground clearance, not just a pitch angle), `Mini119UprightAssistIsolationTest.Run` (zero-input at-rest stability baseline), `Mini119LedgeHillLeanTest.Run` (kerb-height ledge hit + ramp, straight throttle), `Mini119RampClimbLeanTest.Run` (steep ramp climb, with and without sustained full-lock steering), `Mini119WheelieAssistTest.RunAutoRecoverTest`, `Mini065TmaxDropTest.Run` (original bike regression), full Windows player build.
- Verification results: `Input_Manager` count=1/is-the-remap confirmed; real-scene wheelie test reaches ~60-62deg pitch with roll staying at/near 0.0deg; front-wheel-lift test shows 2.35-2.75m of REAL measured front-wheel clearance, 83-100% of an 8s hold (`VERDICT: REAL WHEELIE`); at-rest isolation shows 0.0deg max roll / <1.1rad/s max angular speed; ledge-hit test settles to 0.0deg roll and holds it even while physically stuck against the ledge for 10+ seconds; ramp climb alone holds 0.0deg roll throughout; ramp climb with continuous full-lock steering (an extreme, sustained edge case) sees a transient max of 15.4deg roll but always recovers to <1deg, never stuck; auto-recover self-rights in 0.34s from a 90deg fallen+crashed state; original `TmaxBikeControllerCustom` drop test still PASS. Windows build succeeded each round.
- Known issues / NOT verified in Play Mode by a human on the FINAL state: every check above is batch-mode (`Physics.Simulate` + reflection-driven component invocation against the real scene/spawn method), which this exact task has already demonstrated can disagree with real gameplay (the `Input_Manager` destroy-order bug and the world-axis `FreezeRotationZ` constraint bug from earlier in this session were both invisible to batch tests and only found from the user's own `Player.log` or direct play feedback). The user has hands-on confirmed the wheelie itself works (front wheel visibly lifting, HUD showing `E held: YES`) and confirmed the pitch-lock/lean fix addressed what they were seeing, but has not yet given a final end-to-end sign-off specifically on this exact last build (`5004058`).
- Next action: user plays the current build, specifically tries to reproduce a stuck lean (hard hit, steep ledge, hill climb, combined climb+turn) and confirms it now self-corrects; report back anything that still doesn't recover cleanly.

### MINI-118 — Bike made a lot heavier to stop bump-launching, plus a second "extra air gravity" safety net

- Date: 2026-08-22
- Owner: Claude; released.
- Request, verbatim: "with the bike, I am fedup with this. try to make the bike heavier a lot heavier but still keep the same wheeling and leaning. because anytime i hit a slight bump on the road or on a hill or any bump the bike bumps too high or too much fix this. really fix this" — followed by: "or could there be a gravity added so when then bike reaches a certain height when it goes up from the ground if what you did doesnt work."
- **Why increasing mass is the physically correct fix, checked against the actual code rather than assumed**: `Mini064TmaxAssetPrep.cs` already derives the WheelCollider spring rate and damper FROM `BikeMassKg` (both scale linearly with mass), so a heavier bike keeps the exact same suspension sag depth and damping ratio - not a different feel, just a stiffer spring holding up more weight the same amount. Separately, a bump/road irregularity applies roughly a fixed physical impulse; for a fixed impulse, the resulting velocity change is inversely proportional to mass (Δv = J/mass) - a heavier bike is genuinely harder to launch from the same bump.
- **Why "keep the same wheeling and leaning" holds automatically**: read `TmaxBikeController.ApplyWheelie` before touching anything - the wheelie's pitch is fully kinematic (`Rigidbody.MoveRotation`/`MovePosition` about the rear contact patch), a deliberate "ROUND 14" design decision the code's own comments explain was chosen specifically because torque-based lifting never worked reliably. Mass cannot affect a kinematic rotation. The lean/stability corrections (`ApplyStability`, `ApplyDebugForcedPitch`, yaw assist) all use `Rigidbody.AddTorque(..., ForceMode.Acceleration)`, which Unity defines to ignore mass entirely (unlike `ForceMode.Force`). Both systems were left completely untouched.
- Increased `BikeMassKg` 220 -> 480 (~2.18x, deliberately above real-world TMAX weight). Scaled `motorTorque`, `brakeTorque` and `wheelieRearTorqueBoost` by the same ratio, since unlike the systems above, these ARE real mass-dependent WheelCollider forces - left unscaled, the heavier bike would have accelerated/braked/crept-forward-during-a-wheelie noticeably weaker as an unintended side effect nobody asked for.
- **Measured a real, if minor, side effect rather than assuming the fix was complete**: the drop test's REAL measured wheelie pitch (not the controller's own setpoint) dropped from an established 85.9deg baseline to 77.4deg - still above the test's own 76.6deg pass threshold, but with far less margin than before, most likely because the heavier bike's real WheelCollider traction curve isn't perfectly linear even with `motorTorque` scaled to match, shifting how much of the drop test's fixed duration lands inside the wheelie's speed-eligible window. Compensated by raising `wheelieRiseRate` 60 -> 72 and re-ran the SAME drop test to confirm, rather than assume the nudge worked: 89.6deg, matching/exceeding the original baseline; roll 2.7deg peak / 1.9deg settled (baseline ~2.4deg peak), comparable and fully recovering.
- **Second, independent safety net, per the user's own follow-up suggestion**: `TmaxBikeController.ApplyExtraAirGravity()` - once both wheels have been genuinely ungrounded for longer than a short grace period (0.18s, long enough that an ordinary single-frame bump never triggers it), extra downward acceleration ramps in over 0.25s to pull an unwanted launch back down faster. Explicitly gated off during `WheelieForcingPose` so it can never fight an intentional wheelie. Added proactively, not after a failed report - the user asked "if what you did doesn't work," and testing "how high does a bump actually launch it" isn't provable in this batch environment (Play Mode never ticks), so a second, independent, low-risk layer was added now rather than waiting for a round-trip.
- `Mini065TmaxValidation`'s plausible-mass range widened (100-400kg -> 100-900kg) to reflect that a real-world TMAX weight is no longer the design target, per the user's own explicit ask - documented as a deliberate change, not a loosened check.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini064TmaxAssetPrep.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/TmaxBikeController.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini065TmaxPhysicsTest.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini065TmaxValidation.cs`.
- Verification: clean compile; full vehicle-pipeline rebuild; drop test PASS (89.6deg real pitch, 1.9deg settled roll); `Mini065TmaxValidation` PASS; `Mini071RoverDriveTest`, `Mini070RoverValidation`, `Mini067ChainValidation`, `Mini058FactionsValidation`, `Mini100GrandBayMapValidation` all still PASS; Windows build succeeded; 12-second headless built-player smoke test showed no fatal errors.
- **Found but explicitly OUT OF SCOPE, not fixed here**: re-running the full standing suite surfaced `Mini069GangWarValidation` failing ("Zoomy is 22.4m from the recruiter - he is meant to stand next to him") - a file this packet neither touches nor reserves (NPC positioning, unrelated to bike physics). Almost certainly a validator stale relative to an earlier map migration rather than anything this task caused; recorded as a new blocker below for its own task.
- Known limitations / NOT verified in Play Mode: nobody has actually driven the heavier bike over a real bump or hill yet - the physics reasoning is sound and the drop test confirms wheelie/lean are preserved, but "does it feel planted now" is exactly the kind of claim this project's own standing limitation (Play Mode never ticks in this batch environment) cannot prove. The extra-air-gravity grace/ramp values (0.18s/0.25s) are a first reasonable guess, not tuned against real driving feel.
- Next action: user drives the heavier bike over real bumps/hills/ramps and confirms the launch behaviour actually improved, and separately confirms wheelie/lean still feel the same. If bumps still feel too bouncy, the next lever is loosening the extra-air-gravity grace period or raising its strength - not another mass increase.

### MINI-113 — World and navigation cleanup: road-junction smoothing, BikeHomePoint repair, vehicle minimap markers

- Date: 2026-08-22
- Owner: Claude; released.
- Request: per `Docs/CLAUDE-HANDOFF-CURRENT.md` section 6 — smooth road-ribbon intersection bumps, pull the farm hedge off the Highland road, add vehicle minimap markers, spawn vehicles road-safe.
- Measured every road-root pair before touching anything. Of the four junctions the map's own `Mini100GrandBayMapValidation.ValidateRoadJoin` actually tracks as real connections, only "Highland inroad to farm spur" had a genuine step - 0.286m, sitting right at that check's own 0.30m tolerance ceiling (passing the static gate while still reading as a real bump to drive over). Root cause: each road ribbon is height-graded independently against its own slope cap in the map-lab generator, with no shared constraint at the point two ribbons meet. Fixed with a localized post-migration mesh-vertex blend (`SmoothRoadJunctionHeight`) rather than reworking the deeper generator - confirmed 0.286m → 0.000m.
- **A more serious bug found along the way**: `BikeHomePoint` — the bike's save/load return point — was a standalone object never added to the migration's tracked-and-repositioned set, and measured 303.52m from the real, migrated `FarmSafehouse`. A bike returning home on load would have been stranded in the old world's empty space. Fixed the same way this file already repairs the player's own spawn point; confirmed 303.52m → 7.34m.
- **Self-correction, recorded honestly**: the farm hedge was first measured at 0.00m clearance to the road on all 5 segments using `Collider.ClosestPoint` - but that API is documented-unreliable on non-convex colliders, which road ribbons are, and was silently returning the query point itself regardless of true distance (confirmed when a hedge "pushed" 15m still read 0.00m). Re-measured against real mesh vertices and found genuine clearance of 0.92m-15.21m on every segment - the hedge was never actually on the road. Kept a defensive `ClearFarmHedgeFromRoads()` function anyway (confirmed a no-op against the real geometry) as a guard against a future regression.
- Added `MiniMapMarkerKind.Vehicle`; `VehicleSpawnController.SpawnPurchasedVehicle` now attaches a marker at spawn time (the vehicle doesn't exist at scene-build time, so the usual build-time marker wiring doesn't apply).
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini100GrandBayMapMigration.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/UI/GtaMiniMapMarker.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/VehicleSpawnController.cs`, new `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini113WorldCleanupValidation.cs`.
- Verification: clean compile; canonical rebuild; new focused `Mini113WorldCleanupValidation` PASS; standing regressions `Mini058FactionsValidation`, `Mini100GrandBayMapValidation`, `Mini095LalayMapLabSetup.BuildValidateCapture`, `Mini108/109/112`'s own validators all PASS; Windows build succeeded; 12-second headless smoke test clean.
- Known limitations / NOT verified: nobody has driven the smoothed junction or watched the bike return home in a real playthrough. No screenshots this round - every change is sub-metre geometry/position correction, not a new visible object.
- Next action: user drives the Highland farm spur junction, buys a vehicle and checks the minimap, and saves/reloads with an owned bike to confirm it returns correctly.

### MINI-117 — Fixed the recruiter regression (two real bugs), plus breeding hints

- Date: 2026-08-22
- Owner: Claude; released.
- Request: "fix the recruiter stuff and onetime" (following up on the `Mini058FactionsValidation` failure flagged in MINI-112), then a direct follow-up: "using what buttons are the mixed strains planted. give hint n instructions to the process."
- **Root cause #1 (validator gap, corrected from an earlier wrong guess in MINI-112's own writeup)**: `TownNPCInteractable.CanUseCurrentRole()` gates `NpcRole.GangRecruiter` on `ProgressionGate.IsMissionReached("M15")`, which reads `MissionSystem.Instance`. `Mini058FactionsValidation` initialized `EconomyManager`, `ProgressionManager` and `CharacterSwitchManager` before testing the recruiter, but never `MissionSystem` - so `Instance` stayed null, the gate always refused, and the real recruiter code was never actually broken. Fixed by initializing `MissionSystem` and advancing its simulated mission index to M15 (found by id, not hardcoded, since MINI-110/111 inserted several missions before it), plus giving the test the reputation the gate also requires - and correcting the test's own money-drop expectation, since M15's own objective is literally "TalkTo GangRecruiter," so the very first recruit interaction also completes M15 and pays out its reward in the same call.
- **Root cause #2 (a real bug, only exposed once fix #1 let the test run far enough to reach it)**: `GangMemberInteractable`'s Chevy recruitment path checked the COMBINED `ProgressionGate.CanRecruit` (mission-reached AND reputation) before its own, separate, more specific `reputation < recruitReputationThreshold` check - meaning that second check (and its clearer "You nuh have di respect yet" message) was dead code; any low-reputation refusal was always caught first by the generic gate's own message. Split the checks: this component now only gates on the mission being reached itself; the reputation threshold is checked once, in its own existing branch, restoring the intended message hierarchy.
- **Breeding hints added, per direct follow-up**: the breeding station's own prompt only ever said "[E] Interbreed strains," with no explanation of which two crops to hold or which number key plants the result. Added three `GameplayHintController` entries (fired the first time each hybrid mission's harvest objective becomes current): Purple Sugar needs Purple + Black Sugar, plant with `[7]`; Sugar Cheese needs Blue Cheese + Black Sugar, plant with `[9]`; Purple Cheese needs Blue Cheese + Purple, plant with `[0]`.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini058FactionsValidation.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Interaction/GangMemberInteractable.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/UI/GameplayHintController.cs`.
- Verification: clean compile; canonical scene rebuild; `Mini058FactionsValidation` PASS; every standing validator from MINI-108 through MINI-112 plus `Mini100GrandBayMapValidation` re-run PASS; Windows build succeeded; 12-second headless built-player smoke test showed no fatal errors.
- Known limitations / NOT verified in Play Mode: the new hint text has not been watched firing in a real playthrough - correct timing/wording is validator-confirmed via the mission/objective key match, not eyes-on. The recruiter/Chevy fix is likewise validator-confirmed, not hands-on played.
- Next action: user confirms recruiting (both the paid pool and Chevy) feels right, and that the three new breeding hints appear and read clearly when reaching each hybrid mission.

### MINI-112 — Dog Life escalation: jealousy, respawn cooldown, leash/return-to-block, occasional group walks

- Date: 2026-08-22
- Owner: Claude; released.
- Request: `Docs/WorkPackets/MINI-112.md`, per `Docs/CLAUDE-HANDOFF-CURRENT.md` section 6 — Dog Life becomes jealous as the boys' strains/stock grow; defeated members stay down ~100s; fighters stop chasing when the player is too far, return to their block and resume block behaviour; a small group occasionally walks Lalay; the whole pool must not be permanently active.
- **Real bug found and fixed, the core of the leash mechanism**: `FactionBrawler` and `PatrolNPC` both call `CharacterController.Move` on the same GameObject every frame with zero coordination between them — during any chase or fight, ambient wandering was contesting combat steering, and once a chase broke off there was nothing to actually walk the fighter back anywhere. Added `FactionBrawler.IsEngaged` (true while `_chaseTarget != null` or mid-swing); `PatrolNPC.Update()` now returns immediately while the sibling `FactionBrawler` is engaged. This single fix delivers "stop chasing... return to block and resume block behaviour" without any new steering code — `PatrolNPC`'s own waypoints already ARE the block behaviour; they just needed to stop being fought over.
- **Respawn cooldown**: `NpcCombatHealth.OnEnable()` previously called `ResetForRespawn()` unconditionally whenever a `despawnOnDefeat` character reactivated — meaning simply toggling the pool back on undid a defeat instantly regardless of elapsed time. Added `NpcCombatHealth.LastDefeatedAt` (set the moment a hit brings health to 0) and moved the reset decision to `RivalGangSpawner`, which now reactivates each pooled member individually only once `Time.time - LastDefeatedAt >= respawnCooldownSeconds` (100s default) — a member downed seconds ago stays gone while an untouched one still shows up immediately.
- **Jealousy dialogue**: one new Dog Life line reusing the existing `DialogueConditionType.CropUnlocked` (already true via either the pre-existing Boss-exploitation path or MINI-111's Rasta ladder) alongside `DogLifeRevealed` — no new condition type needed, and `SelectLine()`'s own "most specific wins" rule means it naturally supersedes the plain reveal line once eligible.
- **Occasional group walk**: `RivalGangSpawner` now periodically (every 75-150s while active) picks up to two members who are active, not `IsEngaged`, and not `IsDown`, and temporarily swaps their `PatrolNPC` waypoints for a longer road-direction route, restoring their original small loop afterward — deliberately simple (reusing the pool's own existing patrol component, not a new navigation system), matching the brief's own "within mobile simulation limits."
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Combat/FactionBrawler.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Combat/NpcCombatHealth.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Interaction/RivalGangSpawner.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Interaction/PatrolNPC.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`, new `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini112DogLifeEscalationValidation.cs`.
- Verification: clean compile; canonical scene rebuild; new focused `Mini112DogLifeEscalationValidation` PASS (engaged-state steering yield, cooldown-gated reactivation both ways, jealousy line present, group-walk picker correctly excludes an engaged member); Windows build succeeded; 12-second headless built-player smoke test showed no fatal errors.
- **Found but deliberately left unfixed, out of scope**: re-running `Mini058FactionsValidation` as part of this round's regression pass surfaced a failure — "paid recruit #1: expected a successful $2000 recruit line, got 'Keep doing your ting...'" — traced to `GangMemberInteractable`'s existing `GrandBayGangs` reputation gate firing before the cost check. None of this packet's touched files intersect with that code path, and it is not reserved here; recorded as a new blocker below rather than absorbed into this packet.
- Known limitations / NOT verified in Play Mode: no screenshot evidence this round — every change is behavioural/timing, not a new visible object, so a fixed-camera shot would show nothing the validator hasn't already proven numerically. The leash/return feel, the group-walk's naturalness, and the jealousy line's timing all need a human to watch a real Dog Life confrontation.
- Next action: user plays a real Dog Life confrontation before `MINI-113`. Separately, someone should investigate the `Mini058FactionsValidation` recruiter-gate failure as its own task.

### MINI-111 — Rasta's strain-mentorship ladder

- Date: 2026-08-22
- Owner: Claude; released.
- Request: `Docs/WorkPackets/MINI-111.md`, per `Docs/CLAUDE-HANDOFF-CURRENT.md` section 6 — Rasta stops asking for tomatoes and becomes the production/strain school: Bushers -> Black Sugar -> Purple -> Blue Cheese -> Purple Sugar (hybrid) -> Sugar Cheese -> Purple Cheese, each tier story-gated so the player cannot see/buy a locked strain early.
- **Why this matters beyond the literal ask**: the existing crop-unlock booleans (`BlackSugarUnlocked` etc.) are gated on `BossExploitationStage`/Boss-J reputation — a WeedRoute-only track (`M9W`-`M11W`). A Legitimate Farmer path player never sees those missions and previously had NO route to any strain past the basics. Rasta's ladder is now that route for both paths.
- Implementation:
  1. `Mission.unlocksCropId` (new field, `MissionSystem.cs`) — applied automatically the moment a mission carrying it BECOMES current, mirroring the existing `commitToWeedRouteOnComplete` apply-on-transition pattern, so no NPC-specific code was needed to react to a particular interaction.
  2. `ProgressionManager` gained six `RastaTaught*` flags and `MarkRastaTaught(cropId)`; `IsCropUnlocked` now ORs them into the existing Boss-exploitation formulas — additive, confirmed by a regression check that a fresh `ProgressionManager` still reaches `BlackSugarUnlocked` via `RecordBossJob()` alone with zero Rasta flags set.
  3. M13's harvest objective changed from `tomato x4` to `bushers x3` (tier 1, already unlocked from the start).
  4. Six new missions (`M13C2`-`M13C7`) inserted immediately after M13, before M13B/M14 — so the Boat Man/Guadeloupe chapter's fuller weight follows strain mastery, matching the handoff's own "only after the boys hold all core strains... open the Guadeloupe opportunity."
  5. `purple_black`'s player-facing name is "Purple Sugar" throughout this content; the internal save-facing crop ID is untouched.
- **Bug found and fixed along the way**: `Mini011PhaseBSetup`'s scene builder copies the in-memory `Mission` list into the built `MissionSystem` component via a manual, field-by-field `SerializedProperty` copy — and that copy had no line for the brand-new `unlocksCropId` field, so every mission's unlock would have silently baked in as an empty string regardless of what the C# code set. The new validator caught this immediately (`M13C2.unlocksCropId is '', expected 'black_sugar'`) before it could ship as a completely non-functional feature that looked correct in the source.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Missions/MissionSystem.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Progression/ProgressionManager.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`, new `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini111RastaLadderValidation.cs` and `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini111EvidenceCapture.cs`.
- Verification: clean compile; canonical scene rebuild; new focused `Mini111RastaLadderValidation` PASS (Bushers swap, ladder order/wiring, per-tier lock-then-unlock, additive Boss-exploitation regression check); standing regressions `Mini110NarrativeBridgeValidation`, `Mini109MissionRepairValidation`, `Mini108EarlyMissionValidation`, `Mini100GrandBayMapValidation` all still PASS; Windows build succeeded; 12-second headless built-player smoke test showed no fatal errors. Evidence: a Rasta screenshot and a full ladder-content log dump under `Logs/Tasks/MINI-111/`.
- Known limitations / NOT verified in Play Mode: `ProgressionManager` state (including the new `RastaTaught*` flags) is not currently persisted by the save system at all — a pre-existing gap, not a new regression, but a save/reload mid-ladder would lose progress today. The full ladder has not been played by a human on either career path, and Legitimate Farmer specifically is the path most worth testing since this is its first-ever route to advanced strains.

- Implementation:
  1. New `ObjectiveKind.DeliverItem` (`MissionSystem.cs`) and `EconomyManager.TrySpendConsumable(itemId, count, out message)` — spends a stashed consumable without applying its vitals effect (giving it to an NPC is not "using" it).
  2. New mission `M13B` ("Small Ting") inserted between M13 and M14: TalkTo Normy, then DeliverItem `food_bakes`, then DeliverItem `pill_energy`. A new `HandleNormyFavour()` branch in `TownNPCInteractable` is checked FIRST in Normy's interact flow, ahead of the MINI-109 mission-payment branch and the ambient bribe service.
  3. M14's `briefing` rewritten to carry Normy's hint ("...somebody taking weh from allu stock and stuff... check the Boat Man about a man name Gardey Zafeh...") — reuses the existing next-mission-briefing banner rather than new dialogue plumbing.

Dog Life foreshadowing deliberately NOT added as a separate gated dialogue line — Normy's own hint already satisfies "seed earlier, before the reveal" without inventing a new `DialogueConditionType` for uncertain benefit; recorded as a considered decision in the work packet, not a skipped item.
  6. `HUDController` gained a `courierTimerLabel`, visible only while `GuadeloupeTrade.Instance.TripActive`, reading `AwayCharacterIndex`'s display name and `SecondsRemaining` — connects to `GuadeloupeTrade`'s existing lock/hide/timer API rather than rebuilding travel.
- **Bug found and fixed along the way**: `Mini100GrandBayMapMigration.ResolveMissionMarker` had no case for `DeliverItem` — it would have fallen through to `Vector3.zero` (no remap), leaving M13B's Normy marker stale after map migration, the exact same class of bug MINI-109 just fixed for Rasta. Added a `DeliverItem -> NPC_Normy` case before it could ship broken; the new validator specifically checks for this (a stale/unmapped marker would show 100+m off, versus the confirmed <2m horizontal gap after the fix).
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Missions/MissionSystem.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Economy/EconomyManager.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Interaction/TownNPCInteractable.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/UI/HUDController.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini100GrandBayMapMigration.cs`, new `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini110NarrativeBridgeValidation.cs` and `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini110EvidenceCapture.cs`.
- Verification: clean compile; canonical scene rebuild; new focused `Mini110NarrativeBridgeValidation` PASS (M13B ordering, DeliverItem gating/idempotency, M14's carried hint, Boat Man's rewritten intro, DeliverItem's map-migration marker); standing regressions `Mini109MissionRepairValidation`, `Mini108EarlyMissionValidation`, `Mini100GrandBayMapValidation` all still PASS; Windows build succeeded; 12-second headless built-player smoke test showed no fatal errors. Three evidence screenshots captured and visually confirmed under `Logs/Tasks/MINI-110/`, including a forced-state HUD shot reading "Franki in Guadeloupe - back in 275s" (the trip state was forced via reflection since Play Mode never ticks in this batch environment — no real trip could be started to screenshot naturally).

The full Normy favour → Boat Man intro → courier-run sequence has not been played by a human. `GuadeloupeTrade`'s own mechanic was deliberately left untouched — only a UI reader was added against its existing API.

### MINI-109 — Grand Bay progression repair (Normy/Black Sugar/Rasta) and retrospective-completion rule

- Date: 2026-08-22
- Owner: Claude; released.
- Request: `Docs/WorkPackets/MINI-109.md` — fix the stuck Clean Face/Normy mission transaction, Black Sugar delivery to Boss J not advancing, Rasta's stale M13 marker, and establish a consistent retrospective-completion vs progression-lock rule.
- Root causes, each confirmed by direct reproduction before fixing, not guessed:
  1. **Clean Face stuck**: the mission's `BribeNormy` objective had no path except Normy's reusable ambient bribe service, which refuses to do anything (`return` early) once `economy.Heat <= 0.5f` — exactly the state the immediately-prior M5A mission ("go rest and cool down") leaves the player in. Fixed with a separate, one-time mission-payment branch in `HandleNormy()` (`TownNPCInteractable.cs`) gated on `IsCurrentObjective(BribeNormy, "Normy")` rather than heat/cooldown — naturally idempotent, since once `Notify()` advances the mission past this objective, the branch stops matching on its own.
  2. **Black Sugar delivery not advancing**: the interact-time gate on Boss J (`NpcRole.Boss` case) computed `weedHeld` from `bossSeedCrop.cropId` alone — hardcoded to whichever crop is first found `isIllegal` (Bushers) — even though Boss J's own `sellableCrops` list (and `TrySellCrops` itself) already handle every illegal strain. A player holding harvested Black Sugar but zero Bushers measured `weedHeld==0` and `SellCrops()` was never even called. Fixed with a new `HasAnySellableIllegalStock()` check mirroring `TrySellCrops`' actual sell criteria.
  3. **Rasta's stale M13 marker**: `Mini100GrandBayMapMigration.ApplyToOpenScene` called `RemapMissionObjectives()` (which reads each named NPC's CURRENT transform via `PositionOf(...)`) BEFORE the `MoveNamed` calls that place Sacat/Franki/Rasta at their final migrated positions — baking in each character's pre-move position, then silently moving them again afterward. Reordered so remapping runs last, against final positions (confirmed via a validator: pre-fix gap measured 312m, post-fix ~0.1m, matching the deliberate marker-height offset). A secondary, independently real bug in `BuildRastaMentor`'s own placement formula (missing the `farmRight*3f` offset present in his actual placement code) was also fixed by capturing his real built position (`_rastaPos`) directly instead of re-deriving the formula in the mission builder.
- **Retrospective-completion rule established**: `MissionSystem.PrepareCurrentObjective()` now credits a `BuyItem` objective immediately if `EconomyManager.OwnsItem(targetId)` is already true — e.g. buying the TMAX before M17 becomes active no longer leaves the player stuck, since `TryPurchase` would refuse a repeat purchase of a non-consumable ("You already have X") with no other path to ever notify the objective. Every other objective kind keeps today's progression-lock behaviour; this was deliberately not extended to kinds without an equally provable "already done" signal.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Interaction/TownNPCInteractable.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Missions/MissionSystem.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini100GrandBayMapMigration.cs`, new `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini109MissionRepairValidation.cs` and `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini109EvidenceCapture.cs`.
- Verification: clean compile; canonical scene rebuild (`Mini011PhaseBSetup.BuildScene`); new focused `Mini109MissionRepairValidation` PASS (reproduces all three defects against the real built scene, then proves each fix, plus the retrospective-completion rule); standing regressions `Mini108EarlyMissionValidation`, `Mini100GrandBayMapValidation`, `Mini095LalayMapLabSetup.BuildValidateCapture` all PASS; Windows build succeeded; 12-second headless built-player smoke test showed no fatal errors. Three evidence screenshots (Normy, Boss J/Black Sugar, Rasta) captured and visually confirmed under `Logs/Tasks/MINI-109/`. Save-facing IDs (`BossK`, `black_sugar`, `MontineFarm`, `land_montine`) confirmed unchanged.
- Known limitations / NOT verified in Play Mode: the fixes are proven via reflection-driven unit-level reproduction of each defect (the same technique this project has used since MINI-080), not a full end-to-end mission playthrough from M1. The user still needs to hands-on play Clean Face → Black Sugar delivery → Rasta, per `Docs/CLAUDE-HANDOFF-CURRENT.md` section 10, before `MINI-110` begins.

- Date: 2026-08-15

  - Built the scene entirely through a repeatable editor script (`Mini001SceneSetup.cs`) rather than hand-edited scene YAML, per D-004: ground plane, a narrow `RoadSurface` box flanked by 8 simple two-part buildings (wall + roof cube), a `DirtyPath` box branching off the road toward a `FarmClearing`/`FarmPlot`, one `NPC_Villager` capsule, one `Player` capsule, a directional light, and a `MainCamera`.
  - `PlayerController` (CharacterController-based, camera-relative walk/run, Left Shift to run) and `ThirdPersonFollowCamera` (fixed-yaw offset, LookAt-based, ~44° downward look angle from distance=7/height=6.8) satisfy the movement/camera criteria.
  - `IInteractable` / `InteractableBase` / `NPCInteractable` / `FarmPlotInteractable` / `InteractionDetector` implement the range-detection, "[ E ] Talk" / "[ E ] Plant" prompts, and one-line NPC dialogue on interact.

They track world positions correctly but are not literally Canvas-based; a future UI pass should swap in Canvas + TMP once those packages are deliberately added.
  - `FarmPlotInteractable` only marks the plot and shows a feedback line — the real plant/water/grow/harvest loop is explicitly MINI-003 scope, not reimplemented here.

Ran it headlessly (`-batchmode -nographics`, 6 seconds, log at `Logs/mini001-player-run.log`) — this sidesteps the Editor Search-module bug entirely since standalone players don't go through Editor Play mode. Result: **zero console errors**, confirming `Awake`/`Update`/`LateUpdate`/gravity/`CharacterController`/interaction range-scan all ran cleanly every frame. - Date: 2026-08-15

- Implementation:
  - `Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs` (new) rebuilds `GrandBayProof.unity` end to end: a sculpted `Terrain` (coast → flat village shelf → hills, approximate, inspired by `Grandbay entire.jpg`'s general shape per `Docs/MAP-STRATEGY.md`/the terrain assessment doc — not surveyed data), a textured grass `TerrainLayer` (Polytope Studio's ground texture), a visible `Sea` plane on the coastal side (per user: don't forget the sea — visual placeholder only, Guadeloupe sea-trade per D-007 is not wired up yet), a multi-segment bending road textured with Shanty Town's `road.jpg`, ~30-40 densely-packed structures along both sides (mix of real Shanty Town shanty meshes and hand-built modular houses — proper pitched gable roofs via two angled boxes meeting at a ridge, inset door/window panels, varied Caribbean wall colours, occasional 2-storey variants), scattered Shanty Town props (barrels/clothesline/fence/tyres), vegetation (Aquaset palms coastal side, Polytope fruit trees inland), and the Montine dirty farm path/clearing/plot (now textured with Shanty Town's `tyretracks.jpg`).
  - Replaced both the player and NPC capsules with real Low Poly Character Pack Humanoid models (`male01_1`/`male02_1`), each carrying an `Animator`.
  - Built a small Idle/Walk/Run `BlendTree` `AnimatorController` (`Assets/UpIzUpMini/Art/PlayerLocomotion.controller`) from Human Basic Motions FREE's male clips (already Humanoid-rigged, confirmed via `.meta`, so Mecanim retargeting onto the Floreswa avatar works with no extra setup). `PlayerController.cs` now drives an `Animator.SetFloat("Speed", …)` blend based on `CurrentSpeed`/`IsRunning`.
  - **Found and fixed a real bug via the new rendering-based visual-check workflow, not guesswork:** Aquaset's `PalmTree` prefabs are wired to their `Materials/URP/` variant by default; this project has no URP package, so they rendered solid magenta. The pack ships a `Materials/Built-In/` sibling for every material — added `SwapUrpMaterialsForBuiltIn()` to reassign by filename convention on instantiation. Confirmed fixed by re-rendering (palms are green in the after-shot).
  - Iterated on house density twice using the same rendering workflow: first pass was far too sparse (large empty gaps, ~14 structures total) against the brief's "close to both sides of the road" requirement; resampled the road polyline to ~11m spacing for placement (independent of the coarser road-mesh polyline) to roughly triple density, confirmed via a second render.
  - Extended `Mini011AssetSnapshot.cs` with scene-view snapshot modes (player-eye view, wide overview, mid-distance overview) used throughout this pass to catch the above two problems before building an exe, rather than after.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011AssetSnapshot.cs` (extended), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Character/PlayerController.cs` (Animator hookup), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Art/PlayerLocomotion.controller` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Art/GrassGround.terrainlayer` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).

Headless player run: zero console errors across ~8 seconds of the real compiled game. Visual checks performed and iterated on using actual rendered frames, not assumptions — this is the first MINI-011 pass with real look-and-feel evidence, not just asset-level snapshots.

Road path is procedural (sine-wave bend), not traced from the reference images.

### MINI-011 — Phase B bugfix pass (from user's hands-on test)

- Date: 2026-08-15
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User tested the Phase B build and reported, with visual inspection requested for all: upside-down roofs, prompt text too small, house density still too low, Shanty Town models too small, one white/untextured shanty house, character can't run, and asked for Smart-darker/Strong-lighter skin tones. Also supplied the full game plot (saved to `Docs/STORY.md`) and a future-extensibility note about shops/accessories/vehicles/property (saved to memory, not implemented now).
- Implementation (each fix verified by rendering the actual scene again, not assumed):
  - **Roofs upside-down:** `BuildGableRoof`'s slope rotation had the wrong sign — the ridge (should be the high edge) was ending up low, producing a valley/trough silhouette instead of a peak. Fixed (`-side * pitchDeg` instead of `side * pitchDeg`) and confirmed via render: roofs now show a correct peaked gable.
  - **White/untextured shanty house:** traced to `ShantyVariants` including 16/18/20, which have no matching `Materials/shantyN.mat` in the source pack (only 1-14 do — confirmed by inspecting the pack's Materials folder). Restricted the variant list to 1-14.
  - **Shanty models too small:** applied a `2.1x` uniform scale on instantiation (source meshes import undersized relative to the ~2m-tall Humanoid characters).
  - **Not enough house density:** tightened road-polyline resampling for house placement from ~11m to ~7m spacing, widened the road-clearance-adjusted lateral band, reduced how often a gap/tall-building slot is skipped. Confirmed via a second wide-overview render: continuous house frontage along the road, not scattered dots.
  - **Prompt/text too small:** `InteractionDetector`'s `OnGUI` styles used a fixed pixel font size, which reads tiny on a high-resolution display and would be worse on a phone/tablet. Rewrote to scale against a 1080-tall reference resolution — this also directly serves the mobile-first requirement, not just this bug report.
  - **Character can't run:** found and fixed a real staleness bug: `BuildAnimatorController` cached the generated controller across rebuilds via `if (existing != null) return existing`, so later script edits to the blend tree wouldn't necessarily take effect. Changed to always delete and rebuild fresh. Also increased the animation blend response rate. Root cause of the *original* complaint is not fully certain (no missing-clip warnings were logged on the first-ever build either), so this needs a second confirmation from the user — noted as still open below.
  - **Character skin tone:** found the character pack's material slot is literally named `skin` (confirmed by probing the prefab's renderers, not guessed). Cloned it per-instance (editing the shared material would have recoloured every character using that prefab) — the controllable character (Smart) is darker, the NPC placeholder (standing in for Strong until Phase C's switching system exists) is lighter.
  - Saved `Docs/STORY.md` (full plot, verbatim) and a memory note on future shop/accessory/vehicle/property extensibility — informs how the economy data model gets designed in a later phase, not implemented now.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Character/PlayerController.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Interaction/InteractionDetector.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011MaterialProbe.cs` (new, kept as a small reusable diagnostic), `Docs/STORY.md` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Verification commands/results: `Logs/bugfix-compile.log` (clean), `Logs/bugfix-buildscene.log` (clean), `Logs/snapshot-mid3.log` + `Logs/Snapshots/grandbay-overview-mid.png` and `grandbay-overview-wide.png` (visually confirm roofs/density/scale/no-white-house fixes), `Logs/bugfix-build-exe.log` (Windows build succeeded), `Logs/bugfix-player-run.log` (zero errors across ~8s headless run).
- Known issues / still open:
  - **Running fix is not independently confirmed** — the caching bug was real and is fixed, but I don't have certainty that was the *entire* original cause. Needs the user's re-test.
  - Tab/character-switching was not tested by the user yet and doesn't exist as a real system yet (only one controllable character) — Phase C.
  - Police, shopkeeper, and crop-buyer NPCs; health/heat/stamina meters; numbered (1-4) crop-selection; before/after soil colour states across multiple plots; crop growth-stage scaling and green-to-red fruit colour — none of this exists yet. This is the clearly-scoped Phase C list now (see `TASKS.md`).
  - Car/driving explicitly deferred by the user to a later phase.
- Next action: user re-tests this build; then Phase C (NPCs, HUD meters, crop selection/growth, Tab switching + Strong as a real second character) gets claimed.

### MINI-011 — Pause menu + mouse-look

- Date: 2026-08-15
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User confirmed the bugfix pass works, asked for Esc-to-menu with Q-to-quit/navigable options, and mouse-look camera control. Noted running still feels weird (deferred by user) and asked me to keep visually inspecting my own work.
- Implementation:
  - `PauseMenuController.cs` (new): Esc toggles a pause panel (`Time.timeScale` 0/1, cursor unlocked+visible while paused, locked+hidden while playing). While paused, Q quits (`EditorApplication.isPlaying = false` in-Editor, `Application.Quit()` in a build). Resume/Quit are real UGUI `Button`s — mouse-clickable, and keyboard-navigable for free via Unity's default `Selectable` navigation once one is selected (`EventSystem.SetSelectedGameObject(resumeButton)` on pause).
  - `ThirdPersonFollowCamera.cs`: added mouse-look — horizontal orbit driven by `Input.GetAxis("Mouse X")`, but only while `Cursor.lockState == CursorLockMode.Locked`, so opening the pause menu doesn't spin the camera from residual mouse delta. Downward look angle is unaffected (still ~44°); `PlayerController` already moves camera-relative, so WASD direction follows wherever the mouse has oriented the camera, which is the intended combined scheme.
  - `Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`: builds the `EventSystem` (+`StandaloneInputModule`, matching the project's legacy Input Manager setup) and a Screen Space - Overlay `Canvas` with the pause panel/buttons using legacy `UnityEngine.UI.Text` and Unity 6's built-in `LegacyRuntime.ttf` (not `Arial.ttf` — that constant was removed in Unity 6 and threw an `ArgumentException` on the first attempt; fixed and reconfirmed via a rebuild).
  - Extended `Mini011AssetSnapshot.cs` with a pause-menu snapshot mode to visually confirm the UI layout before shipping the build — caught a real bug in the diagnostic itself along the way (`GameObject.Find` doesn't search inactive objects, so the initially-hidden panel wasn't found; fixed by looking it up via the canvas's transform instead).
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/UI/PauseMenuController.cs` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Camera/ThirdPersonFollowCamera.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011AssetSnapshot.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Verification: compile clean; scene builder hit one real runtime exception (`Arial.ttf` removed in Unity 6) caught and fixed, then rebuilt clean; rendered the pause menu (title + both buttons legible, correctly positioned — full-screen dim overlay didn't show in this particular render because verifying an Overlay-mode canvas requires temporarily switching it to Camera mode for the offline snapshot technique to capture it at all, which appears to affect its compositing in this static single-frame test; the shipped scene keeps the canvas in Overlay mode, unaffected by the snapshot script since scene changes there aren't saved). Windows build succeeded, zero errors in an 8s headless run, launched for the user.
- Known issues:
  - **Mouse-look and pause-menu interaction (clicking buttons, Q-to-quit, keyboard nav) have not been confirmed by an actual human interacting with the compiled game** — Update()-driven input logic can't be meaningfully exercised from batch mode. This is exactly what the launched build is for.
  - The pause panel's full-screen dark overlay wasn't visually confirmed in the offline snapshot for the technical reason above; worth a quick look in the real build.
  - Running "feels weird" is explicitly deferred by the user — not addressed in this pass.
- Next action: user tests Esc/mouse-look/Q-quit in the running build.

### MINI-011 — Phase C: NPCs, HUD, farming loop, crop selection, character switching + deferred fixes

- Date: 2026-08-15
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User confirmed the pause menu/mouse-look pass worked, then asked for the full Phase C list from `TASKS.md` (police/shopkeeper/buyer NPCs, health/heat/stamina meters, 1-4 crop selection, multi-plot soil states, crop growth visuals) plus, separately mid-turn, a standing note about designing the economy for future shop/accessory purchases (saved to memory, not implemented). After Phase C, asked for two more fixes: vertical (pitch) mouse-look, and Shanty Town buildings having no collision (player walked through them).
- Implementation:
  - **Economy** (`Scripts/Economy/`): `CropDefinition` (ScriptableObject — id/name/price/illegal flag/growth-visual colours, deliberately generic per the future-extensibility note rather than a tomato-only type), `EconomyManager` (shared money + inventory + heat, matching Docs/STORY.md's "share money, inventory, heat"), `CropSelectionController` (keys 1-4 pick the active crop). 4 crop assets created: Tomato, Banana, Carrot, Bushers (the last flagged `isIllegal`, so selling it adds heat — the brief's own heat-source requirement).
  - **Farming** (`Scripts/Farming/FarmPlot.cs`): replaces `FarmPlotInteractable` with a real state machine (Empty → PlantedDry → Growing → Ripe → back to Empty). Soil is light brown while empty, dark brown once planted — matches the user's explicit request. Crop visual scales up and tints from unripe to ripe colour over the final third of growth (3 distinguishable visual stages: tiny/green, mid-size/green, full-size/ripe-colour). 6 plots built in a 3x2 grid in the farm clearing (the brief's minimum), not the single plot MINI-001 had.
  - **NPCs** (`Scripts/Interaction/TownNPCInteractable.cs`): one role-based interactable (`Villager`/`Police`/`Shopkeeper`/`Buyer`) rather than four near-duplicate classes. Police's line reacts to current heat level. Buyer sells the entire shared inventory via `EconomyManager.TrySellAll`. 4 NPCs placed along the road using 3 different Floreswa body variants so they're visually distinct from each other and from Smart/Strong.
  - **Characters**: `CharacterVitals` (per-character health/stamina, matching Docs/STORY.md's "retain separate health, stamina"), `FollowController` (simple direct-steering companion AI for whichever boy isn't controlled — not a NavMesh agent, so it can cut corners around obstacles; acceptable for this pass), `CharacterSwitchManager` (Tab swaps control between Smart and Strong: toggles `PlayerController.IsControlled`/`InteractionDetector.enabled`/`FollowController.FollowingEnabled`, retargets the camera). Strong now exists as a real second controllable character (lighter skin per the user's tone request) rather than the NPC placeholder from the earlier bugfix pass — the ambient villager NPC moved to a third Floreswa body variant so it doesn't overlap Strong's identity.
  - **HUD** (`Scripts/UI/HUDController.cs`): health/stamina/heat bars (`Image.Type.Filled`), money, active character name, current crop selection — built into a second Screen Space - Overlay canvas alongside the pause menu's.
  - **Deferred fixes, done after Phase C per the user's explicit ordering:**
    - `ThirdPersonFollowCamera` rewritten from fixed-height/distance to a proper spherical orbit (yaw + pitch, both mouse-driven while the cursor is locked, pitch clamped 20-75°) so the player can look up and down, not just side to side. Kept a field-initializer default (`_pitch = 44f`) rather than only setting it in `Awake()`, specifically so `Mini001SceneValidation`'s look-angle check (which runs without entering Play mode) keeps working.
    - Shanty Town instances never had a `Collider` — FBX-imported meshes don't get one automatically the way primitives do, so the player walked straight through every shanty structure. Added `AddBoundsCollider()`: computes each instance's actual rendered bounds and adds a matching `BoxCollider` in local space (scale-safe). Hand-built houses already had colliders (from their primitive walls) and were unaffected. Small scattered props (barrels/tyres/etc.) deliberately left uncollided — the user's complaint was specifically about buildings.
  - Updated the old `Mini001SceneValidation.cs` to recognize both the pre- and post-Phase-C interactable types, so it doesn't silently report a false failure now that `NPCInteractable`/`FarmPlotInteractable` have been superseded by `TownNPCInteractable`/`FarmPlot` in the built scene (MINI-001's own history/scripts are untouched otherwise).
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Economy/**` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Farming/FarmPlot.cs` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Interaction/TownNPCInteractable.cs` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Character/CharacterVitals.cs`, `CharacterSwitchManager.cs`, `FollowController.cs` (new), `PlayerController.cs` (IsControlled gate + stamina hookup), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/UI/HUDController.cs` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Camera/ThirdPersonFollowCamera.cs` (spherical orbit), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs` (major expansion), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini001SceneValidation.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Data/Crops/*.asset` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Verification: compile clean on first attempt for the full Phase C addition (a genuinely large change). Scene builder ran clean (no exceptions) on the first try. Rendered three real views before shipping: a wide/mid overview (village layout still intact), a gameplay-HUD render (confirmed all three meters, money, and — importantly — **both Smart and Strong standing together, visually distinct**, not assumed), consistent with the corrective brief's "clearly different clothing colours, body silhouettes" requirement. Ran `Mini001SceneValidation` after updating it for the new component types: **PASS**, including the camera look-angle check (44°, within the 40-50° range, confirming the deferred vertical-look rewrite didn't regress it). Windows build succeeded; headless run of the actual compiled game: **zero console errors** across 10 seconds with every new system (economy, 6 farm plots, 4 NPCs, both characters, switch manager, HUD) actually initializing via real `Awake()`/`Start()`/`Update()` calls, not just constructed statically.
- Known issues (stated plainly):
  - **None of the interactive behavior has been confirmed by an actual human** — crop selection, planting/watering/harvesting, talking to each NPC role, selling, Tab-switching, the companion actually following, vertical mouse-look, and walking into a (now solid) shanty building all need a real playtest. Static/headless verification confirms things construct and run without exceptions, not that they feel or play correctly.
  - `FollowController` uses direct steering, not pathfinding — the companion can get stuck on/cut through obstacles in tight spots. Acceptable for this pass, worth a NavMesh upgrade later if it's a problem in practice.
