### MINI-031 - Reusable Humanoid Animation Manager (foundation)

- Date: 2026-08-16
- Owner: Claude
- Request: A large open-ended feature dump (animation manager, wardrobe/IK accessories, exact Grand Bay map, weed strain interbreeding, farm-to-ownership progression, bike/car with purchase, rival gang combat, companion auto-assist, police heat +100 on hit, death fee to the health center, expensive seed seller, cell phone to recall companion, buyable Lalay house, land purchase via survey/office instead of the farm shop, farmhand-for-hire and Guadeloupe-NPC-first as later unlocks, colored higher-tier strains unlocked by mission). Per `CLAUDE.md`'s instruction not to start broad implementation from an open-ended request, this was first scoped into a ranked easiest-to-hardest list (see session transcript) and the user chose the Animation Manager as the one task to build now, since wardrobe/IK, bike, car, and shooting all depend on it.
- Acceptance criteria: A reusable `HumanoidAnimationManager` usable on any Humanoid-rigged character (not player-specific); the existing base locomotion layer (Speed/Grounded/Jump/FreeFall) unchanged in behaviour; a new upper-body-masked "Action" layer plus an unmasked "FullBodyOverride" layer, both weight 0 by default, that fade in/out around one-shot or sustained actions without freezing locomotion; actions registered as data (id + clip + fullBody flag), not per-action C#; `SimpleMeleeCombat` migrated onto it as the proof it works end to end; compiles clean, scene builder clean, Windows build succeeds, zero console errors in a headless run, no T-pose/broken-pose regression confirmed by a rendered snapshot.
- Implementation:
  - `HumanoidAnimationManager.cs` (new): the runtime component. Holds a `List<ActionEntry>` (`id`, `clip`, `fullBody`) per character. `PlayAction(id)` looks the id up, cross-fades the correct layer's weight to 1 and plays the matching Animator state (`CrossFadeInFixedTime`), then a coroutine fades the layer weight back to 0 (and cross-fades back to an empty state) once the clip's own length elapses - so it composes with whatever locomotion is doing underneath instead of overriding the whole body. `BeginSustainedAction`/`EndSustainedAction` hold a full-body pose indefinitely for states with no natural end (seated driving); nothing calls these yet, they exist so vehicle-entry work has a stable place to plug in later. Calling an id a character doesn't carry, or whose state isn't baked into the controller, is a safe no-op.
  - `HumanoidAnimationLayerBuilder.cs` (new, editor-only): bakes the "Action" (upper-body `AvatarMask` - legs/feet/root excluded so locomotion keeps driving them) and "FullBodyOverride" (no mask) layers into the shared, already-authored `StarterAssetsThirdPerson.controller` every character uses - additive, not a rebuild, and idempotent (checks for existing layers/states by name before adding, updates a state's motion in place if the clip changed) so it's safe to call on every scene rebuild. This is deliberately one call per controller for the union of every character's actions, since the layers/states are shared infrastructure, not per-instance.
  - `SimpleMeleeCombat.cs`: migrated onto the manager - F now calls `animationManager.PlayAction("Melee")` (plays even on a swing that connects with nothing, so it reads as a fight) alongside the existing damage/heat/cooldown logic, which is untouched.
  - `Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`: calls `HumanoidAnimationLayerBuilder.EnsureActionLayers` right after loading the shared locomotion controller; `BuildControllableCharacter` now also adds `HumanoidAnimationManager` to both Deril and Franki (wired via `SerializedObject`, matching every other component in this method) with the shared action list, and wires `SimpleMeleeCombat.animationManager` to it. `GetSharedActionEntries()` is the one place new actions get added later - today it holds exactly one entry, `"Melee"`, using a Kevin Iglesias one-handed sword-swing clip (`HumanM@Attack1H01_R.fbx`) as a placeholder, since no bare-fist punch clip exists in any imported package; it reads as a believable unarmed swing since the characters hold no weapon, but it is a stand-in pending a real punch animation.
  - Deliberately did **not** build eating, shooting, riding, driving, vehicle enter/exit, or wardrobe/accessory content in this pass - there is no food-eating clip, no weapon-hold clip, no vehicle rig, and no separate garment geometry anywhere in the project yet, so there is nothing real to wire those actions to. What this task delivers is the place those things plug into later: a new action is a data entry in a per-character list plus a `PlayAction`/`BeginSustainedAction` call from whichever gameplay script needs it, not a new animation system. Follow-on tasks `MINI-032` through `MINI-037` are recorded in `TASKS.md` with what each specifically needs.
  - Also discovered, while reading `CharacterEquipment.cs` for this task, that bone-anchored accessory attachment (cap/shades/chain/watch via `Animator.GetBoneTransform`) already exists from MINI-022 - the user's "attach chain/accessories to bones" ask is therefore mostly done; what's missing is swing/sway *physics* on top of the existing rigid attachment, and separate garment meshes instead of material-recolor for clothing. Recorded as `MINI-032`, not attempted here (out of this task's scope, and no Animation Rigging package is installed yet).
- Files changed: `Scripts/Character/HumanoidAnimationManager.cs` (new), `Editor/HumanoidAnimationLayerBuilder.cs` (new), `Scripts/Combat/SimpleMeleeCombat.cs`, `Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`; `Art/Animations/UpperBodyMask.mask` (new, generated); `Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt); `TASKS.md`.
- Scene/prefab changes: `GrandBayProof.unity` rebuilt via the standard scene-builder script (no hand edits) - both protagonists now carry `HumanoidAnimationManager`; the shared `StarterAssetsThirdPerson.controller` asset gained the two new layers (additive, existing locomotion layer/states untouched).
- Verification commands: `Logs/MINI-031-Compile.log` (batch-mode compile), `Logs/MINI-031-BuildScene.log` (scene builder), `Logs/MINI-031-WindowsBuild.log` (Windows player build), `Logs/MINI-031-PlayerRun.log` (headless run of the built player, ~15s), `Logs/MINI-031-SnapshotNpcStance.log` + `Logs/Snapshots/npc-stance.png` (rendered visual check).
- Verification results: compile clean, 0 `error CS`. Scene builder ran clean (`MINI-011 Phase B: GrandBayProof rebuilt and saved`), no exceptions - the only stack traces in the log are routine `Debug.Log`/`Debug.LogWarning` call-site traces (Unity logs those for every call at this project's log verbosity), not errors; confirmed by grepping for `NullReferenceException`/`MissingReference`/`error CS`, zero matches. Windows build succeeded (`MINI-001 BUILD SUCCEEDED`). Headless run of the compiled game: zero error/exception lines across ~15 seconds with the new components actually initializing (`Awake`/layer resolution) via real gameplay code, not just constructed. Rendered `npc-stance.png`: characters stand in a normal arms-down idle pose, not T-posed or twisted - this is the real risk with adding new Animator layers (a bad `AvatarMask` or stuck layer weight shows up exactly as a broken pose), and it isn't happening.
- Known issues (honest):
  - **Melee animation is not hands-on confirmed.** The compositing (upper body swings while legs keep walking, then fades back to nothing) is architecturally sound and the pose renders correctly at rest, but nobody has pressed F while moving and watched it blend in real time yet.
  - **The melee clip is a sword-swing borrowed as a placeholder unarmed animation** - it will very likely look slightly off (arm/hand shaped for gripping something) and should be replaced with a real punch clip when one is available.
  - **No content exists yet for eating, shooting, riding, driving, or vehicle entry/exit** - this task built only the plumbing those need, not the actions themselves. See `TASKS.md` MINI-032 through MINI-037 for what's still required and roughly how hard each is.
  - The used-by-name walk-pose snapshot tool (`Mini011AssetSnapshot.SnapshotWalkPose`, from MINI-016) renders a camera buried inside a building wall at the current world layout - unrelated to this task (world geometry has shifted through many rebuilds since MINI-016 authored that camera offset), not fixed here since it's a stale diagnostic, not part of MINI-031's scope. `SnapshotNpcStance` was used instead and works correctly.
- Next action: user playtests - specifically, press F while walking/running as either Deril or Franki and confirm the punch animation plays and blends naturally with the legs still moving, then fades back out. Once confirmed, MINI-032 through MINI-037 can be claimed in any order (see `TASKS.md`), though MINI-033/034/036 (bike/car/shooting) are the ones that actually exercise `BeginSustainedAction`/`FullBodyOverride` and are worth prioritizing to prove that half of this task's architecture too.

### MINI-038 - NPC hit reactions and knockdown

- Date: 2026-08-16
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User confirmed the punch (MINI-031) works and asked to continue with NPCs able to receive punches and reactions, including falling and lying down when knocked down by multiple punches.
- Acceptance criteria: A punched NPC (police - the only role with `NpcCombatHealth` today) plays a stagger reaction on a hit that doesn't finish them, and a fall/lie-down pose (held for the recovery window, not an instant vanish) on the hit that drops them to 0 health; a knocked-down officer stops chasing/patrolling instead of fighting the pose; state transitions verified against real code, not just "it compiled."
- Implementation:
  - `NpcCombatHealth.cs` rewritten: non-fatal hits call `HumanoidAnimationManager.PlayAction("HitReaction")` (a one-shot stagger); the hit that crosses 0 health calls `BeginSustainedAction("KnockedDown")` instead - held at full layer weight for the whole `recoverSeconds` window rather than auto-fading, and the officer is no longer hidden (renderers stay on) since the point is to see them lying down, not disappear. Added a public `IsDown` flag other scripts can check. Recovery (`Update()`) calls `EndSustainedAction()` and re-enables the `CharacterController`. Falls back to the old vanish-and-recover behaviour only if a character has no `HumanoidAnimationManager` or the id isn't baked in, so nothing regresses on a character this doesn't apply to.
  - `PoliceOfficer.cs`: added an `IsDown` check at the top of `Update()` that returns immediately (no `SimpleMove` on a `CharacterController` `NpcCombatHealth` has already disabled, no fighting the sustained pose by writing to the base locomotion layer's `Speed`/`Grounded`).
  - `Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`: `GetSharedActionEntries()` gained two real clips from the same Kevin Iglesias pack already used for melee - `HumanM@CombatDamage01.fbx` (`HitReaction`) and `HumanM@Death01.fbx` (`KnockedDown`, full-body; its clip has Loop Time off, so Mecanim already holds the last frame - lying down - once played through, no extra code needed for that). Extracted the repeated `HumanoidAnimationManager` + `SerializedObject` wiring block from MINI-031 into `AddHumanoidAnimationManager(GameObject, Animator)` so both the two protagonists and police officers use the same setup path instead of duplicated boilerplate. Police officers now get a `HumanoidAnimationManager` (previously only the two protagonists had one) wired to a new `NpcCombatHealth.animationManager` field and `PoliceOfficer.combatHealth` field.
  - `Mini038CombatValidation.cs` (new, editor-only): rather than trusting "compiles and the headless run has no exceptions" the way most prior entries had to, this instantiates a real Humanoid character against the actual shared (already action-layer-baked) controller, invokes `Awake()` via reflection (MonoBehaviour lifecycle methods don't run outside Play mode - same pattern as `Mini028FarmhandValidation`), and asserts on real state: two hits at 35/100 damage each leave `IsDown` false; the `HitReaction` `PlayAction` call synchronously sets the `FullBodyOverride` layer weight to 1 (confirming the action actually fired, not just "didn't throw"); the third hit sets `IsDown` true and the layer weight to 1 via `BeginSustainedAction`; a fourth hit while already down is confirmed to be a no-op. One assertion was wrong on the first run and had to be fixed - I initially expected the layer weight to have decayed back to 0 between hits, forgetting the fade-out coroutine only ticks across real frames, which back-to-back synchronous `Hit()` calls in an edit-mode harness never provide; that decay is what the headless player run instead exercises, not this harness.
- Files changed: `Scripts/Combat/NpcCombatHealth.cs`, `Scripts/Interaction/PoliceOfficer.cs`, `Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`; `Editor/Mini038CombatValidation.cs` (new); `Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Scene/prefab changes: `GrandBayProof.unity` rebuilt via the standard scene-builder script; police officers now carry `HumanoidAnimationManager` in addition to `NpcCombatHealth`/`PoliceOfficer`.
- Verification commands: `Logs/MINI-038-Compile.log`, `Logs/MINI-038-BuildScene.log`, `Logs/MINI-038-WindowsBuild.log`, `Logs/MINI-038-PlayerRun.log` (headless run of the built player, ~15s), `Logs/MINI-038-Validation3.log` (`Mini038CombatValidation.Run`).
- Verification results: compile clean, 0 `error CS`. Scene builder clean (`rebuilt and saved`), no exceptions. Windows build succeeded. Headless run of the compiled game: zero error/exception/nullreference lines across ~15 seconds. Combat validation: **PASS** - `MINI-038 COMBAT VALIDATION PASS: stagger reaction on hits 1-2 (no knockdown), knockdown on hit 3 (FullBodyOverride weight 1), hit-while-down is a no-op.` This is real evidence the state machine and animation-layer wiring are correct, not just that nothing crashed.
- Known issues (honest):
  - **The actual fall/lie-down pose has not been visually rendered or hands-on watched.** The validation harness proves the *code path* is correct (right layer, right weight, right state transitions at the right hit counts) but not that the Death01 clip looks good playing on this character, or that the transition from standing to lying reads naturally in real time. That needs either a snapshot render mid-clip or, better, the user watching it happen live.
  - **Recovery has no "get up" animation** - the officer simply pops back to normal idle/patrol once `recoverSeconds` elapses and `EndSustainedAction` fades the layer out; there's no reverse-death/stand-up clip in the imported packages. Worth a real "get up" clip later if the instant pop looks bad.
  - Only police carry `NpcCombatHealth` today, so this only applies to officers - villagers/shopkeepers/buyers/Boss K are not punchable and were not touched.
  - The `HitReaction` clip plays full-body (it's a stagger, legs included) rather than upper-body-only, which means it briefly overrides the officer's own locomotion layer for its short duration - acceptable for a stagger (they should visibly flinch, not just wave an arm) but worth knowing if it ever looks like a weird foot-plant hiccup mid-chase.
- Next action: user playtests - punch a police officer 2-3 times and watch for a stagger reaction, then a 3rd hit and watch them fall/lie down instead of vanishing, then watch them get back up ~12s later. If the death-clip-as-knockdown reads badly (too dramatic/too slow), say so and it's a one-clip swap.

### MINI-038 addendum - Heat jumps to max on hitting police

- Date: 2026-08-16
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User asked directly whether hitting/shooting police already spikes the heat meter by 100. It didn't - `SimpleMeleeCombat` was still adding a flat 12 heat per landed punch, a leftover from MINI-030 predating the user's later explicit "when hit or shoot police meter goes up by 100" ask, which had not yet been implemented (only tracked in `TASKS.md` MINI-036 as "heat +100 once shooting exists" - the melee half of that ask was missed).
- Implementation: `SimpleMeleeCombat.Update()` now calls `EconomyManager.Instance?.AddHeat(EconomyManager.MaxHeat)` instead of `AddHeat(12f)` on a landed hit. `AddHeat` clamps at `MaxHeat` (100), so this is an instant jump to full heat on any punch that connects, matching "goes up by 100" against a meter whose ceiling is 100. Only police carry `NpcCombatHealth` today, so every landed hit through this code path is already striking an officer - no role check needed. Shooting doesn't exist yet (`MINI-036`); when it's built, it should apply the same `AddHeat(EconomyManager.MaxHeat)` call.
- Files changed: `Scripts/Combat/SimpleMeleeCombat.cs`; `Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Verification: compile clean (`Logs/MINI-038b-Compile.log`); scene builder clean (`Logs/MINI-038b-BuildScene.log`); Windows build succeeded (`Logs/MINI-038b-WindowsBuild.log`); headless run of the built player, zero errors across ~15s (`Logs/MINI-038b-PlayerRun.log`).
- Known issues: Not hands-on confirmed - the HUD heat bar should visibly snap to 100%/blink and a second officer should spawn (per MINI-021's reinforcement thresholds) the instant a punch lands; nobody has watched that happen live yet.
- Next action: user punches an officer once and confirms the heat bar jumps straight to 100% (not a gradual climb) and a second officer spawns.

### MINI-039 - Tier-1 economy/mission tuning batch

- Date: 2026-08-16
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User said "go on to the next" meaning the next items from the earlier ranked easiest-to-hardest list (not vehicles/driving, which the user explicitly deferred). Covers: death fee to the health center, expensive strain seeds, fixing the land-purchase mission to point at the Land Office, corrected strain bud colours, and police confiscating weed near the plantation.
- Investigation first: several items on the original list turned out to already exist in more depth than `PROJECT-HANDOFF.md`'s summary suggested - `NpcRole` already has `LandOffice`/`StrainBoss`/`CarDealer`/`FoodShop`/`Pharmacy` roles, a separate "Land and Surveys" shop already sells `land_montine`/`land_hillside`/`prop_safehouse`, a bike/van/pirogue are already purchasable from a Car Dealer (not yet drivable - out of scope here), and `StrainBoss` NPCs (BossM/BossP) already gate Black Sugar/Purple behind `ProgressionManager` reputation thresholds. This changed what actually needed building: the land mission was a stale-marker bug, not a missing feature, and "expensive seeds" meant pricing an existing free grant, not building a new seller.
- Implementation:
  - **A. Death fee.** `CharacterSwitchManager.HandleDeath` now deducts a new `deathFee` (default 500) via `EconomyManager.AddMoney(-deathFee)` before respawning - `AddMoney` has no floor, so this can put `Money` negative (a debt), matching the user's "taken or owed" wording. Scoped to the health-zero death path specifically, not the shared `RespawnAtSafehouse` method `WorldSafetyController` also calls for falling out of the map - that isn't a medical event and shouldn't carry a health-center bill.
  - **B. Land mission fix.** Mission M3 ("More Land")'s `BuyItem` objective said "Buy the Montine Land Plot from the Farm Shop" with its marker at the market/Farm Shop position - stale text from before the Land Office existed; `land_montine` has only ever been stocked in the separate `landShop`. Added a `landOfficePos` (same road-index convention as the other marker positions) and pointed the objective's instruction and marker there instead.
  - **C. Expensive strain seeds.** Boss K's and both `StrainBoss`es' seed grants (`EconomyManager.AddSeeds(...)`, previously free) now go through a new `TryBuySeed` helper that charges a `[SerializeField] int seedPrice` and declines cleanly (no seed, no charge, an in-character "you short" line) if the player can't afford it - unlike the death fee, this one does not allow debt. Priced by the strains' existing rarity ordering (bushers 22 < black_sugar 38 < purple 55 sell price): Bushers $150, Black Sugar $320, Purple $500.
  - **D. Strain bud colours.** Black Sugar's ripe colour is now orange, Purple's a brighter purple, per the user's ask. **This needed two attempts** - see Known Issues/lesson below.
  - **E. Police confiscate weed near the plantation.** `LandRiskController` previously only ran a blind 180-second timer gated on owning the bought `land_montine` expansion. Added a second, proximity-driven check (`CheckPoliceProximity`, 25s cooldown once triggered): if any `NpcRole.Police` officer comes within `plantationRadius` (15m) of `plantationCenter` (the starting Montine plots, not just the bought expansion) while the player holds any illegal crop or seed, 40% of illegal stock is confiscated immediately with an in-world alert. This is additive to the existing timer check, not a replacement.
  - **Lesson worth recording:** a first attempt at (D) hand-edited the `black_sugar.asset`/`purple.asset` YAML files directly - Unity's asset pipeline silently reverted that edit on the very next batch-mode run. A second attempt via a proper one-off `AssetDatabase.LoadAssetAtPath` + `SetDirty` + `SaveAssets` script *also* got reverted on the next `Mini011PhaseBSetup.BuildScene` run. Root cause: `BuildEconomyAndCrops`'s `CropSpecs` table is the actual source of truth and is reapplied unconditionally to every crop asset on every scene build - editing the generated `.asset` file (by any method) doesn't survive the next rebuild. Fixed at the real source (the `CropSpecs` hex values) and deleted the now-pointless one-off script. **Generalizes:** any future change to a crop's data fields must go in `CropSpecs`, not the generated asset - this scene builder is a from-scratch regenerator for these assets, not a one-time seed.
- Files changed: `Scripts/Character/CharacterSwitchManager.cs`, `Scripts/Interaction/TownNPCInteractable.cs`, `Scripts/Progression/LandRiskController.cs`, `Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs` (mission marker, `BuildStrainBoss`/Boss K `seedPrice` wiring, `LandRiskController.plantationCenter` wiring, `CropSpecs` colour fix); `Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Scene/prefab changes: `GrandBayProof.unity` rebuilt via the standard scene-builder script; no hand edits.
- Verification commands: `Logs/MINI-039-Compile2.log`, `Logs/MINI-039-BuildScene3.log`, `Logs/MINI-039-WindowsBuild.log`, `Logs/MINI-039-PlayerRun.log` (headless run of the built player, ~15s).
- Verification results: compile clean, 0 `error CS`. Scene builder clean (`rebuilt and saved`), no exceptions. Windows build succeeded. Headless run of the compiled game: zero error/exception/nullreference lines across ~15 seconds. Bud colour fix specifically re-verified by grepping the generated `.asset` files' `ripeColor` field after a second, independent scene rebuild to confirm it now actually survives (it does - `black_sugar` reads `{r: 0.85, g: 0.45, b: 0.078}`, `purple` reads `{r: 0.549, g: 0.122, b: 0.678}`).
- Known issues (honest):
  - **Nothing here is hands-on playtested.** In particular: the death fee going through and Money going negative on a second death without paying it off; the land mission marker actually appearing at the Land Office; a "you short" decline line actually showing when broke at a strain boss; the bud colours reading as intended in real lighting (verified only as correct RGB values in the data, not rendered); and an officer actually triggering the plantation-proximity confiscation by walking close (officers patrol a market-centred beat today - whether that beat ever brings one within 15m of `plantationCenter` in practice is unconfirmed; if it never happens, the radius or the officers' patrol route needs adjusting).
  - `land_hillside` and `prop_safehouse` (also in the Land Office's stock) are unrelated to this batch and untouched - only `land_montine`'s mission marker was fixed.
  - Bike/van/pirogue purchases exist in the Car Dealer's stock already but remain non-functional (economy entries only, per MINI-013's original note) - explicitly out of scope for this batch per the user's "leave that for later."
- Next action: user playtests - die once and check the money owed and the "$500 owed" message; check M3's marker now points at Land and Surveys; try buying a strain seed broke and confirm the decline line, then with enough money; look at a ripe Black Sugar and Purple plant for the new bud colours; and see whether an officer ever wanders close enough to the farm to trigger a confiscation (may need a patrol-route adjustment if not).

### MINI-040 - Cell phone to call your partner

- Date: 2026-08-16
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User confirmed MINI-039 works hands-on ("almost perfect"), reported that reinforcement police officers disappear, but explicitly asked to do the next task before addressing that bug. Continuing the ranked list: "Ability to buy cell phone to call other partner to come to you if he is working on the farm."
- Implementation:
  - `CellPhoneController.cs` (new): press **C** to instantly bring the inactive boy to the active character's side - including pulling him off farm work (`FarmhandController.SetWorking(false)`) if he was assigned there, which is the specific case the user named. Does nothing (silently) if `phone_basic` isn't owned yet, so the key has no effect and no confusing message before the player has a reason to press it. Reuses the same teleport-next-to-the-active-character pattern `GuadeloupeTrade.CompleteTrip` already established (disable `CharacterController` → reposition → re-enable) rather than pathfinding him over - there's no navmesh in this project. Refuses with a "no signal" message if the inactive character is currently away on the Guadeloupe run (`CharacterSwitchManager.IsLocked`). 15-second cooldown between calls, purely to stop key-spam.
  - `phone_basic` added to the Farm Shop's stock ($200, new `ShopCategory.Communication` - a one-time unlock like Land/Property, not a consumable like Food/Enhancement, so it correctly goes through `EconomyManager`'s owned-items path rather than being usable repeatedly or refunding).
  - `ControlsPanelController`'s H-overlay lists the new key.
- **Investigated but did not fix (per the user's explicit "do the next task first" instruction):** read `PoliceReinforcementSpawner.cs` to understand the reported disappearing-reinforcements bug. Root cause looks clear: `wanted` (how many reinforcements should be active) is recalculated every frame purely from the *current* heat value with no hysteresis - `heat >= 55` spawns one, and the instant heat ticks back below 55 (which `PoliceHeatController` does at 9/sec the moment the player steps away from any officer), that same officer is `SetActive(false)` immediately, which reads as vanishing rather than leaving. There's also no walk-away/fade, just an instant toggle both directions. Likely fix next time: separate, lower despawn thresholds (hysteresis) so heat has to drop further before a reinforcement leaves, and ideally have them walk off rather than pop out of existence. Not touched this pass.
- Files changed: `Scripts/Character/CellPhoneController.cs` (new); `Scripts/Economy/ShopItemDefinition.cs` (new `Communication` category); `Scripts/UI/ControlsPanelController.cs`; `Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs` (shop stock, singleton wiring); `Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Verification commands: `Logs/MINI-040-Compile.log`, `Logs/MINI-040-BuildScene.log`, `Logs/MINI-040-WindowsBuild.log`, `Logs/MINI-040-PlayerRun.log` (headless run of the built player, ~15s).
- Verification results: compile clean, 0 `error CS`. Scene builder clean (`rebuilt and saved`), no exceptions. Windows build succeeded. Headless run of the compiled game: zero error/exception/nullreference lines across ~15 seconds.
- Known issues (honest): not hands-on playtested - buy the phone at the Farm Shop, send the other boy to the farm (or just leave Tab on one), walk away, press C, and confirm he teleports in and stops farming. The 15s cooldown and the teleport-in-place (no walk-over animation, no `Seated`-style transition) are first-pass choices, not confirmed against how it feels.
- Next action: user playtests the phone; then the reinforcement-disappearing bug (root cause already identified above) is the next claim.

### MINI-041 - Reinforcement officers no longer disappear

- Date: 2026-08-16
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User asked to do the next task(s). Fixing the reinforcement-officer-disappearing bug diagnosed (but deliberately not fixed) at the end of MINI-040 was the flagged next claim.
- Root cause (as diagnosed last pass, confirmed while fixing): `PoliceReinforcementSpawner` recalculated how many reinforcements should be active every frame purely from the *current* heat value, with the same threshold used for both directions (`heat >= 55` spawns one; the instant heat ticks back under 55, that same officer is `SetActive(false)` immediately). `PoliceHeatController` cools heat at 9/sec the moment the player steps away from any officer, so a reinforcement could spawn and vanish within a second or two of appearing - read by the user as "disappear."
- Implementation:
  - **Hysteresis**: added separate, strictly lower despawn thresholds (`firstDespawnHeat` 35, `secondDespawnHeat` 65, vs. spawn thresholds 55/85 unchanged). `wanted` is now computed by only ever *raising* it via the spawn thresholds and only ever *lowering* it via the despawn thresholds, carrying forward from the previous frame's `_activeCount` - a heat dip back under the spawn line no longer undoes a spawn; heat has to fall meaningfully further.
  - **Walk-off instead of instant vanish**: when an officer is due to leave, `WalkOffAndDeactivate` disables its `PoliceOfficer` AI, turns it to face away from the player, drives it with `CharacterController.SimpleMove` for `walkOffSeconds` (2.5s), then deactivates it - reads as the officer leaving, not popping out of existence. Re-entering the "wanted" state mid-walk-off cancels the coroutine and hands the officer straight back to normal spawn logic rather than letting it despawn out from under a heat spike.
  - Tracked a bug in my own first draft before shipping it: the "already in the state we want, nothing to do" check compared only against `activeSelf`, which stays `true` for the whole walk-off duration - a heat re-spike mid-walk-off would have been silently ignored instead of cancelling the departure. Fixed by tracking the leaving coroutine explicitly and checking both flags.
  - `Mini041ReinforcementValidation.cs` (new, editor-only): drives a real `PoliceReinforcementSpawner` through a heat sequence via a real `EconomyManager` (reflection-invoked `Start`/`Update`, same pattern as `Mini038CombatValidation`) and asserts `_activeCount` at each step, specifically proving the case that was broken (heat dipping from 60 back to 50 must *not* undo the spawn) alongside the ordinary spawn/despawn transitions. **PASS.**
- Files changed: `Scripts/Interaction/PoliceReinforcementSpawner.cs` (rewritten); `Editor/Mini041ReinforcementValidation.cs` (new); `Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Verification commands: `Logs/MINI-041-Compile2.log`, `Logs/MINI-041-BuildScene.log`, `Logs/MINI-041-Validation.log`, `Logs/MINI-041-WindowsBuild.log`, `Logs/MINI-041-PlayerRun.log` (headless run of the built player, ~15s).
- Verification results: compile clean, 0 `error CS`. Scene builder clean. Validation harness: **PASS** - `MINI-041 REINFORCEMENT VALIDATION PASS: spawn thresholds raise, despawn thresholds are strictly lower (hysteresis holds), no premature undo of a spawn from a small heat dip.` Windows build succeeded. Headless run: zero error/exception lines across ~15 seconds.
- Known issues (honest): the validation harness proves the spawn/despawn *decision* logic (the actual bug), not the visual walk-off - `StartCoroutine` doesn't tick outside Play mode, so `WalkOffAndDeactivate`'s movement itself is unverified beyond "doesn't throw in the headless run." User should watch a reinforcement actually leave and confirm it walks off rather than freezing mid-stride or clipping through something.
- Next action: user playtests - get heat above 55 for a reinforcement, let it dip slightly (should stay), then let it cool properly (should walk off, not vanish).
