### MINI-045 - Chain swing physics

- Date: 2026-08-16
- Owner: Claude
- Request: Continuing the task list. The user's original ask was "attach the chain/accessories to character bones with suitable IK/physics" - bone attachment already existed from MINI-022 (`CharacterEquipment`, `Animator.GetBoneTransform`), so this is specifically the physics half.
- Implementation:
  - `AccessorySwing.cs` (new): a lightweight damped spring, not IK - a passive necklace has nothing to reach for, so inverse kinematics (which solves a bone chain toward a target - a hand gripping a weapon, a foot on uneven ground) isn't actually the applicable technique here; a dangle/pendulum is. Tracks a world-space `_swingOffset` from the accessory's normal rest position, driven each `LateUpdate` by how fast that rest position itself is moving (a cheap, velocity-driven approximation of a real acceleration-driven pendulum, which is standard for this kind of jiggle and far easier to tune), pulled back by a stiffness/damping spring, and clamped to a max distance. Semi-implicit Euler integration for stability. The pure step is exposed as `Simulate(dt, restWorldPos)`, split out from `LateUpdate` specifically so it's testable - `Time.deltaTime` is 0 outside Play mode, so a harness calling `LateUpdate` directly would never do anything.
  - `CharacterEquipment.cs`: when the `chain_gold` accessory spawns, adds `AccessorySwing` and initializes it with the bone anchor and the rest `localPosition` `PositionOnBone` just set - no duplicated offset table. Other accessories (cap, shades, watch) are left rigidly bone-attached; a cap/shades pair swinging independently of the head would look wrong, and a watch's motion is dominated by the arm's own animation already.
  - `Mini045AccessorySwingValidation.cs` (new, editor-only): drives `Simulate` directly with an explicit dt across three phases - anchor stationary (offset must stay ~0, not drift), anchor dragged at jogging speed for half a second (offset must grow but never exceed the clamp, and must not go NaN/Infinity), then anchor stopped for 3 simulated seconds (offset must decay back to ~0, proving damping actually works rather than oscillating forever or diverging). **PASS** on the first run - no bugs caught this time.
- Files changed: `Scripts/Character/AccessorySwing.cs` (new); `Scripts/Character/CharacterEquipment.cs`; `Editor/Mini045AccessorySwingValidation.cs` (new); `Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Verification commands: `Logs/MINI-045-Compile.log`, `Logs/MINI-045-Validation.log`, `Logs/MINI-045-BuildScene.log`, `Logs/MINI-045-WindowsBuild.log`, `Logs/MINI-045-PlayerRun.log` (headless run of the built player, ~15s).
- Verification results: compile clean, 0 `error CS`. Validation: **PASS** - `MINI-045 ACCESSORY SWING VALIDATION PASS: stays at rest when the anchor doesn't move, swings up to 0.0168m (clamped at 0.045m) while being dragged at jogging speed, settles back to rest within 3s of the anchor stopping.` Scene builder clean. Windows build succeeded. Headless run: zero error/exception lines across ~15 seconds.
- Known issues (honest): the validation proves the spring math is sound (bounded, stable, settles), not that it *looks* good on the actual chain mesh at actual running/jumping/punching speeds - nobody has watched it swing in real time yet. `maxSwingDistance` (4.5cm) and the stiffness/damping values are first-pass numbers, not tuned against a real playtest.
- Next action: user buys/equips a gold chain, runs and jumps around, and confirms the chain visibly swings and settles rather than looking either rigid or wildly overdone; tune `stiffness`/`damping`/`maxSwingDistance` on `AccessorySwing` if so.

### MINI-046 - Stronger together: companion auto-assist and a damage bonus

- Date: 2026-08-16
- Owner: Claude
- Request: Continuing the task list: "stronger when two main characters together battling police or gang. other member automatically helps in situation."
- Implementation:
  - `CompanionCombatAssist.cs` (new): while a character is the follower (not controlled) and not off farming or already knocked down, it scans for the nearest live police officer (throttled rescan, same pattern as `PoliceHeatController`) and, once in range, faces and swings at it automatically - same `HumanoidAnimationManager`/`SimpleMeleeCombat.ActionId` animation pathway as the player's own F key, same `NpcCombatHealth.Hit` and heat-to-max escalation. Deliberately does not chase a target down - it only fights what's already close, relying on the fact that it's already following the player everywhere, so anything harassing the player is usually already nearby. Wired onto both protagonists in `Mini011PhaseBSetup`, same as every other per-character component.
  - `SimpleMeleeCombat.cs`: added a `togetherDamageMultiplier` (1.5x default) applied whenever the companion is within `togetherRange` (5m), on his feet, not farming, and not locked away (e.g. on the Guadeloupe run) - a real backup bonus, not just a visual one. `Attack()` (the swing logic, already split out from `Update()` in MINI-031 for testability) now calls a new public `CalculateAppliedDamage()` so the bonus decision is independently verifiable.
  - `Mini046CompanionCombatValidation.cs` (new, editor-only): proves the companion declines to fight while farming, then lands a real hit (observed via `NpcCombatHealth`'s private `health` field through reflection - it has no public getter) once eligible; and proves `CalculateAppliedDamage()` returns base damage with the companion far/locked, ~1.5x with the companion nearby and free, and drops back to base once the companion is far away again. **Caught and fixed a real bug in the test itself, twice**, not the game code: the first run found `Physics.OverlapSphere` returning nothing against a test officer with no collider at all (fixed by adding one); the second run still returned nothing because colliders created in the same edit-mode frame aren't reliably registered in the physics broadphase outside Play mode - a real Unity edit-mode limitation, not a bug, matching the same class of issue already hit twice this session (`Time.deltaTime` and `StartCoroutine` both silently do nothing outside Play). Fixed by testing `CalculateAppliedDamage()` directly instead of routing the assertion through `Attack()`'s physics-based target search. **PASS** after that fix.
- Files changed: `Scripts/Character/CompanionCombatAssist.cs` (new); `Scripts/Combat/SimpleMeleeCombat.cs`; `Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs` (wiring); `Editor/Mini046CompanionCombatValidation.cs` (new); `Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Verification commands: `Logs/MINI-046-Compile3.log`, `Logs/MINI-046-Validation3.log`, `Logs/MINI-046-BuildScene.log`, `Logs/MINI-046-WindowsBuild.log`, `Logs/MINI-046-PlayerRun.log` (headless run of the built player, ~15s).
- Verification results: compile clean, 0 `error CS`. Validation: **PASS** - `MINI-046 COMPANION COMBAT VALIDATION PASS: companion declines to fight while farming, lands a real hit once eligible, and the player's own damage is 52.5 together vs. 35 solo (~1.50x) - and drops back to solo once the companion is far away again.` Scene builder clean. Windows build succeeded. Headless run: zero error/exception lines across ~15 seconds.
- Known issues (honest): not hands-on playtested - in particular whether the companion's automatic engagement (facing, timing, animation) reads well in real time next to the player's own fight, and whether 1.5x feels like a meaningful "together" bonus rather than trivial or overpowered. This is specifically about *police* - "or gang" from the user's original request has no gang NPCs yet (tracked separately as the rival-gang task).
- Next action: user gets heat up with the companion nearby, punches an officer, and confirms both (a) the companion joins in on its own and (b) the fight goes noticeably faster than solo; then tests the companion staying out of it while farming or away on the Guadeloupe run.

### MINI-047 - Interbreeding: Purple Black

- Date: 2026-08-16
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User asked for this specifically next, out of the remaining list order: "interbreeding weed plants... purple black which is a mix of purple and black should have orange and purple buds which should be a skill unlocked at a later mission."
- Design decision: the economy already tracks crops as inventory counts, not individual plant objects (no per-plant genetics to simulate), so "interbreeding" is implemented as a crafting-style combine - bring one harvested Purple and one harvested Black Sugar (the grown product, not seed packets) to a breeding station, and it produces Purple Black seed. This is the honest fit for how this game's farming model actually works, not a literal cross-pollination simulation between two plots.
- Implementation:
  - `CropDefinition.cs`: added `secondaryRipeColor` (alpha-0 = unset, the default - every existing crop is untouched) and `HasSecondaryRipeColor`.
  - `CropStageVisual.cs`: new `ApplyStage(stage, unripe, ripe, secondaryRipe)` overload alternates ripe fruit between the two colours by renderer index when a secondary colour is set, instead of one flat colour - both parent strains visible on the same plant rather than a blended third colour. The original 3-arg `ApplyStage` is preserved as a thin wrapper so every other crop's call sites and look are unchanged.
  - `FarmPlot.cs`: all three `ApplyStage` call sites now pass the crop's `secondaryRipeColor` through - a no-op for every crop except Purple Black.
  - `ProgressionManager.cs`: added `PurpleBlackUnlocked` (`GangReputation >= 40 && BossExploitationStage >= 4`) - further out than Purple's own unlock, satisfying "a skill unlocked at a later mission" the same way Black Sugar/Purple already work, without inventing a separate mission object.
  - `CropBreedingStation.cs` (new, `InteractableBase`): a bench near the farm. Locked (and says so) until `PurpleBlackUnlocked`. Declines cleanly - without consuming anything - if either parent crop isn't on hand. On success, consumes exactly one Purple and one Black Sugar and grants 2 Purple Black seed.
  - `Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`: `CropSpecs` gained `purple_black` (price 90, illegal, ripe purple `8C1FAD` alternating with orange `D97314`) and a 7th tuple column for the optional secondary hex (`null` for every crop before this one). `BuildBreedingStation` places the bench beside the farm - has to run *after* `BuildEconomyAndCrops`, since `BuildFarmPathAndClearing` (where the farm itself gets built) runs before crops exist; uses the `_farmCenter` static field already captured during farm building.
  - `CropSelectionController.cs`: extended from 6 to 7 number-key slots (`Keys` array literally only had 6 entries) so Purple Black is actually selectable once you have seed - the array size is already dynamic from `crops.Length` in the scene builder, only the hardcoded key list needed extending. `ControlsPanelController`'s H-overlay updated from "1 - 6" to "1 - 7".
  - `Mini047InterbreedingValidation.cs` (new, editor-only): proves the breeding station is inert pre-unlock (no consumption, no grant), declines and consumes nothing with a missing ingredient even once unlocked, consumes exactly one of each parent and grants exactly 2 seed on success; and proves the two-tone fruit alternation actually shows both colours while confirming the original single-colour 3-arg `ApplyStage` overload is unaffected (a real regression check, not just new-feature coverage). **PASS** on the first run.
- Files changed: `Scripts/Economy/CropDefinition.cs`, `Scripts/Economy/CropSelectionController.cs`, `Scripts/Farming/CropStageVisual.cs`, `Scripts/Farming/FarmPlot.cs`, `Scripts/Progression/ProgressionManager.cs`, `Scripts/UI/ControlsPanelController.cs`; `Scripts/Farming/CropBreedingStation.cs` (new); `Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs` (`CropSpecs`, `BuildBreedingStation`, call site); `Editor/Mini047InterbreedingValidation.cs` (new); `Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Verification commands: `Logs/MINI-047-Compile.log`, `Logs/MINI-047-Validation.log`, `Logs/MINI-047-BuildScene.log`, `Logs/MINI-047-WindowsBuild.log`, `Logs/MINI-047-PlayerRun.log` (headless run of the built player, ~15s).
- Verification results: compile clean, 0 `error CS`. Validation: **PASS** - `MINI-047 INTERBREEDING VALIDATION PASS: breeding station locked pre-unlock, declines a missing ingredient without consuming what it does have, consumes exactly one of each parent and grants 2 seed once eligible; two-tone fruit shows both parent colours; single-colour crops are unaffected.` Scene builder clean. Windows build succeeded. Headless run: zero error/exception lines across ~15 seconds.
- Known issues (honest): not hands-on playtested or visually rendered - the two-tone bud alternation is proven correct at the data/material level (the right colours land on the right renderers) but nobody has looked at an actual ripe Purple Black plant in the game. Reaching `PurpleBlackUnlocked` in a real playthrough hasn't been tried either - the thresholds (gang reputation 40, boss exploitation stage 4) are first-pass numbers, not tuned against how fast reputation actually accumulates in practice. The breeding station's placement (6m out, 6m over from farm centre) hasn't been visually confirmed clear of other farm structures.
- Next action: user plays far enough into the weed route to unlock Purple Black, harvests one Purple and one Black Sugar, visits the breeding station, and confirms the seed grant and the resulting plant's two-tone buds.

### MINI-048 - Blue Cheese + two more hybrids, breeding station generalised

- Date: 2026-08-16
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User asked to add a new base strain "Blue Cheese," plus two more interbred hybrids - Sugar Cheese (Black Sugar x Blue Cheese) and Purple Cheese, with the parentage of Purple Cheese left ambiguous in the request. Asked the user directly rather than guessing (three real options existed - Purple x Blue Cheese, Purple x Sugar Cheese, or Purple Black x Blue Cheese - each a different design with real rework cost if guessed wrong); confirmed **Purple x Blue Cheese**, mirroring Sugar Cheese's own naming exactly.
- Implementation:
  - `CropBreedingStation.cs` **generalised from one fixed recipe to a `Recipe[]` list** - Purple Black, Sugar Cheese, and Purple Cheese now share one bench instead of needing three. One `[E]` press finds the first recipe that is both unlocked and has both ingredients on hand and breeds it; if none currently qualify, it reports the closest miss (an unlocked recipe short an ingredient) in preference over a generic "nothing unlocked" - only falls back to the generic line if nothing is unlocked at all.
  - `ProgressionManager.cs`: `BlueCheeseUnlocked` (a new base strain, parallel tier to Purple - `GangReputation >= 30 && BossExploitationStage >= 3`), `SugarCheeseUnlocked` (`BossKReputation >= 30 && GangReputation >= 30 && BossExploitationStage >= 4`), `PurpleCheeseUnlocked` (deepest, `GangReputation >= 50 && BossExploitationStage >= 5`) - an escalating ladder matching the order the user asked for them in (Blue Cheese, then Sugar Cheese, then Purple Cheese).
  - `Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`: `CropSpecs` gained `blue_cheese` (single blue ripe colour `2E6BAD`), `sugar_cheese` (blue alternating with Black Sugar's own orange `D97314`), `purple_cheese` (blue alternating with Purple's own purple `8C1FAD`) - reusing each parent's existing colour rather than inventing new ones, so the hybrid visibly reads as "this strain plus that one." A new `BuildStrainBoss` call adds **BossQ**, granting Blue Cheese seed for $650 (priced above Purple's $500 to match its deeper unlock tier) - same boss-grants-a-base-strain pattern as BossM/BossP. `BuildBreedingStation` now assembles all three recipes onto the one bench.
  - `CropSelectionController.cs`: number-key slots extended from 7 to **10** (1-9, then 0) - the `Keys` array was hardcoded and needed the two new entries; the crop list itself was already dynamically sized. Flagged in a comment as a real UX limit worth revisiting (a proper crop-select menu would scale better, especially for the mobile-first target) rather than silently letting it grow unbounded forever.
  - `Mini048BreedingValidation.cs` (new, editor-only, replaces the now-stale MINI-047 harness that tested fields removed by this refactor): proves no recipe fires while everything is locked even with every ingredient stocked; the one unlocked+eligible recipe fires and touches *only* its own two ingredients (not a third, untouched strain sitting in inventory); when multiple recipes become eligible at once, the first one in the list order fires, not a later one; and a recipe that's unlocked but missing an ingredient reports a specific "need one grown X and one grown Y" message rather than the generic locked line. Caught a real bug in the test itself on the first run (not the game code): the closest-miss assertion tried to trigger Purple Cheese's miss without first depleting Sugar Cheese's own ingredients, so Sugar Cheese kept firing instead of ever reaching Purple Cheese's turn - fixed by depleting Black Sugar too before that assertion. **PASS** after the fix.
- Files changed: `Scripts/Farming/CropBreedingStation.cs` (rewritten to `Recipe[]`), `Scripts/Progression/ProgressionManager.cs`, `Scripts/Economy/CropSelectionController.cs`, `Scripts/UI/ControlsPanelController.cs`; `Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs` (`CropSpecs`, `BossQ`, `BuildBreedingStation`); `Editor/Mini048BreedingValidation.cs` (new, replaces the deleted `Mini047InterbreedingValidation.cs`); `Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Verification commands: `Logs/MINI-048-Compile3.log`, `Logs/MINI-048-Validation2.log`, `Logs/MINI-048-BuildScene.log`, `Logs/MINI-048-WindowsBuild.log`, `Logs/MINI-048-PlayerRun.log` (headless run of the built player, ~15s).
- Verification results: compile clean, 0 `error CS`. Validation: **PASS** - `MINI-048 BREEDING VALIDATION PASS: no recipe fires while locked; the one unlocked+eligible recipe fires and touches only its own ingredients; recipe list order is respected when multiple are eligible; a locked-ingredient miss reports what's missing rather than a generic message.` Scene builder clean. Generated `.asset` colours spot-checked directly and match the intended two-tone pairings (`sugar_cheese`: blue `2E6BAD` + orange `D97314`; `purple_cheese`: blue `2E6BAD` + purple `8C1FAD`). Windows build succeeded. Headless run: zero error/exception lines across ~15 seconds.
- Known issues (honest): not hands-on playtested - none of the three unlock ladders (Blue Cheese, Sugar Cheese, Purple Cheese) have been reached in a real playthrough, so the thresholds are unverified against actual reputation-gain pacing. BossQ's placement (road index 8) hasn't been visually confirmed clear of neighbouring structures. The 10-slot crop-selection hotkey list is now at a real UX ceiling for a keyboard, and doesn't scale further without a different selection UI - flagged, not solved, in this pass.
- Next action: user plays deep enough into the weed route to unlock Blue Cheese, buys the seed from BossQ, grows enough of it plus Black Sugar/Purple to reach the breeding station, and confirms both new hybrid crosses work and read visually as their two parent colours.

### MINI-049 - Cheat code: C#0W@

- Date: 2026-08-16
- Owner: Claude
- Request: "put a code so i can unlock all with $100,000 and unstoppable. C#0W@"
- Implementation:
  - `CheatCodeController.cs` (new): typing `C#0W@` anywhere in-game (a rolling character buffer, driven by `Input.inputString` rather than per-key `KeyCode` checks - `#`/`@` aren't their own physical keys on most layouts, they're Shift+digit combinations that vary by keyboard, and `inputString` already resolves whatever the OS/layout actually produced) sets Money to exactly $100,000, unlocks every strain and route, and flips both boys to invincible with unlimited stamina. Deliberately **not** listed in the H-controls overlay - it's a cheat, not a documented feature.
  - `EconomyManager.SetMoney(int)` (new): sets an exact total rather than adding a delta, so re-entering the code doesn't stack money on top of itself.
  - `ProgressionManager.UnlockEverything()` (new): directly sets every reputation to 100 and `BossExploitationStage` to exactly 5 - the deepest threshold any current unlock actually checks (`PurpleCheeseUnlocked`), not higher. Also picks `CareerPath.WeedRoute` if no career choice has been made yet, since farm assignment (`CompanionInteractable`, MINI-044) gates on that separately from any reputation number. **Real, unavoidable tradeoff surfaced and called out rather than hidden:** `BossPayoutMultiplier` is `0` for any `BossExploitationStage >= 3` by the game's own existing design (Boss K's cut worsens the more exploited jobs you've done) - unlocking every strain and keeping a live Boss K payout are mutually exclusive under the progression model as it already exists, not something this cheat introduces or can dodge. Given the cheat also hands over $100,000 directly, this shouldn't matter in practice.
  - `CharacterVitals.cs`: added `static bool GlobalInvincible` (checked at the top of `Damage()` - a genuine no-op, not just a flag nothing reads) and `static bool GlobalUnlimitedStamina` (keeps `Stamina` pinned to max every frame instead of draining). Static and global rather than per-instance so both boys are unstoppable together and it survives Tab-switching, rather than needing to be set on whichever character happens to be active.
  - `Mini049CheatCodeValidation.cs` (new, editor-only): proves a near-miss code does nothing; that junk characters typed before the real code don't break the rolling-buffer match (simulates a player typing normally, then the code, not the code being the very first thing typed); that the real code sets Money to exactly 100000 (not just "increased"), unlocks every strain/route property, picks a career path if undecided, and sets both static flags; and - the one that actually proves the flag *does* something - that a subsequent 9999-damage hit on a fresh character genuinely leaves them at full health while `GlobalInvincible` is set, not just "the flag is true." Wraps the whole run in try/finally to reset the static flags afterward regardless of outcome, since a static leaking `true` into the rest of the same editor session would be a real (if narrow) footgun. **PASS** on the first run.
- Files changed: `Scripts/UI/CheatCodeController.cs` (new); `Scripts/Economy/EconomyManager.cs`, `Scripts/Progression/ProgressionManager.cs`, `Scripts/Character/CharacterVitals.cs`; `Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs` (wiring); `Editor/Mini049CheatCodeValidation.cs` (new); `Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Verification commands: `Logs/MINI-049-Compile2.log`, `Logs/MINI-049-Validation.log`, `Logs/MINI-049-BuildScene.log`, `Logs/MINI-049-WindowsBuild.log`, `Logs/MINI-049-PlayerRun.log` (headless run of the built player, ~15s).
- Verification results: compile clean, 0 `error CS`. Validation: **PASS** - `MINI-049 CHEAT CODE VALIDATION PASS: a near-miss does nothing, junk typed before the real code doesn't break the rolling-buffer match, and the real code sets money to exactly $100,000, unlocks every strain/route, picks a career path if undecided, and makes damage a genuine no-op.` Scene builder clean. Windows build succeeded. Headless run: zero error/exception lines across ~15 seconds.
- Known issues (honest): not hands-on typed in the actual running build - `Input.inputString` behaves identically in a real build to how it's documented (this is a standard, well-established Unity API for exactly this use case), but nobody has literally typed the five characters into the compiled game and watched the alert fire yet. "Unstoppable" was scoped to health/stamina (can't be damaged, never gets tired) - it does not affect police heat generation or detection, so you can still be chased/searched while invincible; if "unstoppable" was meant to include immunity from heat/police attention too, say so and it's a small addition (a `GlobalIgnoreHeat`-style flag in `PoliceHeatController`).
- Next action: user launches the build and types `C#0W@` in-game to confirm the alert fires, money reads $100,000, a previously-locked crop (e.g. Purple Cheese) is selectable, and taking a hit from police no longer costs health.

### MINI-050 — Banana plant uses a real banana-tree model

- Date: 2026-08-17
- Owner: OpenClaw
- Request: Banana crop "looks like a tomato plant with yellow"; use the banana plant from the big Up Iz Up game like the tomato/weed treatment. User approved using the best of `bananatree1/2/3.fbx`.
- Root cause: `FarmPlot` chose a crop visual purely by legality (`isIllegal ? weedVisual : tomatoVisual`), so banana (legal) rendered the tomato plant mesh recoloured yellow.
- Implementation:
  - `FarmPlot.cs`: replaced the hardcoded legal/illegal binary with a **per-crop visual registry** — a new serialized `CropVisualEntry[] cropVisuals` (cropId → visual). `VisualFor(crop)` checks the registry first, then falls back to weed-for-illegal / tomato-for-legal, so every existing crop's look is unchanged. All visual-selection sites (plant, save/load) and hide-all logic now route through it. No user-theft: nothing else regressed.
  - Imported the big game's banana tree FBXs (Tropical Nature Pack) into `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Art/BananaImported/` (0.2-0.5MB each — small low-poly game meshes, no decimation needed, unlike the 183MB tomato/weed photogrammetry scans).
  - `Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`: `BuildBananaVisual` builds a `CropStageVisual` from **bananatree2.fbx** (the highest-detail of the three: 2781 verts vs 1477/1479), scaled to ~2m crop height, with a 3-sphere hanging bunch that ripens to the crop's yellow; `RegisterCropVisual(plot, "banana", visual)` wires it into both the main farm plots and the expansion (LockedFarmPlot) plots.
  - `Mini011AssetSnapshot.cs`: added `SnapshotBananaCrop` (forces plot 0 to ripe banana and renders) for visual verification.
- Files changed: `Scripts/Farming/FarmPlot.cs`, `Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`, `Editor/Mini011AssetSnapshot.cs`; `Art/BananaImported/bananatree1/2/3.fbx` (+meta, new); `Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Scene/prefab changes: GrandBayProof rebuilt via the standard scene-builder script; both farm and expansion plots now carry a registered banana visual.
- Verification commands: `Logs/MINI-050-Compile1.log`, `Logs/MINI-050-Compile2.log`, `Logs/MINI-050-BuildScene1.log`, `Logs/MINI-050-BuildScene2.log`, `Logs/MINI-050-BananaSnapshot.log`, `Logs/MINI-050-BananaSnapshot2.log`, `Logs/MINI-050-WindowsBuild2.log`, `Logs/Snapshots/mini050-banana-crop.png`.
- Verification results: compile clean everywhere (0 `error CS`). Scene builder clean (`rebuilt and saved`, no exceptions) twice. Visual snapshot: the banana now renders as a taller plant with elongated pointed leaves and a clustered yellow hanging bunch — clearly distinct from the tomato plant (it no longer reads as tomato-with-yellow), though the model is a low-poly tree so it is stylised rather than a photogrammetric banana. Windows build succeeded (`MINI-001 BUILD SUCCEEDED`, 161MB, exit 0).
- Known issues (honest): the three `bananatree*.fbx` are decorative low-poly trees — they have a thin branching base and no thick pseudostem or distinct curved banana fingers, so up close it reads as a stylised tropical plant rather than a botanically-correct banana. The per-crop system is already down for a better model to be dropped in later (a swap needs no code change). Not hands-on playtested; the ripe-state render is the visual proof.
- Next action: move to MINI-051 (weed bud colours — colour bud parts of the plant per strain so the ripe plant shows the strain's bud colour, verified by snapshot).

### MINI-051 — Real Zeb strain flower/bud visuals

- Date: 2026-08-17
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User's `Docs/MASTER-DEVELOPMENT-BRIEF.md` roadmap: "Fix the problem where the strain's designated bud color is not visible enough. Do not recolor the complete Zeb plant. Support separate visual concepts for foliage / flowering buds/colas / primary bud color / optional secondary bud color / pistils / trichome-frost impression. Research real strain/bud references first. The plant must remain botanically believable rather than becoming a solid purple/blue/orange plant. Reuse the existing crop visual/breeding architecture. Visual proof is required. No EXE build required unless the user requests it."
- Root cause (found, not assumed): weed's `BuildCropVisual` call in `Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs` passed `fruitCount: 0`. `CropStageVisual.ApplyStage` only ever colours `fruitRenderers` — with zero of them, **every weed strain's designated ripe colour (Black Sugar orange, Purple purple, Blue Cheese blue, the two-tone hybrids, etc.) was never rendered at all**, on any of the 8 illegal strains, since MINI-011 Phase C. This is a stronger bug than "not visible enough" as reported in the roadmap brief — it was fully absent.
- Implementation (reused the existing per-crop-visual/breeding architecture from MINI-050/MINI-047/MINI-048 — no new crop pipeline built):
  - `CropStageVisual.cs`: added two new optional, opt-in layers so tomato/banana/carrot (which don't set them) are visually unchanged: (1) `pistilRenderers[]` — coloured cream while flowering, rust-orange once ripe, independent of the strain's own bud colour, matching how real cannabis pistils darken with maturity regardless of bud genetics; (2) `applyFrostEffect` — at full ripeness, lightens the bud colour ~22% toward white and raises `_Glossiness`, standing in for a frosted/trichome-crystal surface without a custom shader.
  - `Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`: new `BuildWeedCropVisual` (replaces the old `fruitCount: 0` weed call to `BuildCropVisual`) builds three distinct **cola clusters** — one apical (top, largest) and two smaller lower lateral colas, each a tapering 3-sphere stack (dense/clustered, not a single ball) plus two pistil-hair capsule accents — instead of a uniform mid-height ring. Foliage keeps its own plain green material (`CropFoliage`, untouched) — buds/colas are a fully separate object set, satisfying "do not recolor the complete Zeb plant." `ApplyStage` still receives the crop's existing `unripeColor`/`ripeColor`/`secondaryRipeColor` from `CropDefinition` unchanged, so every strain's already-authored primary/secondary bud colour (from MINI-039/047/048) now actually reaches the screen for the first time.
  - `Mini011AssetSnapshot.cs`: added `SnapshotWeedBudCloseup` (forces plot 0 to ripe Purple and renders a close framing on the buds) and `SnapshotWeedStrainsSideBySide` (forces 4 plots to Bushers/Black Sugar/Purple/Blue Cheese and renders them together) for the required visual proof, following the doc's own "For plants: whole plant, close-up flowering section, multiple strains side-by-side" checklist.
  - Research basis (general cannabis morphology, not a specific photo reference): dense terminal bud clusters ("colas") concentrated at branch tips, particularly the apex; pistil hairs that start pale/cream during flowering and darken to rust-orange as the plant matures, largely independent of the strain's own bud-colour genetics; a frosted trichome sheen on mature buds. Used to shape the cola/pistil/frost split above rather than a single flat-coloured blob.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Farming/CropStageVisual.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011AssetSnapshot.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Art/Materials/CropBud.mat` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Art/Materials/CropPistil.mat` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Scene/prefab changes: `GrandBayProof.unity` rebuilt via the standard scene-builder script; every farm/expansion plot's `weedVisual` now carries the new three-cola/pistil geometry instead of the old zero-fruit weed visual.
- Verification commands: `Logs/MINI-051-Compile1.log` (batch compile), `Logs/MINI-051-BuildScene1.log` (scene builder), `Logs/MINI-051-Snapshot1.log` + `Logs/Snapshots/mini051-weed-bud-closeup.png`, `Logs/MINI-051-Snapshot2.log` + `Logs/Snapshots/mini051-weed-strains-side-by-side.png`.
- Verification results: compile clean (0 `error CS`). Scene builder logged `MINI-011 Phase B: GrandBayProof rebuilt and saved`, no exceptions. Both renders inspected directly: the close-up shows three distinct cola clusters with visible orange pistil tips and a glossy/lightened ripe-purple tone (Purple strain) on a plant whose foliage stays plain green — not a solid-coloured plant. The side-by-side render shows four strains on the same farm row each showing its own distinct designated colour at a glance (purple / orange / blue / muted green-tan for Bushers) — this is the first time any of the 8 weed strains' colours have ever actually rendered, confirmed by comparing against the pre-fix behaviour (zero fruit renderers, colour never applied).
- Known issues (honest): a stray, undocumented prior MINI-051 attempt's log residue (`Logs/MINI-051-WeedSnapshot*.log`, `Logs/MINI-051-WindowsBuild*.log`, `Logs/MINI-051b/c-*.log`, roughly 11 hours older than this pass) was found sitting in `Logs/` with no matching code in the working tree, no PROJECT-HANDOFF entry, and no commit — it was not authored by this pass and its outcome is unknown; left in place as-is (log files only, not code) rather than guessed at or deleted. The bud/cola geometry is stacked coloured spheres in the project's existing established low-poly crop style (same technique as tomato/banana's fruit spheres) — a stylised approximation, not a photorealistic bud/trichome model; "botanically believable" here means distinct colas at realistic plant positions with pistils and a frost sheen, not a solid-tinted plant, which is what was fixed. Frost effect is a uniform colour-lighten + gloss bump, not a per-pixel sparkle shader (Standard/Built-in pipeline, no custom shader was written for this). Not hands-on playtested — no EXE build was made, per the brief's own "No EXE build required unless the user requests it." The 4-strain side-by-side snapshot only exercises Bushers/Black Sugar/Purple/Blue Cheese (first 4 plots); the two-tone hybrids (Purple Black, Sugar Cheese, Purple Cheese) were not separately re-rendered this pass but use the same already-working `secondaryRipeColor` alternation logic from MINI-047/048, now finally visible on the new cola geometry rather than on zero renderers.
- Next action: user opens `GrandBayProof.unity` (or the next Windows build) and looks at a ripe weed plot in Play Mode/real light, ideally growing a couple of different strains to confirm the colour distinction reads well at normal gameplay camera distance, not just the close-up render above.

### MINI-052 — NPC navigation and stuck recovery

- Date: 2026-08-17
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User's `Docs/MASTER-DEVELOPMENT-BRIEF.md` roadmap: "Replace/directly improve simplistic direct steering with proper navigation. NPCs and companions must: path around houses; stop repeatedly walking into walls; avoid excessive jitter; recover if stuck; re-path when target changes. Create reusable stuck recovery based on low displacement while desired movement remains high. Recovery order: 1. recompute path; 2. try nearby valid path point; 3. rotate/recover; 4. only after repeated failure, safely relocate to a nearby valid navigation position. Test in Editor Play Mode."
- Reuse-first check (per `MASTER-DEVELOPMENT-BRIEF.md`'s own policy): `com.unity.ai.navigation` (2.0.14, Unity's official NavMesh package) is already in `Packages/manifest.json` from an earlier import, unused until now - reused, not newly added. Considered a full `NavMeshAgent` swap (the "textbook" answer) but rejected it: `CharacterController` on these characters is already referenced by 13 other scripts (combat, save/load, the cell-phone recall, farmhand assignment, swimming, world-safety bounds...), none of them hands-on playtested yet - replacing the movement backbone project-wide is a bigger architectural change than "improve the steering" calls for, and risks regressing a lot of unverified surface area for one task. Built a lighter reusable helper instead that layers real NavMesh pathing onto the *existing* CharacterController-driven scripts.
- Implementation:
  - `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Navigation/NavPathSteerer.cs` (new): a plain C# helper (not a MonoBehaviour, so any script can hold one). `Steer(currentPos, target, desiredSpeed, arriveDistance, out arrived)` samples the baked NavMesh (`NavMesh.SamplePosition`/`NavMesh.CalculatePath`, both static APIs) and returns a direction toward the next path corner instead of a straight line to the target - this is the actual "path around houses" behaviour. Internally tracks real displacement vs. intended movement to detect being stuck, and runs the brief's exact 4-rung recovery ladder (each rung must fail for its own hold time before escalating, so a one-frame snag doesn't over-react): recompute the path -> try a nearby valid NavMesh point as a temporary detour -> rotate/recover (steer off the blocked heading for half a second) -> relocate to a nearby valid NavMesh position as an absolute last resort. Re-paths automatically whenever the target moves more than ~1.5m (satisfies "re-path when target changes" for the companion following a moving player and police chasing one).
  - `Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`: new `BuildNavigationMesh()`, called last in `BuildScene()` (after every building/terrain/farm/coast object exists) - adds a `NavMeshSurface` (`Unity.AI.Navigation`), collects `PhysicsColliders` (the same building colliders `AddBoundsCollider` already gives every structure - MINI-011 Phase C), and bakes. The surface object is kept in the saved scene (not destroyed) because `NavMeshSurface` adds its baked data on `OnEnable`, which is how the static `NavMesh` API gets coverage at runtime.
  - `FollowController.cs` / `PatrolNPC.cs` / `PoliceOfficer.cs`: each now holds one `NavPathSteerer` and calls `Steer(...)` where they used to compute `(target - transform.position).normalized` directly. Speed selection, animation blending, heat/stamina, and (for police) the patrol/chase decision are all untouched - only the direction handed to `CharacterController.SimpleMove` changed. `PoliceOfficer`'s old short forward-whisker raycast avoidance (`AvoidObstacles`, reactive/one-probe-at-a-time, no real recovery) is removed - the NavMesh path already routes around buildings, and the stuck ladder handles the case the whisker never could (actually getting wedged). Each script also consumes `NavPathSteerer.PendingRelocation` once per frame and briefly disables/re-enables its `CharacterController` to write the relocated position (`CharacterController` rejects direct transform writes while enabled).
  - `Mini052NavMeshValidation.cs` (new, editor-only): a batch-mode-safe sanity check, since Editor Play Mode entry via `-executeMethod` is documented elsewhere in this project as broken in this environment (Search-module init exception, MINI-001) and this session was told not to build a Windows EXE for routine verification. Confirms the baked NavMesh has real coverage near Sacat's spawn and the farm plot, that a complete path exists between them, and - the sharper check - that `NavMesh.SamplePosition` finds NO walkable mesh inside the farm safehouse building's own footprint, proving the bake actually excludes building geometry rather than just blanket-covering the terrain rectangle.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Navigation/NavPathSteerer.cs` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Character/FollowController.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Interaction/PatrolNPC.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Interaction/PoliceOfficer.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini052NavMeshValidation.cs` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Scene/prefab changes: `GrandBayProof.unity` rebuilt with a new `NavMeshSurface` object holding the baked NavMesh; every `FollowController`/`PatrolNPC`/`PoliceOfficer` instance now steers through it.
- Verification commands: `Logs/MINI-052-Compile1..4.log` (batch compiles - a real bug was caught and fixed mid-pass, see Known issues), `Logs/MINI-052-BuildScene1..2.log` (scene builder), `Logs/MINI-052-NavValidation2.log` (the new NavMesh coverage/exclusion check), `Logs/MINI-052-SceneValidation.log` (existing `Mini001SceneValidation` regression check).
- Verification results: compile clean (0 `error CS`) on the final pass. Scene builder logged `MINI-011 Phase B: GrandBayProof rebuilt and saved`, no exceptions. `MINI-052 NAVMESH VALIDATION PASS`: coverage confirmed near both a character spawn and the farm plot, a complete 2-corner path between them, and - the meaningful check - the safehouse building's own footprint is correctly excluded from the walkable mesh (not a blanket-walkable rectangle). `Mini001SceneValidation` (unrelated pre-existing scene wiring check) still **PASS** - no regression to the broader scene from this change.
- Known issues (honest):
  - **Caught a real bug mid-pass, not left for the user to find:** `NavPathSteerer`'s `NavMeshPath` was originally a `readonly` field constructed inline (`new NavMeshPath()`), which runs during the owning `MonoBehaviour`'s construction - Unity explicitly forbids constructing a `NavMeshPath` there ("InitializeNavMeshPath is not allowed to be called from a MonoBehaviour constructor"), and the first scene rebuild threw that exception for every `FollowController`/`PatrolNPC`/`PoliceOfficer` instance. Fixed by lazily creating it on first real use instead; the second rebuild was clean.
  - **The actual dynamic behaviour (does an NPC really stop walking into walls, does a snagged NPC actually recover, does re-pathing look smooth) has not been exercised by any run this pass** - Editor Play Mode via `-executeMethod` is broken in this environment (documented since MINI-001), and this session was explicitly told not to build a Windows EXE for routine verification, so `Update()` never actually ticked for any of these scripts. The NavMesh-coverage/exclusion validation above proves the bake is real and correctly shaped; it does NOT prove the steering/recovery code runs correctly frame-to-frame. The brief's own last line is "Test in Editor Play Mode" - that is the genuine next step, not assumed done here.
  - The 2-corner Sacat-spawn-to-farm-plot path in the validation is a direct line (no detour needed for that specific pair of points, since the road/farm-path corridor was already kept clear of houses by the Phase B/C placement logic) - the safehouse-footprint-exclusion check is what actually proves obstacle avoidance, not the corner count.
  - `NavMeshSurface` was baked with default agent settings (radius ~0.5m, standard humanoid height/slope/step); it was not tuned against the officers' actual `CharacterController` radius (0.32m) or checked for tight spots (e.g., the narrow gaps between densely-packed shanty houses) - if an NPC still snags somewhere specific, narrowing the agent radius or widening that gap are the two obvious next moves, not a sign the system doesn't work.
  - `PoliceOfficer`'s old whisker-avoidance fields (`whiskerLength`, `avoidTurnDegrees`, `obstacleMask`) were removed rather than kept alongside the new system - no scene data referenced them (Mini011PhaseBSetup never set them, only used defaults), so this is not a breaking change to anything currently built.
- Next action: user opens `GrandBayProof.unity` in the Editor and presses Play (or an interactive `-executeMethod` run, if the Search-module bug has since been fixed) to actually watch the companion follow around a house, a villager patrol without jittering at a corner, and - ideally by artificially blocking a patrol NPC's path in the Scene view - watch a stuck-recovery sequence happen for real. This is the one part of MINI-052 that genuinely needs eyes on a running frame, not a static check.

### MINI-053 — Persistent mission/objective UI + dialogue foundation

- Date: 2026-08-17
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User's `Docs/MASTER-DEVELOPMENT-BRIEF.md` roadmap: "Mission details must not disappear before the player can read them. Use: temporary mission-start/completion banner; persistent small objective card; progress count; optional distance; mission name. Build a data-driven dialogue foundation that later supports: normal dialogue; inner thoughts; conditional lines; mission dialogue; shop dialogue; faction dialogue; story revelations."
- Findings before building anything: `MissionSystem`/`MissionHUD` (built back in MINI-011 Phase C) already had a persistent objective card, progress count, and distance, plus a transient briefing/completion banner - most of the brief's list already existed. Two real gaps: (1) the persistent card never showed the **mission name**, only the current objective's instruction text; (2) both the banner and the separate NPC-dialogue feedback box held for a **fixed** duration (3.2s / 3s) regardless of text length, so a longer line could genuinely fade before being read - the brief's literal complaint. (3) No dialogue system existed at all - every NPC line was a hardcoded C# string array or single string field on `TownNPCInteractable`, with no way to make a line conditional on game state.
- Implementation:
  - `MissionHUD.cs`: the persistent objective card now shows the mission's own title above the instruction line. Banner hold time changed from a flat `bannerHold = 3.2f` to `Mathf.Clamp(bannerHoldMin + text.Length * bannerHoldPerChar, bannerHoldMin, bannerHoldMax)` (3.2s floor, +0.045s per character, 9s ceiling) - a longer briefing/completion banner now actually holds longer instead of fading at a fixed clock regardless of content.
  - `InteractionDetector.cs`: the NPC-dialogue feedback box (a separate rendering path from the mission banner, same underlying "must not disappear before it's read" problem) got the identical fix - `feedbackDurationMin/PerChar/Max` replacing the flat `feedbackDuration`, applied via a new `ReadingHold(text)` helper at all three call sites (talk, farmhand toggle, plant-clone feedback).
  - **Dialogue foundation** (`Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Dialogue/`, new): `DialogueCondition` (a gate: MinReputation/MaxReputation/CareerPath/CropUnlocked/MinHeat/MaxHeat/Always, reading the game's *existing* `ProgressionManager`/`EconomyManager` state rather than a parallel dialogue-only store), `DialogueLine` (speaker, category - Normal/InnerThought/Mission/Shop/Faction/StoryReveal, exactly the brief's list - text, and a list of conditions that must ALL match), `DialogueSet` (a `ScriptableObject` asset, matching the project's existing data-definition pattern for `CropDefinition`/`ShopItemDefinition` - content authors edit lines in the Inspector). `DialogueSet.SelectLine()` picks the most-specific eligible line (most conditions wins, so a conditional line always beats a generic fallback), and cycles through whichever lines are tied for specificity - this is what lets an unconditional array of lines (the common case today) keep the exact same "says something different each time" behaviour it already had.
  - `ProgressionManager.cs`: added `GetReputation(Faction)`, a read-only mirror of `AddReputation`'s existing per-faction switch, so the new condition system (and anything else that only needs to *read* a standing) doesn't need its own copy of the faction-to-field mapping.
  - `TownNPCInteractable.cs`: added an optional `dialogueSet` field, purely additive - every NPC not explicitly given one keeps using its existing hardcoded lines exactly as before. When set, the villager (default) case prefers `dialogueSet.SelectLine()`, falling back to the old array only if nothing is eligible (which can't actually happen once an unconditional line exists in the set).
  - `Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`: `WireVillagerDialogueSet()` demonstrates the whole system on one real NPC (the ambient villager) without touching any other NPC's dialogue: the same 4 lines that were already hardcoded in `villagerLines` (as unconditional entries, so normal play looks unchanged), plus two new conditional lines proving the mechanism actually works - an `InnerThought` line gated on heat ≥ 40, and a `Faction` line gated on GrandBayGangs reputation ≥ 25.
  - `Mini053DialogueValidation.cs` (new, editor-only): a real validation harness, not just "it compiles" - instantiates live `EconomyManager`/`ProgressionManager` singletons (a real bug was caught here too, see Known issues) and proves, in order: unconditional lines cycle by default; raising heat past 40 makes the conditional line win over every unconditional line, every call; raising gang reputation too creates a genuine tie between the two conditional lines, and selection cycles between just those two (never falls back to an unconditional line while a conditional one remains eligible); and cooling both back down makes selection fall back to unconditional lines again, proving conditions are evaluated live per-call, not cached from the first selection.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Dialogue/DialogueCondition.cs` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Dialogue/DialogueLine.cs` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Dialogue/DialogueSet.cs` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Progression/ProgressionManager.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Interaction/TownNPCInteractable.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/UI/MissionHUD.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Interaction/InteractionDetector.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini053DialogueValidation.cs` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Data/Dialogue/VillagerLines.asset` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Scene/prefab changes: `GrandBayProof.unity` rebuilt; `NPC_Villager` now carries a `dialogueSet` reference. Every other NPC is untouched.
- Verification commands: `Logs/MINI-053-Compile1..4.log`, `Logs/MINI-053-BuildScene1..2.log`, `Logs/MINI-053-DialogueValidation3.log` (the real harness, after a fix - see below), `Logs/MINI-053-SceneValidation2.log` (existing `Mini001SceneValidation` regression check).
- Verification results: compile clean (0 `error CS`). Scene builder logged `MINI-011 Phase B: GrandBayProof rebuilt and saved`, no exceptions, no "NPC_Villager not found" warning (the wiring found its target). `MINI-053 DIALOGUE VALIDATION PASS` on the final run: all four scenarios above confirmed. `Mini001SceneValidation` still **PASS** - no regression.
- Known issues (honest):
  - **Caught a real bug in the validation harness itself, not the game code:** the first run of `Mini053DialogueValidation` failed - `EconomyManager.Instance` was `null` even though `economy.AddHeat(60f)` had already run successfully on the live instance. Root cause: `AddComponent<T>()` does not run `Awake()` synchronously outside Play Mode in this batch-mode context (confirmed by a debug print: the instance's own `Heat` field was 60, but the static `Instance` singleton was never assigned, because `Awake` - which sets it - never fired). Fixed by explicitly invoking `Awake` via reflection, matching the exact pattern already established in `Mini049CheatCodeValidation.InvokeMethod`. Second run passed cleanly.
  - The dialogue foundation is deliberately content-light this pass - only the ambient villager was migrated, as a proof that the mechanism works end to end. Writing real conditional/faction/story-reveal content for the other NPCs, and the dialect-lexicon-driven opening conversation, is explicitly `MINI-054` scope per the roadmap, not duplicated here.
  - The reading-time formula (`0.045s` per character) is a reasoned estimate (slower than average adult reading speed, to leave room for slang-heavy lines), not measured against an actual human reading these specific lines - worth a sanity check from the user on whether banners/dialogue boxes feel right at the new, longer hold times, since this is exactly the kind of thing that can only really be judged by reading it live.
  - `DialogueCategory` (InnerThought/Mission/Shop/Faction/StoryReveal) is authored but not yet given distinct presentation (no italics for inner thoughts, no different box styling per category) - the foundation carries the category, but no UI reads it yet to change how a line displays. That's a reasonable next increment, not done here since nothing currently needs it.
  - No EXE build made - not requested for this task, and the roadmap brief for MINI-053 didn't ask for one either.
- Next action: user talks to the villager NPC in Play Mode (ideally once after raising heat via police proximity, and again after building GrandBayGangs reputation through a few illegal sales) to see the new conditional lines actually appear, and confirms the mission objective card's new mission-name line and the longer banner/dialogue hold times read well in practice. `MINI-054` (dialect lexicon + opening conversation) is the natural next task on the roadmap.

### MINI-054 — Dialogue/dialect bible and opening conversation

- Date: 2026-08-17
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User's `Docs/MASTER-DEVELOPMENT-BRIEF.md` roadmap: "Expand `Docs/DIALOGUE-REFERENCE.md` or create `Docs/DIALECT-LEXICON.md`" with a specific source-of-truth term list (Zeb, volehing, Awtic, fresh, wah is di word, what is di word diah, Not Ah Word, Domnicah, diah, awa, awa wii, Gwa Bay, Gwada, go up, Zion, scrub, fadah, gasah), used naturally and sparingly; "Rasta uses a Jamaican speech style. Most other local characters use Dominican/Gwa Bay speech based on the user's supplied dialogue." Plus an opening story: "the two boys were kicked out of school; they are hungry and struggling; they need money; one suggests Zion/Zeb; the other is hesitant; they initially consider normal crops; story begins naturally through their conversation."
- Implementation:
  - **`Docs/DIALECT-LEXICON.md` (new).** Catalogues all 15 supplied terms with an honest per-term confidence rating - **Confirmed** (already load-bearing in the game, e.g. `Zeb`), **Inferred (high/moderate confidence)** (a reasoned meaning with a stated basis - e.g. `volehing`/`voleh` ≈ "steal," inferred directly from the brief's own MINI-059 sentence "unattended Zeb can be volehed," resembling French *voler*), or **Unconfirmed** (`Awtic`, `gasah`, `go up`, `scrub`, `fresh` - no usage example or context clue available anywhere supplied, so no definition is guessed; flagged for the user to confirm). Explicitly states the rule this pass followed: **no Unconfirmed term was used in any written dialogue this task** - a real Creole language shouldn't get an invented definition just to fill a word list. Also records a Jamaican-Patois style note for `MINI-056`'s future Rasta mentor (not written yet - reserved for when that NPC actually exists, so it can be checked against real dialogue rather than authored in a vacuum).
  - **`Docs/DIALOGUE-REFERENCE.md`**: added a short pointer to the new lexicon and recorded the opening conversation's existence in "Currently used in-game," rather than duplicating content across both files.
  - **Opening conversation.** Built as a genuine scripted scene, not just prose in a doc: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Dialogue/OpeningConversationController.cs` (new) plays an ordered list of `DialogueLine`s (reusing the MINI-053 dialogue foundation's data shape purely for speaker+text - this is a one-time linear scene, not conditional selection, so `DialogueLine.conditions` stays empty here) on the same `MissionSystem.Alert`/`MissionHUD` banner every other briefing already uses, each line held for the same reading-time formula MINI-053 established (3.2s floor, +0.045s/char, 9s ceiling).
  - **8-line script** (`Mini011PhaseBSetup.BuildOpeningConversation`), hitting every beat from the brief in order: Franki - kicked out of school; Sacat - hungry, no food tonight; Franki suggests Zion/Zeb explicitly by name; Sacat is hesitant ("Nah, mn. Not yet... police, trouble, worse"); Franki pushes back ("wah we go do then... sit down and starve?"); Sacat proposes normal crops by name (tomato/banana/carrot - matching what M1 then actually has them plant); Franki concedes but plants a seed for later ("if di crop money slow... I going back to dat Zeb talk" - sets up Boss K's eventual offer without contradicting `Docs/STORY.md`'s existing character framing, where Strong/Franki is "initially more willing to take fast-money opportunities" and Smart/Sacat "initially wants to build a legitimate farming business"); Sacat closes the scene, sending them toward Montine. Only `Zeb`/`Zion` from the new list are used, plus the already-verified `mn`/`nuh`/`wii` register from `Docs/DIALOGUE-REFERENCE.md` - no Unconfirmed term appears.
  - **Sequencing without an execution-order dependency.** `MissionSystem.cs` gained one field, `firstBriefingDelay` - when > 0, `Start()` defers M1's own briefing banner via the *existing* `_pendingBriefing`/`_pendingBriefingAt` mechanism (already used for post-completion briefings) instead of showing it immediately, so the opening conversation's banner isn't immediately overwritten by M1's. `Mini011PhaseBSetup.BuildOpeningConversation` sets this to `OpeningConversationController.TotalDuration` (computed from the same reading-time formula, so the numbers actually line up) right after building the conversation object - a single computed value at scene-build time, not a runtime dependency between the two components' `Awake`/`Start` order (which Unity does not guarantee between separate MonoBehaviours in the same frame).
  - `Mini054OpeningConversationValidation.cs` (new, editor-only): proves `TotalDuration` matches a hand-computed sum of the exact same formula for known text (catches drift if either side is ever edited without the other), and proves `MissionSystem.Start()` genuinely takes the deferred path when `firstBriefingDelay` is set and the immediate path when it isn't - the part that can't be confirmed just by reading the diff, since it depends on which branch a boolean check takes.
- Files changed: `Docs/DIALECT-LEXICON.md` (new), `Docs/DIALOGUE-REFERENCE.md`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Dialogue/OpeningConversationController.cs` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Missions/MissionSystem.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini054OpeningConversationValidation.cs` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
