### MINI-055 — Boss consolidation

- Date: 2026-08-17
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User's `Docs/MASTER-DEVELOPMENT-BRIEF.md` roadmap: "Move toward two major bosses: Boss J (lower-level boss, Bushers, street connections, substantial chain, useful at first, later suspicious story behavior); Boss C (bigger boss, more status, Strong, higher-value progression, larger/multiple chains, nice black SUVs nearby). Migrate useful progression responsibilities from old placeholder bosses without destroying working systems."
- Implementation:
  - **Consolidated three physical NPCs into one.** Previously Black Sugar (BossM), Purple (BossP), and Blue Cheese (BossQ) were each a separate NPC at a separate road position. `TownNPCInteractable.cs` gained `multiBossCrops[]`/`multiBossSeedPrices[]` (purely additive - the original single-crop `bossSeedCrop` path is untouched and still used by Boss J and kept as a fallback for any future single-strain boss). `HandleMultiStrainBoss()` offers the first currently-unlocked strain the player doesn't already hold seed for, in array order, at that strain's own price; falls back to "bring back what you growing" once every unlocked strain is already seeded, or "not ready yet" if none are unlocked. `Mini011PhaseBSetup.BuildBossC` replaces the three old `BuildStrainBoss` calls with one NPC (`NPC_BossC`) offering all three, at Boss P's old road position. The two mission objectives that used to target "BossM"/"BossP" separately (M10W, M11W) now both target the single "BossC" targetId/marker position.
  - **Boss K renamed to Boss J - display-only, deliberately.** `Faction.BossK`/`BossKReputation`/`RecordBossJob`/the "BossK" `npcName`/mission `targetId` strings are all **unchanged** - 6+ mission objectives across M5/M8/M9L/M9W/M10W already key off that exact string, and `HUDController`'s reputation line reads the same field. Renaming the internal identifier would have meant touching every one of those for zero player-visible benefit and real regression risk on mission-matching logic nobody has hands-on tested yet. Instead, every string the *player actually sees* - the `[ E ] Talk to Boss K` prompt (hardcoded literal, unrelated to npcName), mission briefings/instructions, and the "Boss K: Money tight..." sell-decline line - now say "Boss J". This is the pragmatic reading of "migrate... without destroying working systems": migrate the presentation, not the working plumbing underneath it.
  - **Visual flourishes** (the brief explicitly listed "substantial chain" for Boss J and "larger/multiple chains"/"nice black SUVs nearby" for Boss C, so these weren't left unaddressed): `ApplyBossChains(instance, chainCount)` builds a beaded gold necklace (a drooping semicircular arc of small spheres, chainCount=1 for Boss J, 2 stacked strands for Boss C) anchored to the neck bone. `BuildBlackSuvProps` places two simple SUV-silhouette placeholders (body + narrower "greenhouse" cabin + four visible wheels, dark body/near-black glass tint) near Boss C - explicitly not a real vehicle (no driving/physics, that's `MINI-033`/`MINI-034`), just parked set-dressing.
  - **Two real bugs found and fixed via the project's own "render and inspect, don't assume" policy, not shipped blind:** (1) the necklace's first two attempts (anchored to the neck bone using the bone's own local axes, then anchored to the chest bone the same way) both rendered wrong - a flat gold disc floating off the shoulder, then a bead string sunk near the hip - because this rig's bone rest orientations aren't simply world-aligned. Fixed by anchoring the necklace's *rotation* to the character root's own rotation (a known, sane frame) while still parenting to the neck bone for position-follow, and pushing the beads far enough forward to actually clear the collar mesh instead of self-clipping into it. (2) The SUVs' first pass (plain box + small wheels) rendered as an unmarked dark crate, not a car - fixed by adding a narrower cabin/"greenhouse" box (the actual shape cue that reads as a vehicle) and correcting the wheel cylinder's scale math (worked out by hand: post-rotation local scale of `(2R, T/2, 2R)` gives an actual visible radius `R` and thickness `T` - a first uniform-scale attempt produced wheels ~4x too thin and half-buried in the body). Also caught, while verifying: the snapshot camera itself was looking at the character's *back* twice in a row (once from an arbitrary offset, once from a sign error on `-forward` instead of `+forward`) before a render finally confirmed the front view.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Interaction/TownNPCInteractable.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/UI/HUDController.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011AssetSnapshot.cs` (two new snapshot menu items), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini055BossConsolidationValidation.cs` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Scene/prefab changes: `GrandBayProof.unity` rebuilt - `NPC_BossM`/`NPC_BossP`/`NPC_BossQ` are gone, replaced by one `NPC_BossC`; `NPC_BossK` renamed to `NPC_BossJ` (GameObject name only, cosmetic); both boss NPCs now carry chain accessories; two SUV placeholder objects sit near Boss C.
- Verification commands: `Logs/MINI-055-Compile1..11.log`, `Logs/MINI-055-BuildScene1..5.log`, `Logs/MINI-055-Validation3.log` (the new harness, final passing run), `Logs/MINI-055-SceneValidation3.log` (existing regression check), `Logs/Snapshots/mini055-boss-c-closeup.png` + `mini055-boss-c-wide.png` (final visual proof, after the fixes above).
- Verification results: compile clean (0 `error CS`) on the final pass. Scene builder logged `MINI-011 Phase B: GrandBayProof rebuilt and saved`, no exceptions, across every rebuild. `MINI-055 BOSS CONSOLIDATION VALIDATION PASS`: six real scenarios confirmed with live `EconomyManager`/`ProgressionManager` state - nothing offered before any unlock, Black Sugar offered and correctly charges/grants exactly on unlock, falls back to "bring back" once seeded with nothing else unlocked, Purple offered next (not Blue Cheese - array order respected) once both cross their thresholds, Blue Cheese offered after Purple is seeded, and "bring back" again once all three are seeded. `Mini001SceneValidation` still **PASS**. Visual proof: the final closeup render shows a clearly-readable two-strand gold chain necklace sitting on the chest (not floating off the shoulder or sunk near the hip, as the first two attempts did); the final wide render shows two SUV-silhouetted placeholders with visible cabins and wheels (not plain crates, as the first attempt did) parked beside Boss C.
- Known issues (honest):
  - Not hands-on playtested - the consolidated Boss C's dialogue flow (offer -> buy -> "bring back" -> next offer) is proven correct by the validation harness against real progression state, but nobody has walked up to him in a running game and pressed E through that sequence.
  - The chain/SUV visual flourishes are simple hand-built placeholder geometry in the project's existing established low-poly style (matching the modular houses/crop buds), not sourced assets - reasonable for a "no vehicle system exists yet" placeholder, but worth revisiting once `MINI-033`/`MINI-034` build real vehicles.
  - `Docs/STORY.md`'s original Mission 1-5 narrative text still says "Boss K" throughout - not updated in this pass (it's a story/narrative reference document, not in-game text, and wasn't in this task's reserved files); worth a pass if the user wants the story doc to match the in-game "Boss J" naming.
  - The Boss K/Faction.BossK/BossKReputation internal-vs-display naming split (documented above) is a real, intentional inconsistency for anyone reading the code cold - flagged plainly rather than hidden, with the reasoning for why a full rename wasn't done this pass.
- Next action: user talks to Boss J and Boss C in Play Mode/a build to confirm the renamed dialogue and the consolidated multi-strain flow feel right, and looks at both NPCs' chains/the parked SUVs in real lighting (the renders above are static frames from batch mode, not a live look). `MINI-056` (Rasta mentor and strain progression) is next on the roadmap.

### MINI-055 follow-up — SUV off the road, chain raised to the chest

- Date: 2026-08-17
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User caught two real problems from actually looking at the renders: one of the two SUVs sat in the middle of the road, and the necklace beads sat on the stomach rather than the chest.
- Root causes (found, not guessed):
  - **SUV on the road:** `BuildBlackSuvProps` computed each car's position as `bossPos - right * side * distance`. `bossPos` is Boss C's own position, which is *already* `roadPoints[index] + right * side * 6.5` (his own stand-off from the road centreline). Subtracting that same `right * side` term back out cancelled the offset to zero, landing the car back on the road centreline itself. Also switched the placement's along-road offset from world-space `Vector3.forward` to the road's actual tangent (`dir`), since the road isn't straight - the original offset direction didn't reliably clear the road either.
  - **Chain on the stomach:** the render used to verify the previous pass was a static frame at a flattering angle; at real gameplay scale the `-up * (0.10 + c*0.07)` drop (plus the bead arc's own radius/sag) put the whole necklace well below the collarbone.
- Implementation: dropped from two SUVs to one (per the user's "remove the suv" - the brief's "SUVs" plural was for flavour, not a hard requirement), repositioned it further off the road along `right` in the same direction Boss C is already offset, and along the road's real tangent `dir` instead of world-forward. Raised the necklace's anchor point (now slightly above the neck bone for strand 0, only slightly below for strand 1) and shrank the bead-arc radius/sag so the strands sit on the upper chest/collar instead of drooping to the stomach.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`.
- Verification: compile clean, scene builder clean (`Logs/MINI-055b-*.log`), `MINI-055 BOSS CONSOLIDATION VALIDATION PASS` (unaffected by this purely-visual change, re-run to confirm no regression), `Mini001SceneValidation` **PASS**. Re-rendered both snapshots after the fix and inspected them directly: one SUV, clearly off the paved road on the grass; the necklace sits visibly on the upper chest/collar in both close-up frames.
- Next action: none outstanding for this specific follow-up: moving on to `MINI-056` per the user's "then move on to the next after."

### MINI-056 — Rasta mentor and strain progression

- Date: 2026-08-17
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User's `Docs/MASTER-DEVELOPMENT-BRIEF.md` roadmap: "Older Rasta mentor teaches advanced strain work after missions. Progression: Bushers from Boss J; Strong from Boss C; advanced strains learned through story/training; Black Sugar; Purple; Blue Cheese; later hybrids through the existing breeding system." Asked the user to disambiguate "Strong" (same ambiguous word as MINI-055's brief) before building - confirmed it's descriptive, not a new strain, so this task is scoped to the mentor NPC only.
- Implementation:
  - `NPC_RastaMentor` (`Mini011PhaseBSetup.BuildRastaMentor`) stands near the farm centre, facing the plots - an older mentor watching over the plantation. Reuses the plain `Villager` role and MINI-053's `dialogueSet` path rather than adding a new `NpcRole` or mechanic - "teaches advanced strain work" is delivered entirely as dialogue, gated on the exact same `CropUnlocked` progression thresholds that already gate Boss C's seed offers, so it can't contradict or duplicate the existing unlock model. Facial hair is deliberately left on (not hidden, unlike Sacat/Franki) as a simple no-new-geometry way to read as older on the same character pack every other NPC uses.
  - Wrote real Jamaican Patois dialogue (`Assets/UpIzUpMini/Data/Dialogue/RastaMentorLines.asset`) - `I an' I`, `Iyah`, `bredrin`, `seen`, `nuh true?`, `Zion`, dropped-`th` `di`/`dis`/`dat` - deliberately none of the Dominican/Gwa Bay register's own markers (`mn`/`nuh`/`wii`/`allu`), so the two read as different people rather than the same slang repainted. This is the first time this project has actually written the Rasta style `Docs/DIALECT-LEXICON.md` documented but deliberately left unwritten until a real character existed to check it against.
  - Four dialogue tiers, using `DialogueSet`'s specificity tie-break deliberately: each deeper tier's line carries ALL of the shallower tiers' conditions too (not just its own), so a player who has unlocked Blue Cheese (which requires Black Sugar and Purple to already be true anyway) always gets the deepest congratulation rather than the selector picking arbitrarily among several simultaneously-true lines. Category `StoryReveal` (documented but unused since MINI-053) is used for the deepest (Blue Cheese) line - reaching the top strain is the actual narrative milestone.
  - `Mini056RastaMentorValidation.cs` (new, editor-only): opens the real built scene (not a duplicate in-memory setup) and drives the actual `NPC_RastaMentor`/`EconomyManager`/`ProgressionManager` scene objects through all four tiers via `RecordBossJob`/`AddReputation`, checking the mentor's live feedback text at each step.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011AssetSnapshot.cs` (new snapshot menu item), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini056RastaMentorValidation.cs` (new), `Docs/DIALECT-LEXICON.md`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Scene/prefab changes: `GrandBayProof.unity` rebuilt with a new `NPC_RastaMentor` object and its `RastaMentorLines` DialogueSet asset.
- Verification commands: `Logs/MINI-056-Compile1..4.log`, `Logs/MINI-056-BuildScene1.log`, `Logs/MINI-056-Validation2.log` (the harness, final passing run), `Logs/MINI-056-SceneValidation.log` (existing regression check), `Logs/Snapshots/mini056-rasta-mentor.png`.
- Verification results: compile clean. Scene builder logged `MINI-011 Phase B: GrandBayProof rebuilt and saved`, no exceptions, no missing-NPC warning. `MINI-056 RASTA MENTOR VALIDATION PASS`: confirmed against the real scene's NPC/managers - starts on a fallback line, correctly switches to Black Sugar's line, then to Purple's (not Black Sugar's, once both are true - the deeper tier wins), then to Blue Cheese's once all three are unlocked. One real bug caught and fixed in the harness itself first: the first attempt's `RecordBossJob` calls' own `GrandBayGangs` reputation side effects (each call adds +5) weren't accounted for, so Blue Cheese's threshold got crossed a step early - fixed by recomputing the exact reputation math instead of guessing round numbers. `Mini001SceneValidation` still **PASS**. Visual check: the mentor renders correctly positioned overlooking the farm plots.
- Known issues (honest):
  - Not hands-on playtested - nobody has walked up to him in a running game and watched the tiered lines actually change as real missions/sales are done.
  - "Teaches advanced strain work" is delivered as dialogue flavour only - the mentor does not grant seeds, discounts, or any mechanical bonus, so it cannot undercut or duplicate Boss C's paid economy. If the user wants a real mechanical teaching effect (e.g., a free first seed, a price discount, or an actual unlock the mentor grants rather than just narrates), that's a deliberately separate follow-up, not assumed here.
  - He doesn't visually read as strongly "older" as intended in the rendered check - facial hair is technically left on, but this particular character-pack body/texture doesn't show it prominently at normal render distance. Not worth further iteration for a background NPC, but flagged rather than silently accepted as "clearly older."
  - `Docs/DIALECT-LEXICON.md`'s several **Unconfirmed** terms (`Awtic`, `gasah`, `go up`, `scrub`, `fresh`) still weren't used - the mentor's lines stick to confirmed/high-confidence vocabulary only, consistent with every earlier dialogue-writing pass this session.
- Next action: user talks to the mentor in Play Mode/a build across a real playthrough (ideally checking in after each strain unlock) to confirm the tiered lines actually appear at the right moments and that the Jamaican-Patois voice reads as genuinely distinct from every Dominican-speaking NPC. `MINI-057` (Normy, the crooked police NPC) is next on the roadmap.

### MINI-057 — Normy

- Date: 2026-08-17
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User's `Docs/MASTER-DEVELOPMENT-BRIEF.md` roadmap: "Create crooked police NPC Normy. Normy is not representative of all police. Support: side missions; information; relationship/reputation; self-interested assistance."
- Implementation:
  - **Self-interested assistance + relationship/reputation**: added `NpcRole.Normy` and a new `Faction.Normy` (`ProgressionManager` - deliberately its own faction, separate from `Faction.Police`, so bribing him doesn't itself move real Police standing). `TownNPCInteractable.HandleNormy()` lets the player pay him to reduce heat by 35, rate-limited (45s cooldown, mirroring `FarmPlot`'s existing clone-cooldown pattern) so it can't be spammed into a free safety net. Cost starts at $80 and falls as `NormyReputation` grows from repeat business (floored at $30) - a real, working "relationship" mechanic, not just a number that's tracked and never used.
  - **Information**: when there's no heat to bribe him over (the common case), he falls back to a MINI-053 `dialogueSet` instead - two unconditional flavour lines, plus two conditional ones (heat ≥ 50, GrandBayGangs reputation ≥ 15) giving "insider" tips. The reputation-gated line deliberately creates vague suspicion ("somebody eyeing up crops dat doe belong to dem, round Montine way") **without naming any faction or group** - Dog Life doesn't exist in the game yet (`MINI-058`+), and the roadmap's own later instruction for the theft mystery is "do not reveal Dog Life immediately... the story should first create suspicion," so this plants that seed now rather than jumping ahead of unbuilt content.
  - **Not representative of all police**: `NpcRole.Normy` is deliberately NOT `NpcRole.Police` - he never runs through `PoliceOfficer`/`PatrolNPC`'s patrol/chase/heat-detection logic, doesn't move, and can't attack or arrest. Visually he wears the same uniform tint real officers do (`ApplyPoliceUniform`, reused as-is) so he still reads as a cop at a glance, distinct only in behaviour.
  - **Side missions**: deliberately NOT added to `MissionSystem`'s existing linear mission queue - that system advances one mission at a time in strict sequence, and forcing an optional side interaction into it would block/delay the main story chain. The bribe mechanic itself already functions as a repeatable "self-interested assistance" side-interaction without needing a separate mission-tracking system; a dedicated branching side-mission (a real multi-step optional quest) is flagged as a reasonable follow-up, not done here, since it would need `MissionSystem` extended to support parallel/optional tracks - a real architecture change outside this task's reserved files.
  - **Found and fixed a real pre-existing bug along the way, not shipped blind**: the first rendered check of Normy showed a black sphere floating on his chest, not a cap on his head. Rendering an ACTUAL existing `NPC_Police` officer with the same camera confirmed the bug already affected every officer in the game, not just Normy - `ApplyPoliceUniform`'s cap was parented directly to the head bone using the bone's own local axes (`SetParent(head, false)`), the exact same root cause as MINI-055's necklace saga (this rig's bone rest orientations aren't world-aligned). Fixed with the same technique: anchor rotation to the character root, position by world-space offset from the bone. Took two iterations to actually land on top of the head instead of over the eyes like a visor - confirmed by re-rendering both a real officer and Normy.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Interaction/TownNPCInteractable.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Progression/ProgressionManager.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/UI/HUDController.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs` (includes the `ApplyPoliceUniform` cap fix), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011AssetSnapshot.cs` (two new snapshot menu items, one kept as a standing regression check), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini057NormyValidation.cs` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Scene/prefab changes: `GrandBayProof.unity` rebuilt with a new `NPC_Normy` object and its `NormyLines` DialogueSet asset; every officer's cap (including Normy's) now sits correctly on the head.
- Verification commands: `Logs/MINI-057-Compile1..8.log`, `Logs/MINI-057-BuildScene1..4.log`, `Logs/MINI-057-Validation2.log` (the harness, final passing run), `Logs/MINI-057-SceneValidation2.log` (existing regression check), `Logs/Snapshots/mini057-normy.png` + `mini057-real-police-closeup.png` (final visual proof, after the cap fix).
- Verification results: compile clean. Scene builder logged `MINI-011 Phase B: GrandBayProof rebuilt and saved`, no exceptions, across every rebuild. `MINI-057 NORMY VALIDATION PASS` on the first run of the harness itself (no bribe/heat/reputation math bugs this time): no charge with no heat, a real bribe costs exactly $80/reduces heat by exactly 35/grants exactly +5 reputation, the cooldown genuinely blocks a second charge, and a second bribe after reputation grows is genuinely cheaper ($75). `Mini001SceneValidation` still **PASS**. Visual proof: both a real officer and Normy render with the cap correctly on top of the head after the fix (two prior attempts are documented above, not hidden).
- Known issues (honest):
  - Not hands-on playtested - the bribe/cooldown/relationship-pricing math is proven correct by the validation harness against real progression state, but nobody has walked up to him in a running game and pressed E through the sequence.
  - "Side missions" is only partially addressed - the repeatable bribe is a genuine self-interested-assistance side-interaction, but a real branching/multi-step side quest was deliberately not built, since it would need `MissionSystem` extended for optional/parallel tracks (a bigger architecture change than this task's scope). Flagged as a follow-up, not silently dropped.
  - The `ApplyPoliceUniform` cap fix is a genuine improvement to already-shipped content (MINI-012), found and fixed opportunistically while building an unrelated task - both real officers' caps looked broken before this pass in a way nobody had apparently rendered a close check on. Worth a quick look in a real build to confirm it holds up outside the static render angles used here.

### MINI-058 — Factions

- Date: 2026-08-17
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User's `Docs/MASTER-DEVELOPMENT-BRIEF.md` roadmap: "Player gang: Not Ah Word. Rival gang: Dog Life. Dog Life controls Lalay initially. Support up to roughly ten Dog Life members in major block encounters, but use pooling/activation/distance logic instead of leaving all NPCs fully active across the map. Player gang can eventually recruit up to four members. Assignments: follow; guard plantation; stay at block/home; unavailable/injured; later errands."
- Scoping note (stated plainly, not hidden): "later errands" is deferred by the brief's own wording - not built. "Up to roughly ten Dog Life members" is read as the *pooling architecture* needing to scale that far, not necessarily ten authored characters on day one - four are built and pooled this pass; adding more later is just more entries in the same pool, not a new system.
- Implementation:
  - **Not Ah Word (player gang), recruitment**: `GangMemberController` (new, `Scripts/Character/`) - deliberately forked from `FollowController` rather than sharing a base class with it, since a gang member has four states (Follow/GuardPlantation/StayAtHome/Unavailable) where the companion boy only ever needed one; steering reuses the same MINI-052 `NavPathSteerer`. `GangMemberInteractable` (new) lets the player cycle a recruited member's assignment face-to-face (Follow → Guard Plantation → Stay at Home → Follow), refusing while Unavailable. `TownNPCInteractable` gained `NpcRole.GangRecruiter` + `HandleRecruit()`: a pooled roster of 4 (`Mini011PhaseBSetup.BuildNotAhWordRoster`), pre-built inactive, each with a name (Chevy/Reds/Ju/Skeng), a `GangMemberController`, and **real combat** (`NpcCombatHealth` + `HumanoidAnimationManager`, MINI-038) - "Unavailable/injured" is something that can actually happen from being knocked down in a fight, not a manually-picked menu state pretending to be one, and the member auto-returns to Follow once they recover. Recruiting costs $150 and simply activates the next free pool slot, so "up to four" is enforced by the pool's own fixed size, not a counter that could drift.
  - **Dog Life (rival gang)**: `RivalGangSpawner` (new) - a pool of 4 territorial NPCs near the road's start ("Dog Life controls Lalay initially"), activated/deactivated by player distance with hysteresis (enter at 26m, exit at 40m - the exit distance is deliberately larger so a member doesn't flicker in and out right at the boundary), modelled directly on `PoliceReinforcementSpawner`'s proven pooling pattern (MINI-041) with distance substituted for heat as the trigger. Visually tinted red (`ApplyGangTint`, the same shirt/pants/shoes recolour-by-material-name technique as `ApplyPoliceUniform`, deliberately without that function's head-bone cap logic - no need to risk that bug again for a prop Dog Life doesn't need). Dialogue is deliberately vague/territorial only ("Dis block belong to us, nuh") - **no faction is named and no theft plot is revealed**, per the roadmap's own later instruction (`MINI-059`) to "not reveal Dog Life immediately... the story should first create suspicion" - important since this task is literally where Dog Life is introduced, so restraint here matters. They carry real combat components too (punchable, reacting/staggering) but no attack AI of their own this pass - ambient presence, not yet an active threat.
  - **Found and fixed a real, genuinely serious bug via the validation harness, not shipped blind**: `RivalGangSpawner`'s pool was a plain `private List<GameObject>` with no `[SerializeField]`. It populated correctly in-memory during the same editor session that ran `BuildScene`, so nothing looked wrong until the validation harness opened the *saved* scene fresh (a separate process, exactly like a real game boot) and found the pool completely empty - Dog Life would never have appeared in an actual build, only ever in the same session that built the scene. Fixed by adding `[SerializeField]`; confirmed fixed by rebuilding and re-validating against the freshly-saved scene.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Character/GangMemberController.cs` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Interaction/GangMemberInteractable.cs` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Interaction/RivalGangSpawner.cs` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Interaction/TownNPCInteractable.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011AssetSnapshot.cs` (two new snapshot menu items), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini058FactionsValidation.cs` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Scene/prefab changes: `GrandBayProof.unity` rebuilt with a new `NotAhWordRoster` (4 inactive pooled recruits), `NPC_GangRecruiter`, and a `DogLifeSpawner` with 4 pooled, initially-inactive `NPC_DogLife_N` objects.
- Verification commands: `Logs/MINI-058-Compile1..6.log`, `Logs/MINI-058-BuildScene1..2.log`, `Logs/MINI-058-Validation3.log` (the harness, final passing run, after the serialization fix), `Logs/MINI-058-SceneValidation2.log` (existing regression check), `Logs/Snapshots/mini058-dog-life.png` + `mini058-gang-member.png`.
- Verification results: compile clean on every pass. Scene builder logged `MINI-011 Phase B: GrandBayProof rebuilt and saved`, no exceptions, across both rebuilds. `MINI-058 FACTIONS VALIDATION PASS` (after the pool-serialization fix): exactly 4 recruits succeed and a 5th is refused with the correct line, each recruit costs exactly $150, a new recruit starts on Follow and cycles Follow→Guard→Home→Follow correctly (wrapping back to Follow), and the Dog Life pool is genuinely inactive at 200m and genuinely active at 5m from the player - real distance-driven activation, not assumed. `Mini001SceneValidation` still **PASS**. Visual proof: Dog Life's red tint reads clearly near the Lalay street sign; a recruited member (Chevy) renders standing beside the player after a real recruit transaction.
- Known issues (honest):
  - Not hands-on playtested - nobody has walked up to the recruiter, actually recruited someone, and watched them follow/guard/wait in a running game, or walked into Lalay and watched Dog Life pop in/out at the real distance thresholds.
  - Dog Life has no attack AI and doesn't initiate anything - ambient/territorial only this pass, matching MINI-059's own "don't reveal them immediately" framing, but also a genuine scope limit: they can be punched (real `NpcCombatHealth`) but won't punch back or chase. Worth revisiting once `MINI-059`/`MINI-060`'s actual antagonism plays out.
  - "Guard plantation" positions a member at the farm centre and "stay at home" at the road's start - reasonable defaults, but neither is yet *read* by anything else (e.g. `MINI-059`'s planned "assigned guard reduces/prevents risk" theft mechanic doesn't exist yet to check a member's assignment). The position is real and correct; the payoff for choosing it isn't wired up yet, since that's explicitly a later task's job.
  - Only 4 of the brief's "roughly ten" Dog Life members are authored - the pooling architecture is real and scales (add more `GameObject`s to the same pool), but ten distinct characters weren't built this pass; stated as a deliberate scope choice, not an oversight.
  - "Later errands" (the fifth listed assignment) is untouched, exactly as the brief itself defers it.
- Next action: user recruits a member, cycles their assignment, and walks into/out of Lalay to confirm Dog Life's pooled activation feels right at real distances (not just the two distances the harness checked). `MINI-059` (plantation guarding/theft mystery) is next on the roadmap - it can now actually check a Not Ah Word member's GuardPlantation assignment, which this task made real.

### MINI-056/058 follow-up — Rasta position, Dog Life sync, Chevy respect-recruit

- Date: 2026-08-17
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User reported three concrete problems from actually looking at/playing the build: (1) the Rasta mentor was standing on the farm itself, mixed in with the plots - move him lower down the farm road; (2) the four red Dog Life members were visibly moving in lockstep ("move left together stop and move right together and stop") - make it random instead; (3) the green Not Ah Word member (Chevy) was positioned mixed in with Dog Life and recruited with money - move him up by Boss C instead, and gate his recruitment on reputation ("respect"), not money.
- Root causes (found, not guessed):
  - **Rasta position**: `BuildRastaMentor` placed him at `_farmCenter + (-4.5, 0, 5.5)` - inside the plantation clearing itself, among the plots.
  - **Dog Life sync**: every one of the four members got the exact same waypoint offset (`pos + (2, 0, 0)`, identical world-space direction) and the same default `PatrolNPC` walk speed and pause duration. With no variation anywhere, and all four starting their patrol loop on the same frame (when the pool activates), they were mathematically guaranteed to move and pause in perfect unison - not a subtle bug, an inevitable one given the inputs.
  - **Chevy**: `BuildNotAhWordRoster`'s loop treated all four roster members identically - same pooled/inactive/paid-recruit path, same general area near the road start (right next to `DogLifeSpawner`'s own block centre, also at road index 1) - which is exactly why he read as "mixed with the other gang."
- Implementation:
  - `BuildRastaMentor` now recomputes the farm access track's own `turnoff`/direction (the same formula `BuildFarmPathAndClearing` uses) and places him at `Lerp(turnoff, farmCenter, 0.4f)` offset to the side of the path - partway down the farm road, watching the plantation from a distance instead of standing in it.
  - Each Dog Life member now gets its own randomised wander angle (0-360°) and radius (1.6-3.2m) for its second waypoint instead of a shared `(2,0,0)` offset, plus randomised `walkSpeed` (1.5-2.3) and `pauseAtWaypointSeconds` (1.0-3.2s) via the existing seeded `Random(seed, min, max)` helper (already used elsewhere in this file, e.g. MINI-051's cola clusters) - different directions and different timing means they can no longer stay in sync by construction.
  - Chevy is no longer part of the paid recruiter's pool (down to 3: Reds/Ju/Skeng). `GangMemberInteractable` gained `recruitViaReputation`/`recruitReputationThreshold` fields: when set, the member is visible in the world from the start (not pooled/inactive) and talking to them directly checks `GrandBayGangs` reputation ("respect") instead of money - below threshold (20) refuses with a "you nuh have di respect yet" line and charges nothing; at or above, recruits for free. Chevy is now positioned further out on Boss C's own side of the road (index 13, same as Boss C, but at 10.5 units off-centre vs. Boss C's own 6.5, so they don't overlap) - "by Boss C" and no longer anywhere near Dog Life's block.
  - `Mini058FactionsValidation.cs` updated to match: paid-pool cap is now 3 (was 4), assignment-cycling checked on Reds instead of Chevy, and a new check proves money alone cannot recruit Chevy (refused, no charge) while 20 GrandBayGangs reputation recruits him for free.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Interaction/GangMemberInteractable.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini058FactionsValidation.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011AssetSnapshot.cs` (one new snapshot menu item), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt). `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Interaction/PatrolNPC.cs` was reserved but ended up untouched - the randomisation was done entirely from the editor script via `SerializedObject`, no game-code change needed there.
- Scene/prefab changes: `GrandBayProof.unity` rebuilt - `NPC_RastaMentor` moved to the farm road; each `NPC_DogLife_N` has its own randomised waypoint/speed/pause; `NotAhWord_Chevy` moved to Boss C's area and is active from scene start (not pooled).
- Verification commands: `Logs/MINI-058b-Compile1..3.log`, `Logs/MINI-058b-BuildScene1.log`, `Logs/MINI-058b-Validation.log` (the updated harness, passing first run), `Logs/MINI-058b-SceneValidation.log` (existing regression check), `Logs/Snapshots/mini056-rasta-mentor.png` (re-rendered - now shows him on the dirt path with the village road/houses visible below, not among the plots), `Logs/Snapshots/mini058-chevy-by-bossc.png` (Chevy in his green shirt standing near Boss C's parked SUV, Boss C visible walking nearby).
- Verification results: compile clean. Scene builder logged `MINI-011 Phase B: GrandBayProof rebuilt and saved`, no exceptions. `MINI-058 FACTIONS VALIDATION PASS` on the first run of the updated harness: exactly 3 paid recruits succeed and a 4th is refused, Reds cycles Follow→Guard→Home→Follow correctly, money alone genuinely fails to recruit Chevy (confirmed both the refusal message and that no money was charged), 20 reputation genuinely recruits him for free (confirmed both the success message and, again, no money charged). `Mini001SceneValidation` still **PASS**. Visual proof for the two position fixes confirmed by direct inspection of the re-rendered frames.
- Known issues (honest):
  - The Dog Life desync fix cannot be visually proven by a static render (the bug and the fix are both about behaviour *over time*) - it's proven by construction (four genuinely different random seeds feeding into position/speed/pause, verified by reading the applied values are in fact different, not just that the code compiles) rather than a rendered frame. A real Play Mode look is the only way to see it move correctly.
  - Chevy's new reputation threshold (20 GrandBayGangs) is a first-pass number, not tuned against how fast reputation actually accrues in real play - worth the user's judgement on whether it feels too easy/hard once they've played enough to reach it normally.
  - Not hands-on playtested - same caveat as the original MINI-058 entry, now doubly true for the changed pieces.
- Next action: user watches Dog Life patrol for real (confirm they no longer move in lockstep), looks at the Rasta mentor's new spot, and reaches 20 GrandBayGangs reputation to confirm Chevy's respect-recruit feels right. Continuing to `MINI-059` next per the user's "continue to the next" instruction.

### MINI-059 — Plantation guarding/theft mystery

- Date: 2026-08-17
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User's `Docs/MASTER-DEVELOPMENT-BRIEF.md` roadmap: "At low reputation, unattended Zeb can be volehed. Rules: risk begins after player has been away; not guaranteed every time; reputation affects risk; assigned guard reduces/prevents risk. Do not reveal Dog Life immediately. The story should first create suspicion."
- Implementation:
  - `FarmPlot.cs` gained `HasStealableZeb` (true only for an illegal crop actually Growing or Ripe - legal crops, empty plots, and dry-soil-not-yet-growing plots are never targets) and `Voleh()` (a full loss - resets the plot to Empty via the existing `ResetPlot()`, returns the stolen crop's display name for the notification, or null if nothing was stealable). Both additive; every existing FarmPlot behaviour (plant/water/harvest/clone) is untouched.
  - `PlantationTheftController.cs` (new) implements all four brief rules literally: an away-timer only starts accruing once the player is genuinely outside `awayRadius` (40m) of the plantation and resets to zero the moment they're back ("risk begins after player has been away," not immediately); once away long enough (20s), a periodic roll (every 30s) at a computed chance (2-35%, "not guaranteed every time"); the chance itself scales with `GrandBayGangs` reputation via `ComputeChance()` (lower reputation -> higher risk, but never zero from reputation alone - "not guaranteed" cuts both ways); and a Not Ah Word member actually on `GuardPlantation` duty (MINI-058) short-circuits the whole roll to zero ("assigned guard reduces/prevents risk" - implemented as full prevention, the simplest reading that gives the mechanic real teeth).
  - `GangMemberController` gained a static `All` registry (`OnEnable`/`OnDisable`, same idiom as `InteractableBase.All`) so `PlantationTheftController` can ask "is anyone guarding right now" without a per-frame scene scan.
  - **No faction is named anywhere in this controller's own text** - the theft notification says "Somebody volehed your {crop}," never "Dog Life." This is deliberate, not an oversight: the brief's own next task (`MINI-060`) is the actual reveal, and this task's instruction is explicitly to "create suspicion" first, not identify the culprit - even though Dog Life NPCs already exist in the world (`MINI-058`), this system doesn't reference them at all.
  - `Mini059PlantationTheftValidation.cs` (new, editor-only): proves the steal-target rules (legal crops and non-growing plots are never stealable, a ripe illegal crop is, `Voleh()` correctly empties the plot and reports the right name), that reputation genuinely lowers the *computed* chance (a pure function, checked exactly rather than statistically), and - the part that has to be RNG-independent to test reliably - that a guarded plantation survives an `Update()` pass with the away/roll timers forced open, regardless of the dice.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Farming/FarmPlot.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Farming/PlantationTheftController.cs` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Character/GangMemberController.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini059PlantationTheftValidation.cs` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Scene/prefab changes: `GrandBayProof.unity` rebuilt with a new `PlantationTheftController` object wired to every real `FarmPlot` (main grid + expansion plots) and the farm's own centre point.
- Verification commands: `Logs/MINI-059-Compile1..6.log`, `Logs/MINI-059-BuildScene1..2.log`, `Logs/MINI-059-Validation4.log` (the harness, final passing run, after two real test-side bugs were found and fixed - see below), `Logs/MINI-059-SceneValidation2.log` (existing regression check).
- Verification results: compile clean on the final pass (one real game-code bug along the way - `ComputeChance` was accidentally marked `static` while reading an instance field, a genuine compile error, not just a style nit). Scene builder logged `MINI-011 Phase B: GrandBayProof rebuilt and saved`, no exceptions. `MINI-059 PLANTATION THEFT VALIDATION PASS` on the final run: all three scenario groups confirmed. `Mini001SceneValidation` still **PASS**.
- Known issues (honest, and unusually test-heavy this pass - two real bugs surfaced entirely in the validation harness, not the shipped game code, but worth recording plainly):
  - The reputation-comparison test's first run failed because `ProgressionManager.AddReputation` takes a **delta**, not an absolute value - going from a clamped -100 and adding +100 only reaches 0, not 100, so both "low" and "high" reputation samples were accidentally identical. Fixed by using +200 to correctly reach the ceiling from the floor. This was a test-construction mistake, not a bug in `ComputeChance` itself.
  - The guard-blocks-theft test's first run failed to find the just-recruited member via `GangMemberController.All` at all (`Count=0`) - confirmed (again, same root cause as several earlier tasks this session) that neither `AddComponent` nor a runtime `SetActive(true)` reliably fires `Awake`/`OnEnable` outside Play Mode in this batch-mode environment. Worked around by finding the object via `GameObject.Find` (which does work, since it really is active) and manually inserting it into the static registry so the test genuinely exercises `IsGuarded()`'s real code path rather than trivially passing against an empty list. This is a test-environment limitation, not a real-game bug - `SetActive`-triggered `OnEnable` is standard, reliable Unity behaviour in actual Play Mode/builds.
  - Not hands-on playtested - the away-timer/probability/guard logic is proven correct in isolation against real game state, but nobody has actually left ripe Bushers unattended in a running game and watched (or not watched) it get stolen over real elapsed time.
  - The theft chance numbers (2-35%, 20s away, 30s roll interval) are first-pass values, not tuned against how long a real session leaves crops unattended - worth the user's judgement once played.
  - "Not guaranteed every time" is satisfied by both the probability roll AND the reputation floor (chance is clamped to a 2% minimum even at 100 reputation) - a deliberate reading, not silently making high reputation a hard immunity, since the guard is meant to be the only sure protection.
- Next action: user plants Bushers (or another illegal strain), walks far from the plantation for a couple of minutes without a guard assigned, and confirms a theft notification eventually appears; then assigns a Not Ah Word member to Guard Plantation and confirms it stops happening. `MINI-060` (the Gardey Zafeh reveal) is next on the roadmap - it can now build on a real, already-firing theft mechanic instead of an imagined one.

### MINI-060 — Gardey Zafeh reveal

- Date: 2026-08-17
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User's `Docs/MASTER-DEVELOPMENT-BRIEF.md` roadmap named "Gardey Zafeh" without defining it - asked the user directly rather than guess (same approach as the "Strong" ambiguity in MINI-055/056). Full answer: "the gardeh zafeh is a charater in guadeloupe that will let you know the rival dog life members are stealing your crops when you are absent. you will pay $1000 euro which is three time the normal ec price. after you can still contact the boat man to go back to the gardeh for protection and give you a temporary energy boost fighting skills. also after the mission the gardeh can also tell you, you will be rich but be careful with jalousie people. make her future readings random. so one time can be energy boost (stay at 100) for 10 minutes, next time can be health boost stays and 100 for 10 minutes. next time police would not trouble you for 10 minutes, another time anything you do will give you double money."
- Implementation:
  - `NPC_GardeyZafeh` stands at the jetty, opposite the Boat Man - reached the same way the existing Guadeloupe trade run already is (D-007: no separate Guadeloupe map exists, so she's represented at the abstracted point of contact rather than invented a location for her).
  - **The reveal** (`TownNPCInteractable.HandleGardeyZafeh`): first visit costs exactly $1000 (declines cleanly if short, same "no charge on decline" pattern as every other paid interaction in this project); on success, sets `ProgressionManager.DogLifeRevealed` and tells the player Dog Life is stealing their Zeb. This flag is read by two other systems, not just this NPC's own future dialogue: `PlantationTheftController`'s theft notification names Dog Life from then on instead of "Somebody" (MINI-059's own text was written anticipating exactly this reveal), and Dog Life's own ambient dialogue (MINI-058) gains a new, more openly hostile line gated on the same flag via a new `DialogueConditionType.DogLifeRevealed` - "only after the reveal should Dog Life rivalry become openly active," read here as a tone shift since no attack AI exists for them yet (a real scope limit, not hidden - see Known issues).
  - **Random readings**: `GardeyZafehBuffState` (new, `Scripts/Economy/`) - a plain static holder (not a MonoBehaviour) tracking one active buff at a time, same idiom MINI-049's cheat code already established with `CharacterVitals.GlobalInvincible`/`GlobalUnlimitedStamina`. Every visit after the reveal grants one of the four buffs the user specified, chosen at random, for exactly 10 minutes (600s); a second visit while one is still running is refused with a countdown line rather than stacking or replacing it early. Each buff hooks into the real system it claims to affect, not a cosmetic flag: `EnergyBoost` pins `CharacterVitals.Stamina` at max (alongside the existing `GlobalUnlimitedStamina` check); `HealthBoost` pins `Health` at max AND makes `Damage()` a genuine no-op (proven by a 9999-damage hit doing nothing while it's active, not just "the flag is true" - the same standard MINI-049's own cheat-code validation held itself to); `PoliceImmunity` blocks `PoliceOfficer`'s `wantsChase` from ever going true; `DoubleMoney` doubles a positive `AddMoney` amount but explicitly leaves negative amounts (costs/fees) untouched, checked directly rather than assumed.
  - The "rich but watch jalousie people" line is included on every reading, not just narrative colour once - `jalousie` (jealousy) is the one Guadeloupean Kreyol word used here, and it's a term the user gave me directly for this exact line, not a guess the way MINI-054's Unconfirmed lexicon terms would be.
  - `Mini060GardeyZafehValidation.cs` (new, editor-only): proves the reveal is genuinely refused without $1000 (no charge, no reveal), costs exactly $1000 and names Dog Life once paid, every reading after that is free, only one reading is ever active (a second visit while one runs is refused and the active buff is unchanged), and - forcing each buff directly via the public `Grant` API rather than trusting the random draw - that `HealthBoost` actually blocks a 9999-damage hit and `DoubleMoney` doubles a gain while leaving a cost alone.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Economy/GardeyZafehBuffState.cs` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Character/CharacterVitals.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Interaction/PoliceOfficer.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Economy/EconomyManager.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Progression/ProgressionManager.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Dialogue/DialogueCondition.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Interaction/TownNPCInteractable.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Farming/PlantationTheftController.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011AssetSnapshot.cs` (one new snapshot menu item), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini060GardeyZafehValidation.cs` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Scene/prefab changes: `GrandBayProof.unity` rebuilt with a new `NPC_GardeyZafeh` at the jetty.
- Verification commands: `Logs/MINI-060-Compile1..4.log`, `Logs/MINI-060-BuildScene1.log`, `Logs/MINI-060-Validation1.log` (the harness, passing first run), `Logs/MINI-060-SceneValidation2.log` (existing regression check), `Logs/Snapshots/mini060-gardey-zafeh.png`. **User explicitly requested a Windows build this pass** (not the routine default) - `Logs/MINI-060-WindowsBuild.log` (succeeded, 165,773,056 bytes / ~158MB, ~47s) and `Logs/MINI-060-PlayerRun.log` (12s headless run of the actual compiled `Builds/GrandBayProof/UpIzUpMini.exe`, zero console errors).
- Verification results: compile clean on every pass. Scene builder logged `MINI-011 Phase B: GrandBayProof rebuilt and saved`, no exceptions, no missing-jetty warning. `MINI-060 GARDEY ZAFEH VALIDATION PASS` on the first run - no bugs this time (unlike MINI-053/055/059's harnesses). `Mini001SceneValidation` still **PASS**. Windows build succeeded and ran clean headlessly.
- Known issues (honest):
  - **She's visually a man.** The Floreswa character pack this entire project draws from was checked, not assumed, before picking her model - it only ships `male01/02/03_1/2/3` (9 variants total), no female body. Every NPC in this project already reuses these same 9 models regardless of who they represent, so this follows existing precedent rather than inventing an asset that doesn't exist - but "she"/"her" in the writing and dialogue against a male-presenting model is a real, visible mismatch, not something to gloss over. If the user wants her to actually look like a woman, that needs a sourced or generated female model (Hi3D, or a purchased/free asset) - flagged here as the concrete next step per `MASTER-DEVELOPMENT-BRIEF.md`'s own asset-acquisition workflow, not solved in this pass.

If "openly active" was meant to include Dog Life actually attacking the player, that's real new work, not done here.

- Next action: user plays the built EXE - pays Gardey Zafeh $1000 to trigger the reveal, confirms the plantation theft notification and Dog Life's own dialogue both start naming them, and tries a few readings to feel out the four buffs. `Docs/MASTER-DEVELOPMENT-BRIEF.md`'s listed roadmap (MINI-051 through MINI-060) is now fully built through this task.

### MINI-060 follow-up — Boat Man consolidation + explicit E/R choice

- Date: 2026-08-17
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User, after the first EXE build: "you could just have the original man on the jetty have the dialogue for the gardey zafeh, and going to gwada to sell crops instead of having two on the jetty. his job is just to provide transportation to gaudeloupe and he will disappear until the time is up so 500s." Then, mid-implementation, refined further: "the boat man goes away on both instances for 500s and just make a dialogue to choose which option you want if is to sell up gwada or gardey zafeh" - an explicit player choice between the two services, not the game silently picking one.
- Implementation:
  - **Removed the separate `NPC_GardeyZafeh`** - `Mini011PhaseBSetup.BuildGardeyZafeh` and its call deleted. `NpcRole.GardeyZafeh` removed from the enum. One NPC on the jetty again (`NPC_BoatMan`), confirmed by render.
  - **Explicit E/R choice, not an automatic priority chain.** A first pass tried to have one `E` press auto-decide (reveal, then trade, then a reading as a fallback when there was nothing to sell) - the user's follow-up message rejected that in favour of a real choice. Reworked to the exact dual-prompt pattern `FarmPlot`'s `[ E ] Harvest` / `[ R ] Clone for seed` already established: `E` (`HandleBoatMan`) is the produce trade, completely unchanged - `GuadeloupeTrade.cs` itself was never touched. `R` is Gardey Zafeh - `TownNPCInteractable.InteractGardeyZafeh()` (public, mirroring `FarmPlot.Clone()`) runs the same reveal/reading logic as the old separate NPC, and `GardeyZafehLabel` (mirroring `FarmPlot.CloneLabel`) supplies the second prompt line. `InteractionDetector` gained an `R`-key branch for `TownNPCInteractable` alongside its existing `FarmPlot` one, and a second stacked prompt box in `OnGUI` (identical layout code to the existing clone-label box) - so a player standing at the jetty sees both `[ E ] Guadeloupe Run` and `[ R ] Ask about Gardey Zafeh ($1000)` / `[ R ] Ask Gardey Zafeh for a reading` at once and picks.
  - **"He will disappear... on both instances, so 500s."** `DisappearForTrip()` (a coroutine on `TownNPCInteractable`) is started from both `HandleBoatMan` (only when a trade trip genuinely just started - checked via `GuadeloupeTrade.TripActive` before/after the call, not merely "E was pressed") and `InteractGardeyZafeh` (on a successful reveal or reading). It waits 4 seconds first (so `InteractionDetector`'s feedback box - which clears itself the instant an in-range interactable goes missing - doesn't hide the line the player just triggered before they can read it), then `SetActive(false)`s the NPC for `boatManAwaySeconds - 4` more seconds before reactivating. While inactive he deregisters from `InteractableBase.All` automatically (the same mechanism every pooled NPC in this project already uses), so he's genuinely uninteractable, not just declining politely.
  - `Mini060GardeyZafehValidation.cs` rewritten to target `NPC_BoatMan`/`InteractGardeyZafeh()` directly (the R path) instead of a separate NPC, plus a new check confirming `NPC_GardeyZafeh` no longer exists at all. `Mini011AssetSnapshot.SnapshotGardeyZafeh` retargeted to `NPC_BoatMan` rather than deleted, keeping the menu path stable.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Interaction/TownNPCInteractable.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Interaction/InteractionDetector.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011AssetSnapshot.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini060GardeyZafehValidation.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt), `Builds/GrandBayProof/UpIzUpMini.exe` (rebuilt, gitignored).
- Scene/prefab changes: `GrandBayProof.unity` rebuilt - `NPC_GardeyZafeh` is gone; `NPC_BoatMan` now carries both the trade and Gardey Zafeh logic.
- Verification commands: `Logs/MINI-060c-Compile1..2.log`, `Logs/MINI-060c-BuildScene1.log`, `Logs/MINI-060c-Validation1.log` (the rewritten harness, passing first run), `Logs/MINI-060c-SceneValidation.log` (existing regression check), `Logs/Snapshots/mini060-boatman.png`. **Second Windows build this session**, per the user's original build request continuing to apply - `Logs/MINI-060c-WindowsBuild.log` and a fresh headless run log.
- Verification results: compile clean. Scene builder logged `MINI-011 Phase B: GrandBayProof rebuilt and saved`, no exceptions. `MINI-060 GARDEY ZAFEH VALIDATION PASS` (rewritten harness) - confirms `NPC_GardeyZafeh` is truly gone, `R` is refused without $1000, costs exactly that and names Dog Life once paid, every reading after is free via `R`, the cooldown genuinely refuses a second reading, and `HealthBoost`/`DoubleMoney` still do what they claim. `Mini001SceneValidation` still **PASS**.
- Known issues (honest):

The logic that DECIDES when to start it (a trade trip genuinely starting, a reveal succeeding, a reading being granted) is fully tested; the actual 500-second disappearance/reappearance timing is not, and needs a real Play Mode/build look.

Not a bug, just two independently-timed systems that don't perfectly line up - flagged rather than silently accepted.
