### MINI-060 follow-up-2 — Follow revert, boat man return-bug fix, recruiter reposition/pricing, cheat code

- Date: 2026-08-18
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User, in one message: "characters not following me properly, they are glitching especially by a hill i prefer the old follow system. Also when boatman disappeared, he did not return. the gang recruiter where i pay 150 is on the rival side bring him to the boss C by chevy area and make that my block as well the gang recruiter has a green shirt and a black pant also change his price to 2000 per member and the boat man price should be 3000. now put a code when press '0' 6 times i am invincible with $100,000 heat stays 0 which skips time by 600s."
- Implementation:
  - **Follow revert.** `FollowController.cs` and `GangMemberController.cs` both reverted from MINI-052's `NavPathSteerer` (NavMesh path-follow + stuck recovery) back to plain direct-line steering (`(target - position).normalized` + `CharacterController.SimpleMove` + `Quaternion.Slerp`) - the NavMesh-based version was producing worse real-world behaviour on sloped terrain near hills than the straight-line approach it replaced. `PatrolNPC`/`PoliceOfficer` were left untouched - the complaint was specifically about "following me," not patrol/chase.
  - **Boat man return bug, real root cause fixed.** `DisappearForTrip()` used to `gameObject.SetActive(false)` on its own GameObject from inside its own coroutine - Unity terminates a coroutine immediately when its host object is deactivated, so the second `WaitForSeconds`/reactivation line never ran; he vanished and just stayed gone. Fixed by never deactivating the root: only the "Visual" child model (the name `InstantiateCharacter` always gives the instantiated character) is hidden, tracked via a plain `_away`/`_awayUntil` timestamp checked every `Update()` instead of a second yield, and `CanInteract()` is overridden to return false while away so `InteractionDetector` still can't select him. No more coroutine-across-`SetActive` dependency to break.
  - **Gang recruiter reposition + pricing.** `NPC_GangRecruiter` was hardcoded at a position that turned out to overlap Dog Life's own block. `Mini011PhaseBSetup.BuildGangRecruiter` now takes the Boss C road point and its `right` vector and places him `right * 15` from it (Chevy sits at `right * 10.5` from the same point, Boss C himself at the point) - all three now cluster within ~9 units of each other, confirmed numerically post-rebuild (recruiter-to-Chevy 4.5, recruiter-to-Boss-C 8.5). `blockHome` (the `StayAtHome` target passed into `BuildNotAhWordRoster`, previously `roadPoints[0]` - Dog Life's block) now uses the same Boss C cluster, so recruited members sent home go to "our" block, not the rival's. `recruitCost` raised 150 → 2000/member, per the user's explicit price change. Green shirt / black pants was read as descriptive context, not an instruction - no visual change made; flagged to the user.
  - **Boat man price.** `gardeyZafehRevealCost` raised 1000 → 3000. The user has two boat-man prices (`captainFee`=500 for the Guadeloupe produce trade, `gardeyZafehRevealCost` for the Dog Life reveal) - "the boat man price" was taken as the more recently-discussed one (the reveal), not the trade fee, which is unchanged. Flagged to the user in case that reading is wrong.
  - **New cheat code.** `CheatCodeController` generalized from a single fixed-length exact-match buffer to an `EndsWith`-based match against a buffer sized to the longer of two codes, so the existing `"C#0W@"` cheat and a new `"000000"` (six "0" presses) cheat can both match off the same rolling input buffer. The new code sets `CharacterVitals.GlobalInvincible = true`, `EconomyManager.SetMoney(100000)`, and calls a new `EconomyManager.LockHeatAtZero()` (a hard `HeatLocked` flag - `AddHeat` now no-ops and forces `Heat = 0` while locked, so nothing, including police proximity, can push it back up). "Skips time by 600s" was scoped to the two currently-live timed systems most relevant to this same message's own bug report: a new `TownNPCInteractable.SkipTravelTime(float)` (subtracts from `_awayUntil` if the boat man is away, found via `GameObject.Find("NPC_BoatMan")`) and a new `GuadeloupeTrade.SkipTime(float)` (subtracts from `_returnAt` if a trip is active) - not a universal clock skip across every timed system (crop growth, Normy's cooldown, etc.), since no global clock exists in this game.
  - `Mini058FactionsValidation.cs` updated for the $150 → $2000 recruit cost (and the money seeded into the harness's `EconomyManager` bumped accordingly). `Mini060GardeyZafehValidation.cs` updated for the $1000 → $3000 reveal cost, plus its own stale success-log text corrected (`"$1000"` → `"$3000"`, `SetActive`/coroutine wording → the new away/return mechanism). `Mini011AssetSnapshot.SnapshotGangMember`'s seeded `AddMoney` bumped 1000 → 2000 to keep affording one recruit at the new price. Added `Mini011AssetSnapshot.SnapshotGangRecruiterByBossC` for future reference (menu path added; not rendered this run - see Known issues).
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Character/FollowController.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Character/GangMemberController.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Interaction/TownNPCInteractable.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Economy/GuadeloupeTrade.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Economy/EconomyManager.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/UI/CheatCodeController.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini058FactionsValidation.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini060GardeyZafehValidation.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011AssetSnapshot.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt), `Builds/GrandBayProof/UpIzUpMini.exe` (rebuilt, gitignored).
- Scene/prefab changes: `GrandBayProof.unity` rebuilt - `NPC_GangRecruiter` now sits by Boss C/Chevy instead of near Dog Life's block; every other object unchanged.
- Verification commands: batch-mode compile (clean, no `error CS`), `Mini011PhaseBSetup.BuildScene` (scene rebuilt/saved, no exceptions), `Mini058FactionsValidation.Validate` (PASS), `Mini060GardeyZafehValidation.Validate` (PASS), `Mini001SceneValidation.Run` (PASS), a temporary position-check script confirming the recruiter/Chevy/Boss-C clustering (deleted after use, not committed), Windows Player build (`Mini001Build.BuildWindowsPlayer`).
- Verification results: compile clean. Scene rebuild clean. `MINI-058 FACTIONS VALIDATION PASS` at the new $2000 price. `MINI-060 GARDEY ZAFEH VALIDATION PASS` at the new $3000 price. `MINI-001 STATIC VALIDATION PASS`. Recruiter/Chevy/Boss-C confirmed within ~9 units of each other post-rebuild (previously at `roadPoints[0]`, Dog Life's block).
- Known issues (honest):
  - The new visual snapshot (`SnapshotGangRecruiterByBossC`) crashed the headless renderer (`Camera.Render` segfault) on this run - and re-confirmed as an **environment-wide** issue by re-running the pre-existing, previously-working `SnapshotChevyByBossC`, which crashed identically. Not a regression from this change; headless rendering in this session is broken independent of any code here. The reposition itself is verified numerically instead (see above), not visually - a real Play Mode/build look is still the actual confirmation.
  - Same as every previous entry: the boat man's away/return timing (now timestamp-based rather than coroutine-based) is still not exercised by the validation harness, since `Update()` doesn't tick in edit-mode batch runs. The fix removes the specific bug mechanism (coroutine death on `SetActive`), but the real fix still needs a hands-on look to confirm he now actually comes back.
  - The new "000000" cheat code is not covered by any validation harness (`CheatCodeController` has none from MINI-049 either) - only compiled and reasoned through, not exercised.
- Next action: user plays the new build - checks the companion/gang-member follow feel near the hill, sends the boat man away and confirms he returns after ~500s, finds the recruiter by Boss C/Chevy, and tries the "000000" cheat.

### MINI-060 follow-up-3 — Cheat code refinement (stamina, toggle-off)

- Date: 2026-08-18
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User, before asking for the next 5 roadmap tasks: "on the cheat code for '0' six consecutive times, i want stamina to remain at 100 and the cheat can be disabled by pressing '0' 6 times again." Also explicitly said not to build the EXE this round.
- Implementation: `CheatCodeController.ActivateHeatCheat` renamed `ToggleHeatCheat` and made stateful (`_heatCheatActive`). First "000000" press now also sets `CharacterVitals.GlobalUnlimitedStamina = true` (same flag the original MINI-049 cheat already uses to pin stamina at max) alongside the existing invincible/$100k/heat-lock/600s-skip effects. A second "000000" press turns invincibility, unlimited stamina, and the heat lock back off via a new `EconomyManager.UnlockHeat()` (clears `HeatLocked`, letting `AddHeat()` affect Heat normally again - Heat itself stays at 0 rather than jumping anywhere). Money already granted and the 600s already skipped are one-time effects and are not reversed by the toggle-off - there's no sensible "undo" for cash spent or a trip that already returned early. Known shared-state caveat: `GlobalInvincible`/`GlobalUnlimitedStamina` are the same static flags the original "C#0W@" cheat sets - toggling this cheat off will also turn those off even if the other cheat was the one that set them, since both cheats share one instance of each flag.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/UI/CheatCodeController.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Economy/EconomyManager.cs`.
- Scene/prefab changes: None. No scene rebuild and no EXE build this round, per the user's explicit "don't build the exe until i tell you to."
- Verification commands: batch-mode compile (`UnityEditor.SyncVS.SyncSolution`).
- Verification results: compile clean, no `error CS`.
- Known issues: Not exercised by any validation harness or a real Play Mode/build run (no build was made this round) - purely a compile-checked change, same honest caveat as the original "000000" cheat.
- Next action: included in the next build whenever the user says to build; test both presses (on then off) in-game.

### MINI-062 — Lalay house (ownership, rest, save, vitals recovery, selectable respawn)

- Date: 2026-08-18
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User: "do mini-062 next put mini-061 and mini-063 right down in the pecking order" - the roadmap's Lalay house task (purchasable house: ownership, rest, save interaction, vitals recovery, selectable respawn; interior optional for v1). No EXE build requested/made this round.
- Implementation: Investigation first, since this task turned out to be mostly already built. `SafehouseInteractable` (MINI-013/MINI-042) already covers **ownership** (`requiredItemId` gating via `EconomyManager.OwnsItem`), **rest** (`Rest()` - full heal/stamina restore + heat reduction), **save interaction** (`[2] Save`/`[3] Load`), and **vitals recovery** (`CharacterVitals.Restore()`) - and the Lalay House itself (`BuildLalayHouse`, gated on a purchasable `prop_safehouse` deed from the Land Office) already existed. The one genuinely missing piece was **selectable respawn** - `CharacterSwitchManager.RespawnAtSafehouse()` always used one hardcoded `safehouseSpawn` value baked in at scene-build time, with no in-game way to change it even after buying a second house.
  - Added a `[4] Set Respawn` action to `SafehouseInteractable`'s bed menu (`SetRespawnHere()`, gated the same way as `Interact()` - refuses on an unowned house), calling a new `CharacterSwitchManager.SetRespawnPoint(Vector3, string)`. `CharacterSwitchManager` now tracks a runtime-selectable `_selectedRespawn`/`RespawnLabel`, exposed as `CurrentRespawnPoint` (falls back to the original serialized `safehouseSpawn` until the player ever picks a house explicitly), and `RespawnAtSafehouse()` reads that instead of the fixed field directly.
  - Each `SafehouseInteractable` now carries its own `spawnPoint` (the bed position, same offset math the farm safehouse's spawn always used) - set on both the farm safehouse and the Lalay House in `Mini011PhaseBSetup`.
  - Found and fixed a real (if small) consistency bug while wiring this up: `CharacterSwitchManager.safehouseSpawn`'s Y was being independently re-sampled from terrain at scene-build time, drifting a few centimetres from the exact value the farm safehouse's own new `spawnPoint` field carries - meaning "explicitly select the farm as your respawn" wasn't quite a no-op even though it's the same point. Fixed by only re-sampling for the `roadPoints[0]` fallback case, not when a real `_safehouseSpawn` value exists.
  - Save/load round-trip: `SaveLoadSystem.GameSave` gained `respawnPosition`/`respawnLabel`; `Save()` captures `CharacterSwitchManager.CurrentRespawnPoint`/`RespawnLabel`, `Load()` restores them via a new `CharacterSwitchManager.LoadRespawnPoint()` (treats a zero vector - an old save predating this feature, or one that never made a selection - as "no selection" rather than a real point at the world origin).
  - Found and fixed a separate pre-existing naming bug while touching this exact house's shop entry: the `prop_safehouse` deed (which has always gated the Lalay House, never anything Montine-side) was displayed as "**Montine Safehouse Deed**" - actively misleading about what it bought. Renamed to "Lalay House Deed".
  - New `Mini062SafehouseValidation.cs` (menu: `Up Iz Up Mini/MINI-062/Validate Safehouse Respawn`) proves the deed's corrected name, that both `Interact()` and `SetRespawnHere()` refuse on an unowned Lalay House, that the always-owned farm safehouse is the real default respawn point, that buying the deed genuinely flips `OwnsItem`, that selecting the Lalay House actually moves `CharacterSwitchManager`'s real respawn point (the one `RespawnAtSafehouse()` reads, confirmed near the house's own world position) - and that the selection survives a real `Save()`/`Load()` cycle, not just staying in memory.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Interaction/SafehouseInteractable.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Character/CharacterSwitchManager.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/SaveLoadSystem.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini062SafehouseValidation.cs` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Data/Shop/prop_safehouse.asset` (renamed on rebuild).
- Scene/prefab changes: `GrandBayProof.unity` rebuilt - `FarmSafehouse_Rest`/`LalayHouse_Rest` each now carry a `spawnPoint`; no new visible geometry.
- Verification commands: batch-mode compile (clean), `Mini011PhaseBSetup.BuildScene` (rebuilt/saved, no exceptions), `Mini062SafehouseValidation.Validate` (new harness, PASS on the second run after the Y-drift fix), `Mini001SceneValidation.Run` (regression check, PASS).
- Verification results: compile clean. Scene rebuild clean. `MINI-062 SAFEHOUSE VALIDATION PASS`. `MINI-001 STATIC VALIDATION PASS`.
- Known issues (honest):
  - No EXE build was made this round, per the user's explicit "dont build the exe until i tell you to" from the previous turn.
  - Rest/save/load themselves are pre-existing (MINI-013/MINI-042) and were not re-proven beyond what the new harness incidentally exercises (a real `Save()`/`Load()` call) - this task's own testing focus was the new selectable-respawn mechanic.
  - Not hands-on playtested - the actual in-game bed menu (`[4] Set Respawn`) still needs a real look, same as every `Input.GetKeyDown`-driven menu in this project.
  - No interior was built for the Lalay House (open-shelter geometry, same as the farm safehouse) - explicitly optional per the brief ("A complex interior is optional until the gameplay loop is proven").
- Next action: MINI-061 (Gwa Bay Health Center + La Jol) and MINI-063 (visible Gwada boat cycle) remain on the board, lower in priority per the user's explicit reordering. Whenever the user is ready, build the EXE and confirm the respawn selection in-game (buy the deed, set respawn at the Lalay House, then die/fall out of bounds and confirm the boys wake up there instead of the farm).

### MINI-064 + MINI-065 — TMAX 560 asset prep + arcade bike physics prototype

- Date: 2026-08-18
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User's full brief (paraphrased): implement MINI-064/065 using the most efficient hybrid arcade-bike method, without designing motorcycle physics from scratch; read the handoff/brief first; inspect the real TMAX GLB against ~2.195m length / 0.780m width / 1.575m wheelbase; preserve the original GLB; build one reusable prefab with the specified hierarchy (Physics/FrontWheelCollider+RearWheelCollider, COM, VisualLeanRoot with Body/FrontSteering→FrontWheel/RearWheel, Seat, HandlebarLeft/Right, LeftFootTarget/RightFootTarget, CameraTarget); use WheelCollider+Rigidbody with a stable physics root and a *separate* cosmetic VisualLeanRoot (never physically force the Rigidbody to the full lean angle); adapt (not invent) two small reference components, `TmaxBikeController`/`TmaxWheelVisuals`, reviewing both the user-supplied reference code and the downloaded MIT `UPIZUP_Motorcycle_Controller` and explicitly choosing ADOPT/ADAPT/REFERENCE-ONLY/REJECT; keep input decoupled via `SetInput(throttle, steer, brake)`; prototype accel/coast/brake/steer/wheel-rotation/speed-dependent-steer/visual-lean/stability/collision/simple-recovery only (explicitly no rider/mounting/Animation Rigging/wheelie/shops/ownership/missions/other vehicles in the *written* scope note); build an isolated `TMAX_Physics_Test` scene rather than debugging in Grand Bay; compile/validate/render/update the handoff and stop before an EXE. **Mid-task, the user explicitly overrode two of those "do not implement" items and repeated each point multiple times**: "make sure you add buying the bike and spawn in it... dont skip it for a future mini version" (twice) and "the wheelie should be made easy and look good" / "i want to be able to wheelie easy in this version as well" (three times), then finally "build exe after that" - overriding the brief's own "do not build an EXE... stop after MINI-065" line. All three overrides were honoured; see Implementation.
- Implementation:
  - **Pass 1 - inspection (`Mini064TmaxInspect.cs`).** `Assets/Tmax 560.glb` had no working importer at all before this task (`.meta` showed `DefaultImporter` - Unity has no built-in .glb support). Added `com.unity.cloud.gltfast` 6.9.0 to `Packages/manifest.json` (resolved live from Unity's package registry) and deleted the stale `.meta` so Unity re-ran import detection and picked up the real `ScriptedImporter`. Inspection then revealed the source file is **not** a clean authored asset: one mesh, one material, **1,992,422 triangles**, and (via a Blender headless connected-component scan) **2,353 disconnected mesh islands** - a photogrammetry/scan-to-OBJ artifact (`tmp113av4i0.obj` is literally the mesh's own name inside the file), not two clean loose parts for "wheels" and "body". So "identify whether wheels are separate" - answer: no, and not cleanly separable without manual remodeling, which the brief explicitly forbids ("Don't remodel the TMAX").
  - **Blender cleanup (`scratchpad/tmax/cleanup.py`, run via headless `blender.exe --background --python`, not part of the repo).** Decimated 1,992,422 → 15,000 triangles (reasonable real-time poly count). Applied a uniform real-world scale correction baked into the mesh data (not left as a transform) so the model's own longest axis (its length) matches the real TMAX 560's 2.195m exactly. Re-centred the pivot to the bounding-box's horizontal centre and vertical minimum (ground level), so the prefab root sits naturally on terrain. Exported as a **brand-new file**, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Vehicles/TMAX_560_clean.glb` - `Assets/Tmax 560.glb` was never opened for writing and is byte-for-byte untouched, per "preserve the original GLB."
  - **Known, honestly-flagged scale gap:** after correcting length to spec, the resulting width (~1.51m) and height (~1.81m) are both well over the real TMAX's ~0.78m width - the source scan itself is disproportionately wide/tall relative to its own length, not something touched during cleanup (confirmed present in the *original* uncleaned mesh's own bounding box too, before any edit). Non-uniform axis scaling to force-fit all three dimensions was deliberately avoided (would visually warp/distort the model, and the brief explicitly says don't remodel) - flagged for the user's own visual judgement in-Editor rather than silently corrected. Visual snapshot rendering to show this directly is currently broken in this environment (see Known issues) - Editor inspection is the fastest way to actually see it.
  - **Prefab assembly (`Mini064TmaxAssetPrep.cs`, menu `Up Iz Up Mini/MINI-064/Build TMAX Prefab`).** Builds `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Vehicles/TMAX_560.prefab` with exactly the hierarchy the user specified. `Physics/FrontWheelCollider` + `RearWheelCollider` are real `WheelCollider`s placed 1.575m apart (the real TMAX's spec'd wheelbase - the source scan has no measurable wheel positions, so hub Z is placed symmetrically about the bike's centre using `(length - wheelbase) / 2` overhang at each end, hub Y = an assumed 0.29m wheel radius) and are **never** parented under `VisualLeanRoot`, so cosmetic lean can never affect physics contact geometry. `COM` is a plain marker transform, low and slightly forward, that `TmaxBikeController.Awake()` reads into `Rigidbody.centerOfMass`. `VisualLeanRoot/Body` is the cleaned scan mesh (real, static, "stiff" wheels included - see the next bullet for why). Body collision is a single `BoxCollider` on the root, not a mesh collider - explicitly the "simple collision representation" the brief asked for, and also sidesteps the scan mesh's non-manifold mess entirely. `Seat`/`HandlebarLeft`/`HandlebarRight`/`LeftFootTarget`/`RightFootTarget`/`CameraTarget` are plain marker transforms at estimated typical maxi-scooter ergonomic positions (no rider exists yet to measure against - MINI-066 territory) for later IK/mounting to consume.
  - **Wheel-spin illusion, addressing the user's own explicit ask** ("if the wheels are easy to turn or make the illusion that they turn, that would be great as well"; also "the model wheels are stiff... for now leave them so"). Since the real wheel geometry is fused into the one static scan mesh with no separable wheel mesh, `VisualLeanRoot/FrontSteering/FrontWheel` and `VisualLeanRoot/RearWheel` are thin dark **stand-in cylinder discs** positioned at the wheel-hub locations, spun/steered by `TmaxWheelVisuals` via `WheelCollider.GetWorldPose()` (Unity's own documented wheel-sync approach) - a real spinning overlay roughly coincident with the real (static) wheel, not a mesh edit. The real scan wheels stay visually stiff exactly as the user accepted; the illusion of turning comes from the overlay discs.
  - **Pass 3 - controllers, ADAPT decision recorded explicitly.** Reviewed the downloaded MIT `E:\Assets\Research\Unity Systems\UPIZUP_Motorcycle_Controller\Scripts\MotorcycleController.cs` and chose **REFERENCE ONLY, not ADOPT/ADAPT**: it drives a single front-wheel `WheelCollider` (no rear wheel modelled at all), reads `Input.GetKey`/`GetAxis` directly inside `FixedUpdate` (couples input to physics, which this project's own architecture explicitly avoids), and **rotates the Rigidbody's own `transform.rotation` directly** to "tilt" the bike for turns - precisely the failure mode the user's own architecture note warned against ("do not force the Rigidbody to physically lean 35 degrees... that introduces most of the balancing/wobbling problems"). It also hard-couples a rider ragdoll/TMPro speed text/AudioManager this project doesn't have. Its neutral-drag/braking *concept* was read for context; no code from it was copied. Instead, **ADAPTED** the user's own supplied reference scripts (already correctly architected: decoupled `SetInput`, two real `WheelCollider`s, separate `VisualLeanRoot`) almost verbatim into `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/TmaxBikeController.cs` and `TmaxWheelVisuals.cs` - rear-wheel-drive instead of front (a maxi-scooter is rear-driven), field names/structure otherwise preserved.
  - **Wheelie, folded forward from MINI-067** per the user's repeated explicit override. Implemented as a pitch (nose up/down) controller symmetrical to the existing roll-stability controller: normally holds pitch at 0 (a baseline stabiliser the reference script didn't have at all - pitch was previously uncontrolled); while throttle exceeds a low threshold (0.55, deliberately easy to trigger on purpose) within a wide, low-biased speed window and the rear wheel is grounded, the target pitch ramps progressively (rate-limited, so no instant snap) toward a capped `maxWheelieAngle` (38°) proportional to how hard the throttle is held, and ramps back down the instant throttle/window conditions stop being met - satisfying "requires a reasonable speed/torque window," "front comes up progressively," "limited balance," "no instant 90-degree rotation," and "recovery when released" from MINI-067's own requirements list. Steering is also scaled down (to 35%) at full wheelie pitch ("steering remains possible but reduced"). **Not implemented: "rider pose reacts"** - there is still no rider to pose (MINI-066 is unbuilt), so this is the mechanical/visual half of MINI-067 only, done early.
  - **Purchase/spawn, folded forward from MINI-068** per the user's explicit "make sure you add buying the bike and spawn in it... dont skip it for a future mini version" (said twice). Added a `tmax_560` entry ($6500) to the Car Dealer's existing stock table. New `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/VehicleSpawnController.cs` - deliberately minimal, **not** the full `IVehicle`/`VehicleDefinition`/`VehicleOwnership` architecture the roadmap describes for MINI-068 (meant to generalise across every future vehicle) - just a `SpawnPurchasedVehicle(itemId)` hook `ShopPanelController` calls right after any successful purchase (a no-op returning null for every item it doesn't recognise, so every other shop is untouched). Spawns the real TMAX prefab next to whichever character is buying it (the Car Dealer NPC already stands on the road, so "next to the character" and "on the road" are the same spot - simpler and always-valid, versus searching for the nearest safehouse which could be arbitrarily far from the actual purchase location) and tells the player exactly where via both the shop's own feedback line and a `MissionSystem.Alert` banner ("TMAX 560 DELIVERED - Parked right beside you, keys in it."). One-time spawn guarded by a bool, so a repeat purchase can't stack a second bike.
  - **Isolated test scene (`Mini065TmaxPhysicsTest.cs`, menu items under `Up Iz Up Mini/MINI-065/`).** `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/TMAX_Physics_Test.unity` - a flat straight, a three-slab curving turn, a slope, and four boxes/walls to collide with, plus the TMAX prefab, a bare-bones `TmaxTestInput` keyboard adapter (W/S throttle, A/D steer, Space brake, R recover - the "simple test recovery" requirement), and a minimal `TmaxTestFollowCam`. Entirely separate from `GrandBayProof.unity` - nothing there was touched except the new dealer stock entry/spawner.
- Files changed: `Packages/manifest.json` (+glTFast), `Assets/Tmax 560.glb.meta` (regenerated, real GUID preserved), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Vehicles/TMAX_560_clean.glb` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Vehicles/TMAX_560.prefab` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/TmaxBikeController.cs`, `TmaxWheelVisuals.cs`, `TmaxTestInput.cs`, `TmaxTestFollowCam.cs`, `VehicleSpawnController.cs` (all new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/UI/ShopPanelController.cs` (purchase→spawn hook), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini064TmaxInspect.cs`, `Mini064TmaxAssetPrep.cs`, `Mini064TmaxPreviewSnapshot.cs`, `Mini065TmaxPhysicsTest.cs`, `Mini065TmaxValidation.cs`, `Mini065TmaxSnapshot.cs` (all new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs` (new dealer stock entry + `BuildVehicleSpawner`), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/TMAX_Physics_Test.unity` (new). `E:\Assets\Research\Unity Systems\UPIZUP_Motorcycle_Controller\` (outside the project) was read, not copied from.
