### MINI-064/065 bugfix — bike free-falling/spinning on first Play Mode test

- Date: 2026-08-18
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User, after actually pressing Play on `TMAX_Physics_Test.unity` for the first time: "the bike looks like it is just spinning in the air like crazy lol... like its not starting off steady on the road... you should have strict visual inspection." A real, reproducible bug - the previous entry's own honest caveat ("physics behaviour is completely unverified... needs the user's real Play Mode test") turned out to hide an actual placement bug, not just an untested-but-fine system.
- Root cause: the isolated test scene spawned the bike at a **hardcoded, unmeasured** height (`Vector3(0, 0.5, -2)`) against a road slab whose real top surface - a unit cube scaled to 0.4m in Y, centred at Y=0 - sits at Y=0.2, not Y=0. Combined with an undersized `suspensionDistance` (0.12m) on both `WheelCollider`s, the wheels' maximum downward reach fell roughly 0.2-0.3m short of the actual road, so the bike simply free-fell through open space with **no wheel-ground contact at all**. `ApplyStability()` already correctly skips its roll correction while fully airborne, but `ApplyWheelie()`'s pitch correction had no such gate and kept firing every `FixedUpdate` regardless of ground contact - an unopposed torque source applied continuously to a body already in free-fall is exactly "spinning in the air like crazy."
- Implementation:
  - `Mini064TmaxAssetPrep.CreateWheelCollider`: `suspensionDistance` 0.12 → 0.25 (real forgiveness margin, also relevant once this prefab is used on `GrandBayProof`'s real terrain, not just a flat test slab), `damper` 3500 → 4000.
  - `TmaxBikeController.ApplyWheelie()`: added the same "return if both wheels ungrounded" gate `ApplyStability()` already had, plus a hard `Mathf.Clamp(correction, -60f, 60f)` safety cap on the pitch-torque magnitude regardless of cause - defense in depth against this exact failure class recurring from a different root cause later.
  - `Mini065TmaxPhysicsTest.BuildTestScene`: the bike's spawn position is now measured with a real `Physics.Raycast` straight down onto the just-built road (landing the root - whose own local origin is ground level, per the MINI-064 Blender cleanup pass - exactly on the measured surface) instead of a guessed constant. Also surfaced and fixed a second, genuinely separate bug this introduced: the very first raycast attempt returned "no hit" even though the road collider was directly beneath it, because Edit Mode doesn't run a physics step on its own - a collider created moments earlier in the same method isn't guaranteed visible to a `Physics` query without an explicit `Physics.SyncTransforms()` call first. Added that call before the raycast.
  - **New, and this is the "strict visual inspection" the user asked for**: `Mini065TmaxValidation.cs` gained a real geometric check (not just wiring/structure) - for each `WheelCollider` on the scene bike, a `Physics.Raycast` straight down measures the actual distance to the ground and confirms it's within `radius + suspensionDistance` (would have caught the exact reported bug) and greater than a small minimum (catches the opposite failure - a wheel already bottomed-out/embedded at spawn). This is the automated proxy for "look at it running" that a broken headless renderer can't currently provide - it can't replace a human's eyes, but it would have caught this specific class of bug before ever telling the user it was ready.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini064TmaxAssetPrep.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/TmaxBikeController.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini065TmaxPhysicsTest.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini065TmaxValidation.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Vehicles/TMAX_560.prefab` (rebuilt), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/TMAX_Physics_Test.unity` (rebuilt).
- Scene/prefab changes: `TMAX_560.prefab` rebuilt (new suspension values, controller re-wired). `TMAX_Physics_Test.unity` rebuilt (bike now spawns exactly on the measured road surface). `GrandBayProof.unity` untouched - the prefab's GUID/reference didn't change, only its internal contents.
- Verification commands: batch-mode compile (clean), `Mini064TmaxAssetPrep.BuildPrefab`, `Mini065TmaxPhysicsTest.WireController`, `Mini065TmaxPhysicsTest.BuildTestScene` (confirmed the raycast now actually hits, no more "raycast onto the road failed" warning), `Mini065TmaxValidation.Validate` (extended harness, PASS - including the new ground-clearance geometry check), `Mini001SceneValidation.Run` (regression, PASS).
- Verification results: compile clean. Test-scene rebuild logged a genuine raycast hit (no fallback warning). `MINI-064/065 VALIDATION PASS`, explicitly including "both WheelColliders genuinely reach the real ground at spawn with some suspension travel still in hand, not floating or bottomed out." `MINI-001 STATIC VALIDATION PASS`.
- Known issues (honest):
  - This fixes the specific reported symptom (no ground contact → unopposed torque → visible spin) with high confidence, since it's a provable geometry bug now numerically confirmed fixed. It does **not** confirm the wheelie pitch-torque *sign* is correct, the lean/steering feel is good, or the tuning values are right - those remain exactly as unverified as the previous entry said, and still need the user's actual Play Mode look. If the bike still misbehaves after this fix, it's very likely one of those separate, still-untested systems, not a repeat of this ground-contact bug.
  - Headless visual rendering is still broken in this environment (unrelated to this fix, on record since the MINI-060 follow-up-2 entry) - this fix could only be verified numerically (the new raycast-based harness check), not by looking at a rendered image.
- Next action: user re-tests `TMAX_Physics_Test.unity` in Play Mode. If it now sits steady and rolls instead of floating/spinning, move on to actual driving-feel tuning; if it's still wrong, describe exactly what's different this time (which end lifts, does it fall over standing still, etc.) since that will point at a specific remaining system rather than the ground-contact issue just fixed.

### MINI-064/065 bugfix round 2 — magenta "missing shader" wheels + still bumpy/sliding

- Date: 2026-08-18
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User, after re-testing the previous fix and sending real screenshots from their own Editor session: "the bike is better but it is still not drivable. it bumps on the road with some purple stuff on the side, its not staying on the road. dont you have a system of taking delayed screenshots to check this or even the game when you test." A direct, fair challenge - I had only been validating that things were *wired*, not that they *rendered correctly*, and said so plainly rather than guessing further blind.
- Root cause (magenta): the wheel-disc overlay's material was created via `new Material(Shader.Find("Standard"))` with **no fallback and no null-check** - every other material in this entire project goes through a helper with `Shader.Find("Standard") ?? Shader.Find("Diffuse")`, and this one skipped it. A first attempted fix (matching that fallback) still failed a newly-added automated shader check, but with a *different* symptom this time - "null material", not "null shader" - revealing a second, more fundamental problem: the material was a throwaway **in-memory** `Material` instance, never saved as a real asset. `Mini065TmaxPhysicsTest.WireController` reloads the just-built prefab via `PrefabUtility.LoadPrefabContents` and re-saves it immediately after `BuildPrefab` runs, and that reload/re-save round-trip does not reliably carry a non-asset Material reference through - the reference itself was silently getting dropped, leaving the renderer's material slot null, which Unity also renders as solid magenta ("Hidden/InternalErrorShader").
- Root cause (bumps/sliding): the suspension was under-damped for the travel range increased in the previous fix round (spring 35000/damper 4000 vs. an estimated critical damping of ~4427 for this mass), and sideways friction (1.6) was on the low side for holding a line.
- Implementation:
  - New `Mini064TmaxAssetPrep.GetWheelDiscMaterial()` - a real persisted `.mat` asset at `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Vehicles/TmaxWheelDisc.mat` (create-if-missing, load-if-exists), the exact same pattern `Mini011PhaseBSetup.GetOrCreateMaterial` already uses successfully everywhere else in this game. Replaces the old inline `new Material(...)` call entirely.
  - Suspension spring softened (35000 → 28000) and damper raised past critical (4000 → 4500); sideways friction stiffness raised (1.6 → 2.2) for better road-holding. Explicitly a best-effort estimate, not verified - see Known issues.
  - `Mini065TmaxValidation.cs` gained a real, precise **missing-shader check**: every renderer on the scene bike is scanned for a null material, a null shader, or Unity's actual internal error-shader name (`Hidden/InternalErrorShader`) - this is what actually caught the second bug (the "null material" symptom) before the user would have seen it again, and is the honest answer to "don't you have a system of checking this" - not a screenshot system (still don't have one that works - see below), but a precise automated proxy for the one specific failure class already seen twice.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini064TmaxAssetPrep.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini065TmaxValidation.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Vehicles/TMAX_560.prefab` (rebuilt), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Vehicles/TmaxWheelDisc.mat` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/TMAX_Physics_Test.unity` (rebuilt).
- Scene/prefab changes: `TMAX_560.prefab` rebuilt with a real wheel-disc material asset and softer/better-damped suspension. `TMAX_Physics_Test.unity` rebuilt against the fixed prefab.
- Verification commands: batch-mode compile (clean), `Mini064TmaxAssetPrep.BuildPrefab`, `Mini065TmaxPhysicsTest.WireController`, `Mini065TmaxPhysicsTest.BuildTestScene`, `Mini065TmaxValidation.Validate` (now including the missing-shader check - failed once with the exact "null material" diagnosis above, passed clean after the persisted-asset fix), `Mini001SceneValidation.Run` (regression, PASS).
- Verification results: compile clean. `MINI-064/065 VALIDATION PASS`, now explicitly including "no renderer on the bike has a broken/missing-shader material." `MINI-001 STATIC VALIDATION PASS`. Confirmed `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Vehicles/TmaxWheelDisc.mat` exists as a real asset file on disk this time (checked directly, not just inferred).
- Known issues (honest):
  - **Directly answering the user's question**: no, there is no working automated screenshot/Play-Mode-observation system in this environment right now. Play Mode does not execute at all in this project's batch-mode setup (a standing limitation, not new). Static edit-mode rendering (the only kind that's ever worked here) has been crashing/blank all session (on record since the MINI-060 follow-up-2 entry) - tried again for this fix, not re-attempted further to avoid repeating a known-broken path. The missing-shader check added this round is a real, precise, automated substitute for *that one specific failure class*, not a general "look at the screen" capability - it would not have caught the ground-contact bug from the previous round on its own (that needed the separate raycast-based check), and it would not catch e.g. a wrong texture, bad UVs, or an ugly-but-technically-valid material. Genuine visual review still depends on the user's own screenshots for now.
  - Suspension/friction tuning (spring/damper/sideways-stiffness) is a reasoned estimate, not verified - same caveat as the previous round, now doubly true since it changed again this round without a Play Mode test in between.
  - The wheelie pitch-torque sign is still completely unverified, as previously flagged.
- Next action: user re-tests `TMAX_Physics_Test.unity` in Play Mode. If the magenta is gone and it drives measurably steadier, report what's still off in plain terms (too bouncy, slides in turns, wheelie direction, etc.) for the next tuning pass.

### MINI-064/065 bugfix round 3 — the real root cause: the source scan was rotated, mis-scaled and tilted

- Date: 2026-08-18
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User, after a third Play Mode test with screenshots: "still not working properly" (magenta gone, but the bike still not drivable). Earlier in the same round: "it bumps on the road with some purple stuff on the side, its not staying on the road. dont you have a system of taking delayed screenshots to check this or even the game when you test."
- **Process failure that caused rounds 1-3, stated plainly**: I had been running Unity with `-nographics`, which disables the graphics device entirely - that is why every attempted render came back blank or crashed, and I wrongly concluded "visual verification is impossible in this environment" and carried on shipping changes verified only by structural/wiring checks. A prefab with its wheels in completely the wrong place passes every wiring check there is. The fix was to stop treating Unity as the only renderer: **Blender runs headless perfectly here**, renders the same asset, and diagnosed in ~10 minutes what three rounds of blind fixes had not. That capability existed the whole time and I did not reach for it.
- Root causes (all three in the source asset, none previously detected):
  1. **The scan is yaw-rotated ~39 degrees** in its own coordinate space. Every dimension I had "measured" came from an axis-aligned bounding box of a diagonal object, so all of it was wrong.
  2. **Therefore the scale was wrong** - I had scaled by 1.347 to force the inflated bbox to 2.195m; the true factor is 1.153. This is why the bike measured 1.507m wide against a real 0.780m and looked bloated.
  3. **The scan is pitched nose-down ~6.7 degrees**, so only the front tyre ever reached the ground and the rear hung ~0.18m in the air. A pitch-levelling step existed but silently did nothing: it searched a fixed 0.12m band above the model's lowest point for contacts in both halves, and the rear tyre at 0.183m was never in that band, so its `if front and rear:` guard skipped the correction without a word.
  4. Separately, the earlier pivot fix moved the *pivot* but never moved the *object*, so a -0.74m Z offset was baked into the export and the body sat sunk into the road.
- Implementation:
  - Rewrote the Blender pass (`Tools/TmaxAssetPipeline/tmax_clean_and_align.py`, now kept in the repo rather than scratch, with a README): decimate to 15k tris, yaw-align by **minimum-area-rectangle fit** (chosen over PCA, which is weighted by vertex density and skewed badly on a decimated scan), orient the nose to +Y by peak height, **level pitch using a binned underside profile** so each tyre's lowest point is found independently, scale from the *true* aligned length, and move the pivot to ground-centre *and* the geometry onto the origin.
  - Switched all mesh edits from `bpy.ops.object.transform_apply()` to direct `mesh.transform()` calls, after discovering the operator was silently using a stale object matrix in headless Blender - a claimed 31-degree yaw correction provably never applied (the re-rendered top view came back byte-identical).
  - Added **hard self-checks that raise** rather than ship: the model must be clearly longer than wide after yaw alignment, and the two tyres must sit within 3cm of the same height after levelling. Both guard exactly the silent-failure modes that caused this.
  - Rewrote `Mini064TmaxAssetPrep.MeasureBike()` so the Unity prefab **measures the real mesh** - wheel contact patches, wheel radius by circle fit, front-facing direction (auto-flipping the Body if the glTF importer's Z-flip put the nose backwards) - instead of hardcoding spec constants and hoping they matched the art. Wheel radius, both axle positions, COM height, body-collider size and every rider/IK marker are now derived from measurements.
  - Relaxed the validation's wheelbase assertion from "exactly 1.575m" to a plausible band, since demanding the spec figure from a measured value is either circular or fails a perfectly good measurement; added instead the checks that actually matter - both wheel bottoms at ground level in prefab space, and both wheels within the bike's own length.
  - Fixed a reporting bug where the build log printed `frontWasFlipped=False` even when a flip had happened, because it read the post-flip struct.
- Files changed: `Tools/TmaxAssetPipeline/` (new: pipeline + 2 diagnostics + README), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Vehicles/TMAX_560_clean.glb` (regenerated), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Vehicles/TMAX_560.prefab` (rebuilt), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini064TmaxAssetPrep.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini065TmaxValidation.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/TMAX_Physics_Test.unity` (rebuilt).
- Verification commands: Blender diagnostics (`tmax_groundprobe.py` underside profile, `tmax_axlecheck.py` axle overlay render), the pipeline's own rendered side/front/top proofs, then `Mini064TmaxAssetPrep.BuildPrefab` → `Mini065TmaxPhysicsTest.WireController` → `BuildTestScene` → `Mini065TmaxValidation.Validate` → `Mini001SceneValidation.Run`.
- Verification results: **Visually confirmed by rendered image for the first time in this task** - the bike is upright, axis-aligned, correctly proportioned, level, with both tyres on the ground plane, and rings drawn at the final computed axle positions sit concentrically on the actual wheels. Numerically: 0.822m wide x 2.195m long x 1.487m tall (real TMAX: 0.780 x 2.195 x ~1.42), front axle +0.820, rear axle -0.704, wheelbase 1.523m (spec 1.575m, within 3%), wheel radius 0.308m (real ~0.30m), both tyres level within 2mm. `MINI-064/065 VALIDATION PASS`. `MINI-001 STATIC VALIDATION PASS`.
- Known issues (honest):
  - **Driving behaviour is still unverified.** Play Mode genuinely does not run in this batch environment, so acceleration, steering feel, visual lean, the wheelie's torque direction, stability and recovery have never executed. What this round fixes is the geometry those systems operate on - which was badly wrong and made everything downstream meaningless. If the bike now sits and rolls correctly but *feels* wrong, that is the tuning pass, and it is a different problem from the last three rounds.
  - Height is ~5% over spec (1.487m vs ~1.42m) because this scan has a tall touring screen; width ~5% over because of mirrors. Both are properties of the source scan, and correcting them would mean distorting the model non-uniformly. Left as-is deliberately.
  - No EXE was built this round - see Next action.
- Next action: user tests `TMAX_Physics_Test.unity` in Play Mode (WASD/arrows, Space brake, R recover). An EXE was deliberately **not** built yet because building locks the project for several minutes and would block exactly the Editor testing that is now the fast iteration loop - say the word and it takes one command.

### MINI-064/065 round 4 — wheel-disc sync, explicit tap-to-wheelie, dealer spawn/stock fixes

- Date: 2026-08-18
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User, after testing round 3's fixes: "this is much better bike stays on the road however if you inspect the image you will see the tires you make is slightly out of alignment with the original model tires. also the bike wheelies too easily i want to press a button like Q or E to wheelie and then they would have to tap and press it to keep on wheelieing properly and then you placed the bike in the store after buying it. place it on the road. and the option should be only the bike and no other vehicles."
- Implementation:
  - **Wheel-disc sync.** The radius fit's own debugging (logged per-probe wMin/wMax/r) showed the previous single-probe measurement at h=0.10m was actually already close to correct, and my first attempt at an "improved" multi-probe fit (sampling 0.02-0.08m) made things worse - that band lands on the front tyre's own hub/disc-brake/spoke clutter and the fit collapsed to as low as 0.05m. Re-targeted the probe band to 0.09-0.12m (confirmed clean and stable via debug output, agreeing closely with an independent Blender-side re-check) and now average 4 probes there rather than trust one sample. Also added a small (8%) visual-only padding to the spinning disc overlay's scale - it does not touch the physics WheelCollider's own radius, just gives the rendered overlay a hair of extra coverage so it reads as sitting exactly on the real tyre rather than a fraction of a centimetre off. Also fixed a real, separate reporting bug found along the way: the build-log line `frontWasFlipped=` always printed `False` even when a 180-degree orientation flip genuinely happened, because it re-read the post-flip measurement struct instead of capturing the flip decision before re-measuring.
  - **Wheelie redesign - explicit button + tap-to-sustain.** Previously any full/near-full throttle triggered a wheelie automatically; per the user's repeated request this is now completely different. `TmaxBikeController` no longer looks at `throttleInput` for wheelie eligibility at all - a new public `TryWheelieTap()` (called from `TmaxTestInput` on `Input.GetKeyDown(KeyCode.E)`, matching the user's "Q or E") is the only way to start one, and holding the key does nothing extra (`GetKeyDown`, not `GetKey`). A new decaying `wheelieSustain` (0-1) value drains continuously (`wheelieSustainDecay`) and each tap adds a boost (`wheelieTapBoost`) - so a single tap gives a brief pop that fades, and holding a wheelie needs a rhythm of repeated taps, exactly "tap and press it to keep on wheelieing properly." Verified with real physics (see below): a tap roughly every 0.24s over 4s progressively lifted the nose from 0 to 33.5 degrees (capped near the 38-degree limit, never flipping) while driving at 26-43 km/h.
  - **Vehicle spawn location bug, real root cause found.** `VehicleSpawnController` used to spawn the bike relative to the *buyer's* own forward direction - but the buyer faces the dealer NPC while shopping, and `Mini011PhaseBSetup.BuildNpc` always orients every shop NPC to face the road (`Quaternion.LookRotation(-right * sideMul, ...)`), so the player's own forward points the *opposite* way, back into the shop/building behind them. That is exactly why it landed "in the store." Fixed by spawning relative to the dealer NPC's own forward direction instead (found by name, `NPC_CarDealer`), which is guaranteed to point at the road regardless of which way the player happened to be standing, plus a ground-snapping raycast for the exact height.
  - **Dealer stock restricted to just the TMAX.** Per "the option should be only the bike and no other vehicles" - removed the flavour-only `bike_scrambler`/`van_pickup`/`boat_pirogue` entries (pure economy placeholders with no working model, from the future-extensibility note in MINI-011) from `Mini011PhaseBSetup.DealerSpecs`, leaving only `tmax_560`. Their `.asset` data files are left on disk (orphaned, not deleted) in case a real scrambler/van/boat model is added later.
  - **New: `Mini065TmaxDropTest.cs`** - actually RUNS the bike's physics via `Physics.Simulate()` in Edit Mode (works even though Play Mode itself doesn't run in this batch environment) instead of only checking wiring. Three phases: (1) drop with zero input and confirm it settles with no bounce and no lean, (2) full-throttle drive and confirm distance/speed/no-auto-wheelie, (3) explicit E-taps and confirm the wheelie mechanic lifts progressively and never flips. This is the first time in this whole task that actual FixedUpdate behaviour - not just structural wiring - has been verified before telling the user something works. Building it surfaced and fixed two real bugs in the test itself along the way: a `Physics.simulationMode` scoping bug where a build-up loop ran before switching into Script mode and silently did nothing, and a test-layout collision where Phase 2's own 54.9 km/h run drove the bike straight into the turn-marker walls placed around z=45, which looked exactly like "the wheelie mechanic is broken" (velocity read exactly zero) until traced back to a wall impact, not a physics bug.
  - `Mini065TmaxValidation.cs` gained a check that the Car Dealer's stock array contains exactly 1 item.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini064TmaxAssetPrep.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs` (DealerSpecs), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/TmaxBikeController.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/TmaxTestInput.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/VehicleSpawnController.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini065TmaxValidation.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini065TmaxDropTest.cs` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Vehicles/TMAX_560.prefab` (rebuilt), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/TMAX_Physics_Test.unity` (rebuilt), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Scene/prefab changes: `TMAX_560.prefab` rebuilt with the corrected wheel radius fit, visual padding, and the same measured axle positions as round 3 (unchanged - only the radius fit and cosmetic padding changed this round). `TMAX_Physics_Test.unity` and `GrandBayProof.unity` rebuilt to pick up the new dealer stock table and spawn logic.
- Verification commands: batch-mode compile (clean throughout), `Mini064TmaxAssetPrep.BuildPrefab` → `Mini065TmaxPhysicsTest.WireController` → `BuildTestScene` → `Mini011PhaseBSetup.BuildScene` → `Mini065TmaxValidation.Validate` → `Mini001SceneValidation.Run`, and (new this round) `Mini065TmaxDropTest.Run` - a real `Physics.Simulate()`-driven test, not just structural checks.
- Verification results: compile clean. `MINI-064/065 VALIDATION PASS` (including the new dealer-stock-count-of-1 check). `MINI-001 STATIC VALIDATION PASS`. **`Mini065TmaxDropTest` PASS** - drop bounce 0.000m, settles from t=0.22s, ends at 0.00deg lean; drive test travels 40.33m reaching 54.9 km/h at a max lean of only 0.47deg with zero auto-wheelie; wheelie-tap test reaches 35.5deg max angle (correctly capped, never flips) while driving 26-43 km/h under a realistic tap rhythm.
- Known issues (honest):
  - This is the first round with a real, passing physics-behaviour test - a meaningfully stronger claim than every previous round's "structurally wired, unverified." It is still not the same as a human playing it in the real Editor with a keyboard: the drop test's reflection-driven `FixedUpdate` loop and `Physics.Simulate()` reproduce PhysX closely but not perfectly identically to the Player-loop-driven Play Mode timing, camera-relative input, or actual keyboard feel.
  - The wheel-disc radius fit is now measured from a narrow, empirically-chosen probe band (0.09-0.12m) that was hand-tuned against this exact mesh via debug output - it is not a generally robust circle-fit algorithm and could need re-tuning if the source scan or its cleanup pipeline ever changes.
  - `bike_scrambler.asset`/`van_pickup.asset`/`boat_pirogue.asset` are now orphaned files (removed from the dealer's stock table, not deleted from disk) - harmless, but flagged rather than silently left unexplained.
  - `VehicleSpawnController` still finds the dealer by the fixed name `"NPC_CarDealer"` (`GameObject.Find`) - fragile if that NPC is ever renamed, though it has a same-position fallback for that case.
- Next action: user re-tests in the Editor - tries buying the TMAX again (should now land on the road, not in the store), confirms the dealer only offers the one bike, and tries the new E-tap wheelie control in `TMAX_Physics_Test.unity` or in-game.

### MINI-064/065 round 5 — real wheel X-alignment fix, hold-to-wheelie redesign, roll-bug root cause found and fixed

- Date: 2026-08-18
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User, after round 4: "i think you are disregarding the attached image every time... the rear tire and the model tire is still out of alignment. it should be aligned with the number plate. stop doing what you want and use the image as proper reference mainly... it rides well but the wheelie isnt working now when i press E. it seems like it wants to go up but it gigs leans more to the left... when you hold down e it should go up more and then the taping would help balancing it in the air... also the lean while riding should be a little more." Followed mid-task by "pause and let me check it manually for the wheel alignment" and "show me the image from the back and not the side."
- Implementation:
  - **Real wheel X-misalignment found and fixed.** Measured the mesh directly (Blender): the front wheel's true X-centre is 0.017m off the bike's own centreline (negligible), but the **rear wheel is genuinely 0.109m off-centre** - a real asymmetry in the source scan, not measurement noise (independently confirmed via a separate rim-band sample of 4000+ vertices). This is why the rear disc visibly sat beside the real wheel rather than on it. Fix required a real architecture decision: the physics `WheelCollider`s stay centred at X=0 (an off-centre rear collider would apply drive force off the vehicle's centreline and induce a persistent yaw pull - bad physics), while the *visual* disc overlays now use the real measured X-offset. This meant decoupling `TmaxWheelVisuals` from fully overwriting the disc's world position - it now only syncs height (suspension) and rotation (spin/steer) from the physics collider, leaving X/Z as authored from the real measurement. Verified by rendering the corrected axle marker directly onto the mesh from both the side and from behind - it sits exactly on the real wheel in both.
  - **Wheelie redesign, round 2 - hold-to-lift.** The previous "tap adds decaying sustain" design (round 3) wasn't landing for the user ("the wheelie isnt working now when i press E"). Replaced with a simpler, more standard scheme: `TryWheelieTap()` → `SetWheelieHeld(bool)`, read continuously (`Input.GetKey`, not `GetKeyDown`) - holding E climbs the target straight toward `maxWheelieAngle` at `wheelieRiseRate`; releasing sends it back to 0 at the same rate. This is also what gives "tapping helps balance" for free: a quick release-and-reapply lets the front settle a little before climbing again, rather than needing a second, separate balance system.
  - **The "leans more to the left" bug - real root cause found via an actual physics test, not guessed at.** Added roll-specific measurement to `Mini065TmaxDropTest` (`Vector3.SignedAngle` around the bike's forward axis, isolated from pitch) and it immediately proved the bug was real: roll grew in lockstep with wheelie pitch, peaking ~47 degrees at a 38-degree wheelie. Root cause, found through several real (not guessed) iterations documented in the code's own comments: **`ApplyStability`'s roll correction used the bike's raw `transform.forward` as both the error-measurement axis and the torque axis.** That is only a pure roll axis while the bike is level - once pitch is significant (exactly what a wheelie is), correcting "roll around a tilted axis" is not the same as correcting roll in the world frame, and the difference leaks into yaw, which is what actually produced the visible sideways lean. Fixed by flattening `transform.forward` onto the horizontal plane first, giving a roll axis that stays mathematically pure regardless of how much pitch is happening.
  - **Two real regressions hit and reverted before landing on the working fix**, both kept as comments in the code as a warning against repeating them: (1) removing the separate `ApplyUprightAssist` "stand" mechanism entirely broke *general driving stability* outright (a plain full-throttle test with zero wheelie involved tumbled past 100 degrees within one second) - it turned out to be genuinely load-bearing for driving, not just a wheelie-era leftover; (2) removing it AND raising `ApplyStability`'s gains to compensate caused a *new, worse* instability (an oscillation that flipped the bike a full 180 degrees). The combination that actually works: keep `ApplyUprightAssist` fully active and unconditional (proven stable for driving, 0.38-0.47 degree max lean across multiple tests) alongside the newly-fixed, flattened-axis `ApplyStability`.
  - **Visual lean while cornering increased** per "the lean while riding should be a little more" - `maxVisualLean` raised from 32 to 40 degrees (purely cosmetic, `VisualLeanRoot` only, never touches the physics root).
  - `Mini065TmaxDropTest.cs`'s wheelie phase rewritten for hold/release (was tap-rhythm) and now tracks roll specifically (not just general "lean", which conflates pitch+roll) both as a peak-during-hold value and a settled-after-release value - the fix that matters is that any transient roll during the most aggressive possible input (continuous full-hold from a standstill) *recovers*, not that it never occurs at all; the test's threshold reflects that honestly (peak transient up to 35 degrees is accepted since it demonstrably settles to ~0.1 degrees within 2 seconds of release, versus the original bug's 46-180 degrees that never recovered at all).
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini064TmaxAssetPrep.cs` (X-centre measurement + wiring), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/TmaxWheelVisuals.cs` (decoupled X/Z sync), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/TmaxBikeController.cs` (hold-based wheelie, `ApplyStability` axis fix, `maxVisualLean` 32→40), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/TmaxTestInput.cs` (hold-key wiring), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini065TmaxDropTest.cs` (hold/release + roll-specific test), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Vehicles/TMAX_560.prefab` (rebuilt), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/TMAX_Physics_Test.unity` (rebuilt), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Verification commands: batch-mode compile (clean throughout, after fixing several self-inflicted sed-editing syntax errors along the way), full rebuild pipeline (`Mini064TmaxAssetPrep.BuildPrefab` → `Mini065TmaxPhysicsTest.WireController` → `BuildTestScene` → `Mini011PhaseBSetup.BuildScene`, re-run a second time after the `maxVisualLean` change specifically to bake it in), `Mini065TmaxValidation.Validate`, `Mini001SceneValidation.Run`, and (the real proof) `Mini065TmaxDropTest.Run` re-run after every single change in this round - seven full iterations, each measured, not assumed, plus a final confirmation run after the visual-lean change.
- Verification results: **`Mini065TmaxDropTest` PASS** (final run): "the bike settles upright, drives under throttle without auto-wheelieing, lifts progressively while E is held, settles back down when released, and any roll during the wheelie fully recovers." Earlier same-round measurements: drop 0.000m bounce, settles instantly, 0.00deg lean; drive 40.33m travelled, 54.9 km/h top speed, 0.38-0.45deg max lean (multiple runs) with zero auto-wheelie; wheelie hold test reaches the full 38deg cap on hold, settles to 0.0deg wheelie angle within 2s of release, peak transient roll 25deg during the most aggressive possible hold (0 to max in ~1.4s) - recovering to **0.1deg** roll by the end of the release phase. `MINI-064/065 VALIDATION PASS`. `MINI-001 STATIC VALIDATION PASS`. Wheel X-alignment additionally confirmed by direct Blender renders of the corrected axle marker against the real mesh, both from the side and from directly behind, per the user's explicit request to check that specific view.
- Known issues (honest):
  - The wheelie still produces a **real, bounded, self-correcting roll transient** (up to ~25-27 degrees measured) during the most aggressive possible input (an instant full 2-second hold from a standing wheelie start). This is a large improvement over the original bug (46-180 degrees, never recovering) but is not mathematically zero. Further reducing it risks the same class of regression hit twice already in this round (removing/weakening the upright-assist stand breaks general driving badly) - the current configuration was reached by real, measured trial and error, not derived from first principles, and represents a considered stopping point given the risk of further tuning without a human in the loop.
  - This entire round was verified through the `Mini065TmaxDropTest` harness (`Physics.Simulate()` in Edit Mode), not real Play Mode - still the standing limitation for this whole task. The actual in-game feel, especially the wheelie's roll transient, needs the user's own hands-on test to confirm it reads as acceptable rather than just numerically bounded.
  - The front wheel's X-offset (0.017m) was left as measured rather than snapped to exactly 0 - it's small enough to be within measurement noise either way, and treating it as "real" is consistent with how the rear offset was handled.
- Next action: user re-tests in the Editor - checks wheel alignment from behind directly (not just via renders), tries holding E to wheelie and tapping to feel the balance behaviour, and reports whether the residual roll transient during an aggressive hold is noticeable/acceptable in practice.

### MINI-064/065 round 6 — wheelie raised to 80 degrees, faster rise, airborne steering, and a real fix for the steep-wheelie roll drift

- Date: 2026-08-18
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User, after round 5: "ok the riding works well. but the wheeliing still has issues but we are getting there. the bike goes up. make it go up alil bit faster and it goes to the right alittle when it goes up. try to make it go up straight." Followed by (after an initial "170 degrees" ask was interrupted/corrected): "make the wheelie go higher like 80 degrees up and faster and now it goes left sometimes. i think is need something to stabilize it in the air like hold it straight and only if i press left or right it will tilt."
- Implementation:
  - **Faster rise, twice.** `wheelieRiseRate` raised 30 -> 45 (first ask) -> 60 deg/s (second ask, "and faster").
  - **Higher cap.** `maxWheelieAngle` raised 38 -> 80 degrees per the user's explicit corrected number.
  - **The "goes to the right" bug - a second instance of the same axis-purity mistake already fixed once in ApplyStability, this time in `ApplyWheelie` itself.** `ApplyWheelie`'s pitch torque was measured and applied around the bike's raw `transform.right`, which is only a pure, roll-free lateral axis while the bike is level; once any residual roll exists, a tilted `transform.right` leaks pitch torque into yaw/roll. Fixed by building the pitch axis from the flattened forward direction instead (`flatRight = Cross(Vector3.up, flatForward)`), the same technique round 5 used for `ApplyStability`'s own axis.
  - **Airborne steering, a genuinely missing capability, not a bug.** `ApplySteering`'s yaw assist is gated on `frontWheel.isGrounded`, which is false throughout a real wheelie - so steering currently did nothing at all while airborne. Added `ApplyWheelieAirControl()`: while wheelieing (`currentWheelieTarget > 3`) AND the player is actually pressing left/right, it applies a direct `Vector3.up` torque scaled by `steerInput` (`wheelieAirSteerTorque`) so the bike can be deliberately turned/tilted in the air - exactly "only if I press left or right it will tilt." Deliberately does nothing when there's no input (see below for why).
  - **The "goes left sometimes" bug at 80 degrees - much worse than round 5's 38-degree case, and NOT the same fix.** A real physics test (`Mini065TmaxDropTest`, extended with roll-specific measurement in round 5) showed the raised/faster wheelie made roll measurably worse (58.7 degree peak vs round 5's 25-27) - genuine rigid-body behaviour (a fast-pitching, non-uniform-inertia body picks up more rotational coupling the harder it's driven), not a simple bug to "fix" once. Two further attempts were tried and real-tested before landing on the actual fix, both kept as warnings in the code:
    1. An initial version of `ApplyWheelieAirControl` also tried to "hold" a locked heading via `Vector3.up` torque when no input was pressed. This made things WORSE, not better - at 80 degrees of pitch the bike's forward axis is close to world-up, so torque meant as pure yaw is nearly indistinguishable from roll around the bike's own long axis (the two rotations stop being separate degrees of freedom near vertical). Removed; `ApplyWheelieAirControl` now only ever acts when the player is actually steering.
    2. Adding extra proportional/derivative gain to `ApplyStability`'s existing torque-based roll correction, scaled by wheelie depth, made things dramatically worse (93.6 degree peak roll, and the bike briefly tipped over sideways outright) - a classic PD-instability symptom: `AddTorque` integrates velocity every physics step, so pushing proportional gain up without bulletproof damping overshoots, and the overshoot compounds every step rather than settling.
    3. **The fix that actually worked**: add the extra roll authority to `ApplyUprightAssist` instead, not `ApplyStability`. `ApplyUprightAssist` already computes the exact zero-roll orientation for the bike's current forward via `Quaternion.LookRotation(forward, worldUp)` and eases toward it with `Slerp`, which is interpolation bounded to `[0,1]` per step - it geometrically cannot overshoot or oscillate the way a torque integrator can, and it stays exact at any pitch (not just small angles). Added `wheelieRollAssist`, additive to `ApplyUprightAssist`'s existing strength and scaled by how deep into the wheelie the bike currently is, so ordinary near-level driving/cornering is completely unaffected.
  - Net result, measured: peak transient roll during the single most aggressive possible input (instant full hold from a standing start, now reaching 80 degrees of pitch instead of 38) dropped to **20.6 degrees** - actually lower than round 5's 25-27 degree peak at the old, lower 38-degree cap, despite the wheelie now being both higher and faster.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/TmaxBikeController.cs` (`wheelieRiseRate`, `maxWheelieAngle`, `ApplyWheelie`'s flattened pitch axis, new `ApplyWheelieAirControl`, `ApplyUprightAssist`'s `wheelieRollAssist`), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Vehicles/TMAX_560.prefab` (rebuilt), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/TMAX_Physics_Test.unity` (rebuilt), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Verification commands: batch-mode compile (clean throughout), full rebuild pipeline (`Mini064TmaxAssetPrep.BuildPrefab` -> `Mini065TmaxPhysicsTest.WireController` -> `BuildTestScene` -> `Mini011PhaseBSetup.BuildScene`), `Mini065TmaxDropTest.Run` re-run after each of the four real iterations in this round (the two reverted regressions included - both measured, not guessed at, per this whole task's discipline), `Mini065TmaxValidation.Validate`, `Mini001SceneValidation.Run`.
- Verification results: **`Mini065TmaxDropTest` PASS** (final run) - drop/drive phases unchanged from round 5 (0.38 degree max lean while driving, 54.9 km/h top speed, 40.36m travelled); wheelie hold test now reaches the full 80-degree cap, settles to 0.0 degrees within 2s of release, peak transient roll **20.6 degrees** (down from round 5's 25-27 at a lower cap), settling to 0.1-0.2 degrees after release. `MINI-064/065 VALIDATION PASS`. `MINI-001 STATIC VALIDATION PASS`.
- Known issues (honest):
  - `ApplyWheelieAirControl`'s "only if I press left or right it will tilt" is a real, working capability (direct torque while steering, gated on wheelie state) but is unverified for FEEL - the drop test drives it with zero steering throughout (it only exists to test the "hold straight, no input" case cleanly), so the actual in-air steering response (how strong/floaty it feels) needs the user's own hands-on test with a controller/keyboard, not just a passing automated number.
  - The 20.6-degree peak transient roll is a real improvement but is still not mathematically zero - it is a genuine, bounded, self-correcting artifact of pitching a non-uniform rigid body this fast and this far, not a residual bug being tracked down. Further reduction risks the exact two regression classes already hit and reverted in this round (torque-based overcorrection, and near-vertical yaw/roll axis confusion) - both are now documented in code as warnings against retrying the same approach.
  - This entire round was verified through `Mini065TmaxDropTest` (`Physics.Simulate()` in Edit Mode), not real Play Mode - the user's own hands-on test (especially of the new airborne steering) is still the next real checkpoint.
- Next action: user re-tests holding E to a full 80-degree wheelie, checks it climbs faster and stays straight with no input, and tries steering left/right while airborne to confirm the new air-control tilt feels right.

### MINI-064/065 round 7 — a real visual-desync bug fixed, plus a live in-Editor wheelie tuner because the automated test can't see everything

- Date: 2026-08-18
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User, with a screenshot of the bike mid-wheelie: "you need to improve on this drastically because the back goes misaligned while trying to wheelie. also the wheelie still not working its wheeling more to the left and the bike hardly goes up. i am now sure how your tests passes but your tests are flawed. you need to have a different and more accurate way of testing the wheelie. maybe after you try again to fix it, you can put the wheelie controls as sliders into unity and i will adjust them to get the wheelie. you will just tell me what sliders to play in." Followed by "stop the bike did not flip upside down" (correcting that the screenshot was not a literal flip) and confirmation that they are testing in `TMAX_Physics_Test.unity` in real Play Mode, not the full game yet.
- Implementation:
  - **The rear-misalignment screenshot - a real, separate bug from every roll/pitch issue chased so far, and a genuine one this time.** The cosmetic cornering lean (`UpdateVisualLean`) rotates `VisualLeanRoot` - the SAME transform both wheel discs and the body mesh live under - around its own local Z axis, with zero awareness of the wheelie's own much larger, physics-real pitch already sitting on the Rigidbody root above it. Composing a local-Z "lean" on top of a large real pitch does not commute the way it does on top of a level bike, and the result visibly skews the whole visual body away from where the physics (WheelColliders, on a separate un-leaned branch) actually is - worse the more the player is also steering during the wheelie. Fixed by fading the cosmetic lean to zero as the wheelie deepens (`* (1f - wheelie01)`) - a wheelie doesn't need cornering lean anyway, so this has zero effect on ordinary riding.
  - **Taking the "your tests are flawed" report seriously rather than re-asserting the last PASS.** The user is right that `Mini065TmaxDropTest` drives the bike with exact synthetic inputs (instant full throttle, a boolean wheelie-held flag) set directly via reflection, never through `Input.GetAxis`/`GetKey` - it cannot reproduce Unity's default Input Manager's keyboard-axis smoothing ("gravity"/"sensitivity"), which ramps throttle up over real time and does not snap steering back to exactly 0 the instant a key is released. That gap plausibly explains both new symptoms directly: "hardly goes up" (throttle ramping more slowly in real play than the test's instant 100%, so wheelieMinSpeedKmh may not be reached as quickly as expected) and "goes left sometimes" (residual decayed Horizontal-axis value feeding the new `ApplyWheelieAirControl` from round 6, which the test - using an exact 0 or 1 - never exercised at a nonzero-but-unintended value). Rather than guess another blind numeric fix, two concrete things were done: (1) `ApplyWheelieAirControl`'s deadzone raised 0.05 -> 0.25, specifically to reject that kind of input-decay residue while still responding to a clear intentional press; (2) a real in-Editor tuning panel was built (`TmaxWheelieTuner.cs`), per the user's own explicit request, exposing `MaxWheelieAngle`, `WheelieRiseRate`, `WheelieHoldStrength`, `WheelieMinSpeedKmh`, `WheelieMaxSpeedKmh`, `WheelieAirSteerTorque`, `WheelieRollAssist`, `UprightAssist`, `UprightAssistLowSpeedBoost`, `UprightStrength`, `UprightDamping`, and `LowSpeedExtraStability` as live `GUI.HorizontalSlider`s that write straight into `TmaxBikeController` (new public get/set properties added for each), plus a live readout of speed/wheelie-target/real-pitch/real-roll, all updating every frame in real Play Mode - not a static number the user has to trust from a log. T toggles the panel. Test-scene-only (added by `Mini065TmaxPhysicsTest.BuildTestScene` alongside `TmaxTestInput`, never baked into the real `TMAX_560.prefab` used in the actual game).
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/TmaxBikeController.cs` (visual-lean wheelie fade, `ApplyWheelieAirControl` deadzone, new public tunable properties + `CurrentPitchAngle`/`CurrentRollAngle` readouts), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/TmaxWheelieTuner.cs` (new), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini065TmaxPhysicsTest.cs` (wires the tuner onto the test-scene bike), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Vehicles/TMAX_560.prefab` (rebuilt), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/TMAX_Physics_Test.unity` (rebuilt, now carries `TmaxWheelieTuner`), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Verification commands: batch-mode compile (clean), full rebuild pipeline, `Mini065TmaxDropTest.Run`, `Mini065TmaxValidation.Validate`, `Mini001SceneValidation.Run`.
- Verification results: all PASS, numbers unchanged from round 6 (0.38 degree max lean driving, 80-degree wheelie cap reached, 20.6-degree peak transient roll, fully recovering) - as expected, since this round's real fixes (visual lean fade, air-control deadzone, the tuner itself) are either purely cosmetic or only engage on values the synthetic test never produces, so a clean re-pass here confirms no regression rather than proving the actual user-reported symptoms are gone.
- Known issues (honest):
  - **This round's core acknowledgment**: the automated `Mini065TmaxDropTest` cannot, by construction, verify whether the "hardly goes up" and "goes left" symptoms are actually fixed - it never drives the bike through real `Input.GetAxis` timing/decay, which is the leading suspect for both. The deadzone widening is a reasoned, plausible mitigation, not a proven fix. The tuner is the real answer: the next real checkpoint is the user dragging sliders in Play Mode and reporting which values (if any) make it feel right, rather than another round of blind numeric guesses re-verified only by the same limited harness.
  - The visual-lean-during-wheelie fix is a genuine, verified-by-reading-the-code root cause for the screenshot's rear misalignment, but has not yet been re-confirmed against a fresh screenshot from the user - only reasoned through and compile/structurally verified.
  - Tuner values are live-only (not persisted to the prefab) - once the user finds numbers that feel right, they need to be reported back so they can be baked into `TmaxBikeController`'s serialized defaults; exiting Play Mode discards whatever the sliders were set to.
- Next action: user opens `TMAX_Physics_Test.unity`, enters Play Mode, drives up to speed, holds E, and drags the tuner's sliders (press T to show/hide the panel) until the wheelie feels right - watching the live speed/target-angle/real-pitch/real-roll readout while doing so - then reports back which slider values worked so they can be made the new defaults.

### MINI-064/065 round 8 — weight-bias sliders (centre of mass)

- Date: 2026-08-18
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User: "can you implement sliders for the weight of the bike as well like back, front, left and right."
- Implementation: The physically correct way to model "shifting the bike's weight" is offsetting the Rigidbody's own centre of mass, not faking it with a multiplier on some other system - a real rider shifting their weight back genuinely makes a motorcycle's front come up easier (less weight over the front wheel, more over the rear pivot). Added `comOffsetForward`/`comOffsetRight` fields to `TmaxBikeController`, applied on top of the already-measured baseline COM (captured once in `Awake` from the `COM` marker, unchanged) via a new `ApplyCenterOfMass()` called every `FixedUpdate` - so a slider dragged mid-Play-Mode takes effect on the very next physics step, same as every other tuner value. Implemented as two offsets (front<->back, left<->right) rather than four independent fields, since "front" and "back" are the two ends of one physical axis, not separate quantities - the tuner's `DirectionalSlider` shows both of the user's own named directions as the slider's own endpoint labels, so dragging toward "Back" means exactly that. Front<->back ranged +-0.5m, left<->right +-0.3m (roughly the bike's own half-length/half-width). Public `CenterOfMassOffsetForward`/`CenterOfMassOffsetRight` properties added for the tuner to write into, matching the pattern from round 7's other tunables.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/TmaxBikeController.cs` (`comOffsetForward`/`comOffsetRight` fields, `baseCenterOfMassLocal`, `ApplyCenterOfMass()`, public properties), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/TmaxWheelieTuner.cs` (two new `DirectionalSlider` rows under a "Weight bias" section), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Vehicles/TMAX_560.prefab` (rebuilt), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/TMAX_Physics_Test.unity` (rebuilt), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Verification commands: batch-mode compile (clean), full rebuild pipeline, `Mini065TmaxDropTest.Run`, `Mini065TmaxValidation.Validate`, `Mini001SceneValidation.Run`.
- Verification results: all PASS, numbers unchanged from round 7 within normal run-to-run noise (0.38 degree max lean driving, 80-degree wheelie cap, ~20 degree peak transient roll) - expected, since both new offsets default to 0 (exactly the original measured COM) until a slider is actually moved.
- Known issues (honest): the weight-bias sliders are, like every other tuner value, live-only and test-scene-only - not persisted, and never baked into the real gameplay prefab. Their actual effect on wheelie feel (how much offset genuinely makes a difference) has not been explored yet; that is exactly what the user is expected to do with them.
- Next action: user drags the two new weight-bias sliders (Front<->Back, Left<->Right) alongside the existing wheelie sliders to see whether shifting the centre of mass rearward makes the wheelie noticeably easier/more controllable, and reports back what felt right.

### MINI-064/065 round 9 — the weight sliders were unreachable, not missing

- Date: 2026-08-18
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User: "where are the weight controls i asked you for."
- Implementation: The round 8 weight-bias sliders WERE present in the panel and in the rebuilt scene (confirmed by grepping the scene file for the component before touching anything), but the panel was a fixed-position, non-scrolling stack of raw `Rect`-positioned controls - twelve other sliders were placed above the weight section, pushing it down to roughly y=470-540 in a panel that kept growing taller with every round added. Any Game view shorter than that showed no way to reach them at all - not absent, just unreachable, and the previous panel had no scrolling to recover from that. Rewrote `TmaxWheelieTuner.OnGUI` from manual `Rect` math to a proper `GUILayout` scroll view (`BeginArea`/`BeginScrollView`), bounded to fit the actual screen height (`Mathf.Min(Screen.height - 20f, 640f)`) so it can never render past the visible area again regardless of Game view size, and moved the weight-bias section to the very TOP of the panel, directly under the title, so it is the first thing visible with zero scrolling required - a direct fix for "where are the weight controls", not just a general robustness pass. The rest of the sliders (Wheelie, Stability) are grouped under their own labelled headers below it, reachable by scrolling.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/TmaxWheelieTuner.cs` (rewritten to GUILayout scroll view, weight-bias moved first), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Vehicles/TMAX_560.prefab` (rebuilt), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/TMAX_Physics_Test.unity` (rebuilt), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).

- Verification results: all PASS, physics numbers unchanged (this round touches no physics/gameplay code at all, only OnGUI layout).
- Known issues (honest): this is a layout fix reasoned from the code and the round-8 change history, not confirmed against a fresh screenshot of the panel actually rendering correctly in the user's own Game view - the true test is the user opening Play Mode and immediately seeing the weight sliders without scrolling.
- Next action: user re-enters Play Mode in `TMAX_Physics_Test.unity` and confirms the weight-bias sliders are now the first thing visible in the tuner panel (T to toggle), then continues tuning per round 8's request.

### MINI-064/065 round 10 — debug force override, tunable lift authority (no more hidden ceiling), pinned live status, precise numeric entry

- Date: 2026-08-18
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User, across several messages: "something is wrong because the bike does not wheelie even if I play in all the sliders, even if i put the slider for the weight all to the back, the wheelie target degrees numbers says it goes up at 80 but it doesnt even lift the ground, maybe we should have invisible holders to bring it up and hold it upright that can be adjusted. and maybe the wheelie degrees is not acurate thats why maybe i need to put it to 270 just to test, and the weight sliders and all the sliders can we make it more so i can be more specific with it." Then: "let the bike flip over if it has to i will fix it with the slider." Then: "make sure i can see the sliders in the ui."
- Implementation:
  - **Likely real root cause of "doesn't lift the ground even with sliders maxed": a genuinely hidden, un-sliderable ceiling.** `ApplyWheelie`'s upward torque correction was clamped to a hardcoded constant (`Mathf.Clamp(correction, -80f, 16f)`) with no field or slider anywhere controlling the `16`. No matter how far ANY other slider was pushed (hold strength, rise rate, weight bias...), the actual applied torque could never exceed that invisible cap - which fits the reported symptom exactly. Replaced with two tunable fields, `wheelieLiftClamp` (was the hardcoded 16) and `wheelieRecoverClamp` (was the hardcoded 80), both exposed as sliders. Per the user's own explicit instruction ("let the bike flip over if it has to i will fix it with the slider"), the lift slider's range goes well past the old safe default (0-150, default unchanged at 16 so nothing regresses silently) - the user now has genuine authority to push it hard enough to flip, and dial it back themselves.
  - **"the wheelie target degrees numbers says it goes up at 80 but it doesnt even lift the ground" - also very plausibly a settings-vs-live-reading mix-up, not (only) a physics bug.** The "Max wheelie angle (deg)" slider is a SETTING (the cap) - it always shows whatever it's set to (80) regardless of whether a wheelie is actually happening, which reads exactly like "it says 80" if mistaken for a live progress number. Added a pinned status box (see below) that puts the live `WheelieAngle` (the real progress target) directly next to `CurrentPitchAngle` (the real physics angle) so the two can never be confused again, plus relabelled the setting slider itself "a SETTING, not live progress".
  - **"invisible holders to bring it up and hold it upright that can be adjusted" - implemented literally.** New `ApplyDebugForcedPitch()` on `TmaxBikeController`: a direct pitch override, gated only by its own strength slider (0 = off), that bypasses every gate the normal wheelie has entirely - speed window, brake, ground, eligibility, the lift/recover clamps, all of it. Exists to answer one clean diagnostic question: can the physics rig be moved to any given angle at all. If this lifts the bike and the normal wheelie mechanic still doesn't, the bug is provably in `ApplyWheelie`'s own gating/authority, not the rig.
  - **"maybe I need to put it to 270 just to test" - explained rather than silently implemented.** Pitch here is measured via `asin(forward.y)`, which is mathematically bounded to +-90 degrees - a unit vector's elevation angle has no meaning past vertical, so "270 degrees of pitch" isn't representable by this metric at all; it would be an entirely different manoeuvre (a full backward loop) needing different physics, not "a bigger wheelie". The debug force's angle slider is capped at the real mathematical maximum (90) with a tooltip explaining why, rather than accepting a number that can't mean what's intended.
  - **"can we make it more so i can be more specific with it" - every slider now has an adjacent numeric field.** Type an exact value and press Enter to commit, in addition to dragging - implemented with a per-field edit-buffer dictionary keyed by label, so a field only shows the live physics value when NOT currently focused (otherwise the value being typed would be overwritten every single frame by Unity's own IMGUI re-render, a known gotcha with naive slider+textfield combos).
  - **"make sure i can see the sliders in the ui" - a pinned, always-visible status box added above the scroll area**, showing live speed, whether E is currently held, WHY the wheelie is/isn't eligible (not moving / speed window / braking / grounded), the live target, real pitch, and real roll - all in one place, outside the scrollable area so it's on screen regardless of scroll position. The panel continues to open visible by default (T only hides it, never required to reveal it).
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/TmaxBikeController.cs` (`wheelieLiftClamp`/`wheelieRecoverClamp` replacing the hardcoded clamp, `debugForcedPitchAngle`/`debugForcedPitchStrength` + `ApplyDebugForcedPitch()`, `WheelieEligible`/`IsWheelieHeld` readouts, matching public properties), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/TmaxWheelieTuner.cs` (pinned status box, debug force section, lift/recover authority sliders, numeric entry fields for every slider), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Vehicles/TMAX_560.prefab` (rebuilt), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/TMAX_Physics_Test.unity` (rebuilt), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Verification commands: batch-mode compile (clean), full rebuild pipeline, `Mini065TmaxDropTest.Run`, `Mini065TmaxValidation.Validate`, `Mini001SceneValidation.Run`, plus a direct grep of the rebuilt scene confirming `TmaxWheelieTuner` is present.
- Verification results: all PASS, numbers unchanged from round 9 (`wheelieLiftClamp`/`wheelieRecoverClamp` default to the exact same values the old hardcoded constants had, and `debugForcedPitchStrength` defaults to 0/off, so nothing regresses silently - every new capability is opt-in via a slider the user hasn't touched yet in the headless test).
- Known issues (honest):
  - The hidden-clamp theory is a strong, reasoned candidate for "doesn't lift the ground even with everything maxed" - it is the one place in the whole wheelie system that genuinely had NO slider controlling it before this round - but it has not been confirmed as THE cause by the user's own hands-on retest yet. If raising "Lift authority" still doesn't produce visible lift, the debug force section is the fallback: if THAT also fails to move the bike, the problem is more fundamental (wiring/references) rather than tuning, and needs a different investigation.
  - Numeric text-field entry is a first pass at "more specific" - it commits on Enter, not on every keystroke, and clicking away without pressing Enter discards the typed value back to the live one; this is a deliberate simplicity trade-off, not fully polished field-editing behaviour.
- Next action: user re-enters Play Mode, checks the pinned status box's ELIGIBLE line while trying to wheelie (to see directly whether the gate is even opening), tries raising "Lift authority" if the wheelie still doesn't lift, and falls back to "Force pitch to"/"Force strength" in DEBUG FORCE as a last resort to confirm the rig itself can move at all.

### MINI-064/065 round 11 — real research, and a redesign to match: real rear-wheel torque instead of abstract chassis torque

- Date: 2026-08-18
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User: "ok so the lift when i adjusted it the bike went up a little. the wheelie says the bike goes up 89 but it doesnt actually go up. can you research online to see the best wheelie codes online and compare it with your but the wheelie degrees reading it very false it should start reading when the front tire is off the ground. do it without the character's weight in mind so when the character gets on the bike his weight will become zero. i think you need to go online if you have to use my browser control do that find out online what is the best way even if you have to go on youtube videos to do so."
- Research (WebSearch/WebFetch, not guessed at): looked up real motorcycle wheelie physics and existing Unity implementations/forum threads.
  - [UNSW Physclips - Wheelies or wheelstands](https://www.animations.physics.unsw.edu.au/jw/wheelies.htm): the real physics is torque = wheelRadius x rear-tyre friction force, and that force acts at ground level - the lever arm between the rear contact patch and the centre of mass (the "L/H ratio": horizontal distance to CoM over its height) is what actually produces the lifting torque, not an abstract chassis torque.
  - [Unity Discussions - "Getting a dirt bike to wheelie?"](https://discussions.unity.com/t/getting-a-dirt-bike-to-wheelie/462606) and [Unity Discussions - "Bike wheelies rigidbody"](https://discussions.unity.com/t/bike-wheelies-rigidbody/23745): multiple independent developers report that naive `AddForceAtPosition`/chassis `AddTorque` approaches "don't work as expected" for a wheelie - the same class of symptom reported here.
  - [Unity Discussions - "Motorbike physics"](https://discussions.unity.com/t/motorbike-physics/503409): the recommended architecture is the OPPOSITE of what this file was doing - apply real motor torque to the rear wheel, and use the pitch angle/velocity as a PD GOVERNOR that reduces power to prevent over-rotation, not as the thing that lifts the front in the first place.
  - Sources: [UNSW Physclips](https://www.animations.physics.unsw.edu.au/jw/wheelies.htm), [Unity Discussions - dirt bike wheelie](https://discussions.unity.com/t/getting-a-dirt-bike-to-wheelie/462606), [Unity Discussions - bike wheelies rigidbody](https://discussions.unity.com/t/bike-wheelies-rigidbody/23745), [Unity Discussions - motorbike physics](https://discussions.unity.com/t/motorbike-physics/503409).
- Implementation:
  - **Redesigned the lift mechanism to match the research.** Added `wheelieRearTorqueBoost`: real extra `motorTorque` added directly to the rear `WheelCollider` (on top of ordinary throttle) while wheelieing, going through the SAME real WheelCollider friction/suspension physics as normal acceleration - not an abstract torque applied straight to the chassis. This is now the PRIMARY lift mechanism (new tuner slider, "REAR TORQUE BOOST - the real lift mechanism, try this first"); the previous PD-based pitch correction (still present, tunable via Lift/Recover authority) is now a secondary assist and governor rather than the sole lift source. As a direct consequence, the weight-bias sliders from round 8 (front/back, left/right) now have a genuinely physically real effect matching the actual L/H criterion from the research, rather than fighting an unrelated abstract number.
  - **"the wheelie degrees reading it very false it should start reading when the front tire is off the ground" - implemented exactly as asked.** `IsWheelieing` used to be `currentWheelieTarget > 1f` (the controller's SETPOINT/intent, climbing the instant the button is held and eligible, regardless of whether the bike had actually left the ground). Changed to the real physical signal: `!frontWheel.isGrounded`. New `IsFrontWheelGrounded` property and a "FRONT WHEEL: on the ground / OFF THE GROUND - genuinely wheelieing" line added to the tuner's pinned status box.
  - **"do it without the character's weight in mind so when the character gets on the bike his weight will become zero" - the bike's mass no longer pre-bakes a rider.** `BikeMassKg` was 280 (real ~220kg wet weight + ~60kg of "headroom for a future rider" from an earlier round) - reduced to 220, the real riderless TMAX weight. That extra 60kg was real, unaccounted weight working directly against every wheelie attempt, since more mass needs more torque to lift per the real physics. Documented that when MINI-066 adds a rider, their mass should be ADDED at mount time, not folded back into this constant.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/TmaxBikeController.cs` (`wheelieRearTorqueBoost` + wiring into `ApplyWheelie`, `IsWheelieing`/`IsFrontWheelGrounded` now liftoff-based, matching public property), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini064TmaxAssetPrep.cs` (`BikeMassKg` 280->220), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/TmaxWheelieTuner.cs` (rear torque boost slider, front-wheel-grounded status line), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Vehicles/TMAX_560.prefab` (rebuilt), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/TMAX_Physics_Test.unity` (rebuilt), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Verification commands: batch-mode compile (clean), full rebuild pipeline, `Mini065TmaxDropTest.Run`, `Mini065TmaxValidation.Validate`, `Mini001SceneValidation.Run`.
- Verification results: **`Mini065TmaxDropTest` PASS.** Drop/drive phases unaffected structurally, though the lighter mass (220 vs 280kg) means faster acceleration and a higher speed reached in the same time window (56.8 km/h at t=4s vs round 10's ~46 km/h) - expected, not a bug. Wheelie hold test reaches the full 80-degree cap and settles to 0.0 degrees within 2s of release (same as before). **Honest regression**: peak transient roll during the most aggressive possible hold rose to 32.2 degrees (up from round 10's ~20.6) - still under the test's 35-degree accepted-transient threshold so it still PASSes, but the real rear-wheel torque genuinely introduces more rotational disturbance than the old abstract approach did, consistent with it now being real physics (real wheel spin/friction forces, not a hand-tuned number). Fully recovers to 0.3 degrees after release either way. `MINI-064/065 VALIDATION PASS`. `MINI-001 STATIC VALIDATION PASS`.
- Known issues (honest):
  - The core, most important claim of this round - "does the bike now actually lift off the ground when driven by a real player, not just in the headless test" - is NOT yet confirmed by the user's own hands-on retest. The redesign is grounded in real, cited research and passes the same automated harness that has passed every prior (sometimes wrong) round, so the harness result alone should not be taken as proof this is felt as fixed in real play.
  - The roll increase (20.6 -> 32.2 degrees peak transient) is a real, measured trade-off of switching to physically-real rear-wheel torque, not free - it may need its own tuning pass (Wheelie roll assist, Upright assist) once the user confirms the lift itself now works.
  - The rider-mass removal (280->220kg) is a real, deliberate scope decision, not a placeholder: MINI-066 (rider) does not exist yet, so there is currently nothing to add rider mass to at mount time - that hook needs to be built alongside MINI-066 itself, not before.
- Next action: user re-tests in Play Mode - checks whether the REAR TORQUE BOOST slider (new, defaults to 900, try raising toward 1500-3000) makes the front genuinely leave the ground (watch the pinned status box's "FRONT WHEEL" line, not just the target reading), and reports back whether this redesign actually solves the "doesn't lift" problem in real play, plus whether the increased roll is noticeable/needs its own tuning pass.

### MINI-064/065 round 12 — simplified to one "invisible hydraulic" slider, after a real regression caught and reverted

- Date: 2026-08-18
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User: "this is stressing me can we just have an invisible hydraullic to bring the bike up to make it appear like it is wheelieing. that sounds easy." Then, mid-task: "i hope the bike stays straight and not turn."
- Implementation:
  - **First attempt (WRONG, caught by the real test, not shipped as-is):** Replaced round 11's multi-part lift system (rear torque boost + separate lift/recover authority clamps) with a single plain SYMMETRIC PD "actuator" - the same shape as the existing `ApplyDebugForcedPitch`, just driven by the wheelie's own target. `Mini065TmaxDropTest` immediately proved this was worse, not simpler-but-equivalent: peak roll rose to 91.8 degrees (worse than round 10's 20.6 at the exact same 80-degree/60deg-per-second settings), and a second attempt at lower strength with more damping made it WORSE AGAIN (103.5 degrees) with roll no longer recovering after release at all. The asymmetric behaviour deleted in this first attempt - gentle while climbing, much stronger while correcting an overshoot - was real, tested safety margin from round 10, not incidental complexity; removing it reintroduced exactly the instability it existed to prevent.
  - **The actual fix**: restored round 10's proven asymmetric shape (gentle climb, strong overshoot correction), but collapsed it onto the single exposed `wheelieHydraulicStrength` slider instead of separate hold-strength/lift-clamp/recover-clamp sliders - the torque ceiling now scales directly WITH the slider (5x on the recovery side, same ratio round 10's separately-tuned 16/80 clamp had) rather than being a fixed number the slider can't reach past, which is what actually caused round 10's original "doesn't lift even with sliders maxed" complaint in the first place. The user gets the one-slider simplicity they asked for without losing the tested safety margin.
  - Removed `wheelieRearTorqueBoost`, `wheelieLiftClamp`, `wheelieRecoverClamp`, and `wheelieHoldStrength` entirely (fields, properties, and their tuner sliders) - `wheelieHydraulicStrength` is now the only wheelie-strength number in the whole system. Debug Force (the literal override) is kept as a last-resort section at the bottom of the tuner, since the normal wheelie now works the same simple way it does.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/TmaxBikeController.cs` (`wheelieHydraulicStrength` replacing four separate fields, `ApplyWheelie` restored to the proven asymmetric shape driven by one slider), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/TmaxWheelieTuner.cs` (WHEELIE section led by one "Wheelie strength (the hydraulic)" slider, Debug Force moved to the bottom as a last resort), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Vehicles/TMAX_560.prefab` (rebuilt), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/TMAX_Physics_Test.unity` (rebuilt), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Verification commands: batch-mode compile (clean throughout, including after the reverted first attempt), full rebuild pipeline, `Mini065TmaxDropTest.Run` (three real iterations: the failed symmetric attempt, a failed lower-strength/more-damping retry, and the working asymmetric-on-one-slider fix), `Mini065TmaxValidation.Validate`, `Mini001SceneValidation.Run`.
- Verification results: **`Mini065TmaxDropTest` PASS.** Peak transient roll during the most aggressive possible hold: **8.6 degrees** - the best result of any round so far (better than round 10's 20.6 at the same 80-degree/60deg-per-second settings), fully recovering to 0.2 degrees after release. `MINI-064/065 VALIDATION PASS`. `MINI-001 STATIC VALIDATION PASS`.
- Known issues (honest):
  - This round is a direct example of why this whole task insists on a real `Physics.Simulate()` test before reporting anything fixed - a first attempt at exactly what the user asked for ("simple, one slider") was genuinely a regression, caught only because the test ran before the change was described as done, not after.
  - As with round 11, whether this ACTUALLY produces a visible, satisfying wheelie in real Play Mode (not just a passing headless number) is still unconfirmed - the user's own hands-on retest with the single "Wheelie strength" slider is the real checkpoint.
  - The panel is genuinely simpler now (one wheelie-strength slider instead of four), but Debug Force still exists as a fallback if the hydraulic somehow still doesn't lift in real play - it hasn't been needed in the headless test, but it's still there.
- Next action: user re-enters Play Mode, holds E, and confirms the wheelie now lifts smoothly and stays straight (not turning/leaning, per their explicit "i hope the bike stays straight and not turn") using just the one "Wheelie strength (the hydraulic)" slider - raising it if the lift still isn't strong enough, per its own tooltip.

### MINI-064/065 round 13 — real physical anti-roll "trike" outriggers, after two real regressions caught and fixed

- Date: 2026-08-18
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User: "this is doing the same this the hydralic lift is lifting it but the bike keeps moving right when wheelieing can you make it two invisible wheels at the back and one invisible wheel at the front so it will appear as a bike but it will be a trike only when you press E to wheelie." Then: "i think also once the bike is wheeling as well the trike colider should still stay on while the bike is up."
- Implementation:
  - **The idea itself is sound and was implemented as real physics, not another correction hack**: two passive rear "outrigger" anchor points either side of the rear axle (same height/Z as the real rear wheel), providing real anti-roll support - a genuine physical trike layout while wheelieing, exactly as asked.
  - **First attempt (WRONG, caught by the drop test): real WheelCollider components.** Added two extra WheelColliders, disabled by default, toggling `.enabled` at runtime to park/deploy them. A real drop test proved this broke the vehicle's basic drop/settle physics - Phase 1 stopped settling cleanly (permanent 0.30 degree lean, resting ~0.045m lower than the proven baseline) even though the toggle only ever wrote `false->false` while parked during that phase. Isolated by literally commenting the call out and re-testing (confirmed: removing it restored the exact baseline numbers).
  - **Second attempt (also WRONG, also caught): moving the WheelColliders' Y position instead of toggling `.enabled`.** Reasoned this might avoid whatever the `.enabled` toggle was disrupting - it did not. The exact same Phase 1 failure occurred, identically, whether the stabilizers were parked 3m or 0.5m above their rest height, which itself proved the failure had nothing to do with distance/suspension effects: merely having two additional ENABLED WheelCollider components attached to the Rigidbody - regardless of where they are or whether they touch anything - measurably corrupts the vehicle's baseline physics (most likely via Unity's automatic inertia tensor calculation, which WheelCollider appears to contribute to independent of ground contact).
  - **The actual fix**: stopped using real WheelCollider (or any Collider) components for this entirely. The two anchors are now plain, physics-inert Transform markers with no Collider at all. `ApplyTrikeStabilizers` raycasts straight down from each one only while active, and if it finds ground within range, applies a real spring+damper force via `Rigidbody.AddForceAtPosition` at the actual hit point - the same "real force at a real contact point" principle the round-11 wheelie research already established as correct, just applied here without ever touching the Rigidbody's own collider/mass setup. This is both correct physics (real lever arm through a real contact point) and provably inert when not active (a plain Transform contributes nothing to Rigidbody mass/inertia calculations at all).
