  - **Active whenever ANY of**: the player wants a wheelie and the eligibility window is open (support exists BEFORE roll can start, not catching up after), the wheelie target is still above zero (covers climb and release ramp-down), OR the front wheel is genuinely off the ground right now - the last condition directly answers the user's follow-up ("should still stay on while the bike is up") using the real physical liftoff state, not just the controller's own target.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini064TmaxAssetPrep.cs` (two plain Transform anchors, no Collider, replacing the two failed WheelCollider attempts), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini065TmaxPhysicsTest.cs` (wires the anchors), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/TmaxBikeController.cs` (`rearStabLeftAnchor`/`rearStabRightAnchor` fields, `stabilizerRayRange`/`stabilizerSpringRate`/`stabilizerDamping` tunables, `ApplyTrikeStabilizers`/`ApplyStabilizerSpring` raycast-spring implementation), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Vehicles/TMAX_560.prefab` (rebuilt), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/TMAX_Physics_Test.unity` (rebuilt), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scenes/GrandBayProof.unity` (rebuilt).
- Verification commands: batch-mode compile (clean after each of the three attempts), full rebuild pipeline each time, `Mini065TmaxDropTest.Run` (four real iterations: two failed and reverted, isolated by direct comparison against a version with the feature removed entirely, plus one confirming distance didn't matter, and the final passing raycast version), `Mini065TmaxValidation.Validate`, `Mini001SceneValidation.Run`.
- Verification results: **`Mini065TmaxDropTest` PASS**, and critically, Phase 1 (drop/settle) and Phase 2 (drive) numbers are back to EXACTLY the proven pre-round-13 baseline (settles at Y=0.199, 0.00 degree final lean, 63.0 km/h top speed, 41.82m travelled) - proving the raycast approach is genuinely inert when not active, unlike both WheelCollider attempts. Wheelie hold test reaches the full 80-degree cap, peak transient roll 8.6 degrees (matching round 12's best result), fully recovering to 0.2 degrees after release. `MINI-064/065 VALIDATION PASS`. `MINI-001 STATIC VALIDATION PASS`.
- Known issues (honest):
  - The stabilizer spring's own tuning (`stabilizerSpringRate`=6000, `stabilizerDamping`=400, `stabilizerRayRange`=0.15m) is a first pass, not exhaustively tuned - the drop test's roll numbers already look good (8.6 degree peak, matching the pre-trike best case) but the real in-air FEEL of a trike-supported wheelie (does it feel like genuine outrigger support, or is it barely noticeable) is unconfirmed by the automated test, which never physically exercises lateral roll disturbance the same way real uneven player input would.
  - This round is a strong example of why every change in this task goes through the real drop test before being reported as working - a plausible-sounding, reasonable engineering idea (real WheelColliders for real physics) was wrong twice in a row for a non-obvious reason (WheelCollider's own contribution to automatic inertia tensor, independent of ground contact), and only the test caught it before either version was described as fixed.
  - No visible/cosmetic representation of the "trike" exists (no wheel disc, as intended - "invisible" per the request) - this is physics-only, matching what was actually asked for.
- Next action: user re-tests holding E to wheelie in real Play Mode, specifically checking whether the sideways drift ("keeps moving right when wheelieing") is now visibly reduced/gone, and whether the trike support still feels present throughout the whole time the bike is up (not just while first lifting), per their own follow-up request.

### MINI-064/065 round 14 — the wheelie never actually worked, and my own test was the reason I didn't know

- Date: 2026-08-18
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User, increasingly frustrated across several messages: "you are not listening to me it is still not working properly... it doesnt life the ground much like maybe 1 or 2 degrees up and also it leans to the right still... research online and set this up." Then: "put the bike to wheelie as you press E from 1mph", "check the wheel lift from the ground make sure the colider and the model wheel lifts the ground", "add more torque to help this on the rear wheels. make it abitrarily life the ground and then i can adjust the slider. do not make it fall or move to any side, keep it straight in the air", "make it go all the way to 360 abitruariliy", "dont put caps on the wheel height yet, i will put the caps with the slider", "make the coliders wide enough so it will wheelie striaght", "just ensure what every changes you make the REAL max pitch: 76.6 or even higher".
- **Root cause of ~10 rounds of false progress: the drop test measured the controller's own setpoint, not the bike.** `Mini065TmaxDropTest` recorded `bike.WheelieAngle`, which returns `currentWheelieTarget` - a value the controller assigns to ITSELF every step, whether or not the Rigidbody moved. So the harness reported "reached 80 degrees, PASS" while the bike physically pitched ~7 degrees and never left the ground. Every wheelie PASS from round 3 onward was worthless, and the user's repeated "it doesn't actually go up" was correct every single time. They had already named it exactly: "the wheelie degrees reading it very false it should start reading when the front tire is off the ground."
- Implementation:
  - **Fixed the test first, then let it fail honestly.** It now measures `CurrentPitchAngle` (real world-frame Rigidbody pitch), counts physics steps where the front WheelCollider is genuinely airborne, and raycasts real ground clearance for BOTH the front collider and the visible front wheel disc separately (they are driven by different code, so one lifting is not proof the other did). First honest run: real pitch 7.2 degrees, front wheel airborne 0 of 100 steps - the failure reproduced exactly as reported.
  - **Also fixed a second measurement bug in the test**: its roll check used the bike's raw pitched `transform.forward` as the roll axis - the same axis-purity bug fixed inside the controller back in round 5, still present in the harness. Once pitch was large it simply reported pitch back as "roll" (the trace showed roll matching REALpitch to the decimal at every step). Replaced with a pitch-independent measurement (lateral component of the bike's up-vector against a ground-flattened heading).
  - **Replaced persuasion with direct drive.** Every earlier round tried to talk the bike into pitching up with torque; 220kg of bike, WheelCollider suspension pushback, and this class's own stabilisers always won. `ApplyWheelie` now directly drives the pose: rotation is built as yaw x pitch with **no roll term at all** (so leaning sideways is mathematically impossible - the "keep it straight in the air"/trike behaviour), and it rotates about the **rear contact patch** rather than the centre of mass, so the front genuinely rises instead of the rear sinking. Angle is tracked as an accumulating number rather than measured back via `asin(forward.y)`, so it is not capped at 90 degrees and can be driven to a full 360 as requested. No internal cap - `maxWheelieAngle`'s slider (now 0-360) is the only limit, per "I will put the caps with the slider".
  - **Made the stabilisers stand down during a forced wheelie** (`WheelieForcingPose`). `ApplyUprightAssist` in particular slerps straight toward a levelled pose every step - leaving it running during an intentional 90-degree pitch simply cancelled the wheelie, and is very likely a major part of why torque never lifted anything in earlier rounds.
  - **Two further real bugs found by reading the passing trace rather than trusting the verdict**: (1) the both-wheels-airborne early return froze `currentWheelieTarget` during a genuinely high wheelie, so the release ramp ran at 0.14 deg/step instead of 1.2 and the bike hung in the air; (2) `rearWheel.isGrounded` chatters at high angle, flickering eligibility true/false every step so `MoveTowards` alternated climb/fall and real pitch stalled at 66.7 degrees. Fixed by separating "can START a wheelie" (needs rear grip and a speed window) from "can SUSTAIN one" (just holding the button).
  - Wheelie now available from **1 km/h** (`wheelieMinSpeedKmh` 12 -> 1) per "from 1mph" - the old 12km/h floor was a real reason pressing E often did nothing. Extra real rear-wheel `motorTorque` while held (`wheelieRearTorqueBoost`) per "add more torque on the rear wheels". Rear stabiliser outriggers widened to 0.9m per side (~1.8m track, vs a 0.82m-wide bike) per "make the coliders wide enough".
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini065TmaxDropTest.cs` (real-pitch/liftoff/clearance measurement, pitch-independent roll, 76.6-degree floor), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/TmaxBikeController.cs` (direct-drive `ApplyWheelie`, `WheelieForcingPose` gating, start/sustain eligibility split, min speed 1km/h, rear torque boost), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/TmaxWheelieTuner.cs` (angle slider to 360), `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini064TmaxAssetPrep.cs` (wider outriggers), prefab/scenes rebuilt.
- Verification results: **PASS, on measurements that now mean something.** REAL max pitch **90.0 degrees** (requirement was 76.6+); front wheel genuinely airborne **95 of 100** hold steps; front **collider** ground clearance **1.706 m**; front **visible wheel** clearance **1.699 m**; **roll 0.0 degrees for the entire lift** (no turning, per "keep it straight"); recovers to 2.4 degrees pitch / 2.1 degrees roll within 2s of release; wheelie initiated from **2.8 km/h**. `MINI-064/065 VALIDATION PASS`, `MINI-001 STATIC VALIDATION PASS`.
- Known issues (honest):
  - The lift is now **directly driven, not simulated** - during a wheelie the bike's attitude is authored rather than emergent. This is exactly what the user asked for ("invisible hydraulic", "arbitrarily lift", "keep it straight") and is a normal arcade technique, but it does mean the wheelie will not react to bumps/slopes the way a torque-driven one would. Landing and everything outside the wheelie remain full physics.
  - `CurrentPitchAngle` is `asin`-based and therefore saturates at 90 degrees, so the test cannot verify angles beyond 90 even though the mechanic can now drive there. If the user runs the slider past 90 toward 360, the test's reported pitch will read 90 - that is a measurement ceiling, not a behaviour ceiling.
  - Still not verified in real Play Mode by a human - but unlike every previous round, the automated claim is now grounded in real Rigidbody pitch and real measured wheel clearance rather than a self-assigned number.
- Next action: user tests in Play Mode - hold E from a crawl and confirm the front genuinely lifts and stays straight, then use the Max wheelie angle slider (now 0-360) to set the height they actually want.

### MINI-066 — rider on the bike: purchased mocap, runtime IK, seats, and a test rig

- Date: 2026-08-18
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User, after the wheelie finally worked: "put it into the upizup mini game and then try to get the animations with the character on the bike and the other one hopping behind him... i want the best animations like gta". Then across several messages: buy/install the Animo mocap pack, "make sure the wheel is invisible before you transfer", "remember bike entry and car entry is different okay", a full hand-written animation mapping read off the vendor's preview video, "can you make me test it in the bike test mode", "put a hide button", camera "do NOT go back to the original view... i want to take screenshots", and finally the Play Mode findings: "the rider is too high and the bending is too hard... i can see the front wheel so make it invisible as well".
- Research before building (the blocker was real): Mixamo has **no** motorcycle riding animations - multiple sources confirm people fall back to bicycle clips and get pedalling legs. The user bought [Animo Mocap Motorcycle Riding](https://www.renderhub.com/animo/motorcycle-riding) ($4.99, 30 humanoid FBX clips) instead. Unity's Animation Rigging package (already installed, 1.4.1) supplies the IK the pack cannot: no generic pack knows where THIS bike's bars and pegs are. That split - clip supplies body attitude, IK pins the four contact points - is exactly what the brief's MINI-066 already specified.
- Implementation:
  - **Pack import.** All 6 FBX shipped as **Generic**, which cannot retarget onto this project's Humanoid characters (all 9 converted back in MINI-016). `Mini066MotoClipHumanoid` reimports them as Humanoid and *verifies the avatars are actually valid* rather than assuming - all 6 passed, 30/30 clips retargetable.
  - **Clip identification was done by the user, not by me, and the names are actively misleading.** I first mapped clips from their filenames and got it wrong. The user watched the vendor's preview video and supplied the real mapping - most importantly that **`cheer01` is the WHEELIE**, not a celebration, and that MOTOIdle01 is the mount/stopped pose while MOTOIdle02 is the riding pose. Every point they made was then **confirmed numerically** by measuring torso angles straight out of the FBX skeletons in Blender (`moto_measure.py`): riding -59.8deg from vertical, stopped -38.8deg, `cheer01` **+1.7deg** (bolt upright - a 61-degree swing nothing else in the pack comes near, exactly a wheelie), Left02/Right02 a symmetric +/-6.6deg lateral lean with pitch unchanged. Visual rendering was attempted first and abandoned: these FBX carry a skeleton with no mesh, Blender does not render bones, and a stick-figure rebuild was too ambiguous to judge - numbers were the better tool.
  - **Seats.** New `VehicleSeat` (role + **EntryStyle**, anchor, per-seat IK targets) and `VehicleRider` (mount/dismount, builds a `TwoBoneIKConstraint` rig at runtime from the character's own Humanoid avatar, so it works for any character with no per-character wiring). `EntryStyle` exists specifically because the user flagged that **bike and car entry differ** - a bike is straddled, a car is entered through a door; cars get `DoorSeated` later without reshaping the rider.
  - **Driver + pillion.** Pillion anchors (seat, grab rails, pegs) added to the prefab - a TMAX has a real pillion seat, so "the other guy hopping on behind" is a genuine second seat, not the driver position reused. Pillion holds grab rails, not handlebars it cannot reach, which is why hand targets are per-seat.
  - **Control scheme, set by the user:** F on/off, W/S throttle, A/D lean, R wheelie, Q/E look, Space brake. E is deliberately no longer the wheelie - it is the on-foot interact key everywhere else, so it collided with getting on.
  - **Test rig.** The bike previously only existed after buying it from the dealer, making the rider impossible to iterate on. `BikeTestAutoMount` seats the real player model on the bike 0.25s into Play in the isolated `TMAX_Physics_Test` scene, going through `BikeInteractable.Interact` - the real mount path, not a test-only shortcut that could pass while the real one is broken. `TmaxTestInput` now stands aside while a rider is mounted so the two input paths don't fight.
  - **Camera.** `TmaxTestFollowCam` gained mouse orbit + zoom, and - per the user's explicit request - **never auto-returns** once they have moved it, because an auto-return swings the shot away exactly when a screenshot is being taken. `C` resets to chase deliberately.
  - **Play Mode fixes from the user's screenshots** (all four real bugs, none of which the automated checks could have caught):
    1. *Rider floating ~1.6m above the bike.* Root cause measured, not guessed: the seated clip already carries its hips **0.846m above its own root**, and the seat anchor was ALSO at seat height (~0.74m), stacking the two. Anchor moved to near ground level so the clip's own hip height supplies the seat height.
    2. *"Bending is too hard"* - the stock riding pose is a -59.8deg racing crouch. Seat anchor now pitches the rider back 18deg, softening it toward the upright end without stealing MOTOIdle01 (which is needed as the stopped/mount pose).
    3. *Front black wheel disc visible.* Now built invisible, same as the rear.
    4. *Overlay text drawing on top of itself* - two hidden-hint labels both at y=10, an unreadable smear in the screenshots. Restacked.
  - **Overlay hide toggle:** `H` hides the controls box and the speed/wheelie readout together (shared state so one key does both); the tuner keeps its own `T` since it is often wanted while readouts are hidden. A one-line hint stays visible so the key can't be lost.
- Files changed: `Mini064TmaxAssetPrep.cs` (pillion/seat anchors, rider seat height+pitch constants, both discs invisible, `CreateMarker` returns the transform), `Mini065TmaxPhysicsTest.cs` (`WireSeat`, test rider), `Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs` (8 riding actions + `LoadNamedClip` + public shared-entry accessor), new `VehicleSeat.cs`, `VehicleRider.cs`, `BikeInteractable.cs`, `BikeRiderAnimation.cs`, `BikeTestAutoMount.cs`, `TmaxTestOverlay.cs`, new `Mini066MotoClipInspector.cs`, `Mini066MotoClipHumanoid.cs`, updated `TmaxTestFollowCam.cs`, `TmaxTestInput.cs`, `TmaxWheelieTuner.cs`, plus prefab/scenes rebuilt and 6 FBX added under `Art/Animations/Moto/`.
- Verification results: compile clean throughout. Both wheel discs confirmed `m_Enabled: 0` **by parsing the built prefab**, not by trusting the code. All 8 riding states baked into the shared Animator controller; all 4 action ids present on 12 characters in `GrandBayProof`. Physics unaffected by the rider work: `Mini065TmaxDropTest` **PASS** (90.0deg real pitch, front wheel airborne 95/100 steps, roll 2.1deg). `MINI-064/065 VALIDATION PASS`, `MINI-001 STATIC VALIDATION PASS`.
- Known issues (honest):
  - **The seat height and back-lean values are a first correction, not a verified fit.** The height is derived from a real measurement so it should be close, but "close" and "looks right" are different things - this needs another Play Mode look, and both are single constants (`RiderSeatHeight`, `RiderSeatPitchBack`) to re-tune.
  - **Transition clips are not sequenced yet.** The pack ships enter/hold/exit triples (`MOTOIdle02_Left02` -> `Left02_Loop` -> `Left02_MOTOIdle02`) and only the loops are wired, so leans/wheelie snap between poses. This is the single biggest remaining gap between "working" and the GTA-like feel the user asked for.
  - **The pillion is built but untested** - seats, anchors and pose are wired, but nobody has ridden on the back yet. Driver first was the user's own sequencing.
  - **The pack has no mount clip in the sense I first assumed.** `Idle_MOTOIdle01`/`MOTOIdle01_Idle` (5.33s/6.33s, far longer than the 0.33-2s seated transitions) are being used as mount/dismount; the user identified these from the video and they measure consistently, but they have not been watched in motion in-engine.
  - Riding IK reach depends on the character's arm length - it is built from Sacat's proportions and has not been checked on Franki or any NPC.
- Next action: user re-tests in `TMAX_Physics_Test` and reports whether seat height and lean now look right; then transition-clip sequencing, then the pillion.

#### MINI-066 follow-up — the hand IK was never running at all

- Request: User after the second Play Mode test: "the riding starting position is excellent and riding is very good, the bending is a problem it bends too much even the hands come off the handlebar and the wheeling goes too much back... we can keep the character hand anchored at the handlebar area."
- **The hands coming off the bars was not tuning - the IK had never executed once.** Two compounding faults, both mine:
  1. The rig was built with Animation Rigging (`Rig` + `RigBuilder` + `TwoBoneIKConstraint`) constructed **at runtime, after Awake**. By then the Animator's playable graph is already initialised, and newly-added rig layers do not reliably take effect. Replaced with Unity's built-in humanoid goal IK (`OnAnimatorIK` + `AvatarIKGoal`), which needs no graph rebuilding and runs after the animation every frame - the thing AvatarIKGoal exists for.
  2. Even correct IK code would have done nothing, because **`OnAnimatorIK` is only called on Animator layers with IK Pass enabled**, and `HumanoidAnimationLayerBuilder` never set it. Now set on every action layer it creates, *and* patched onto already-existing layers (the early-return path meant a controller baked before this change would silently keep the old flag - the exact class of bug that hides for another whole round).
- **"Bends too much"**: seat back-lean 18 -> 30deg, landing the clip's -59.8deg crouch near -30deg (upright cruiser rather than racing tuck, which suits a maxi-scooter). This is only safe *because* the hands are now independently pinned - previously leaning the body back would have dragged them off the bars, which is likely why both symptoms appeared together.
- **"Wheeling goes too much back"**: the rider is parented to the bike, so a 90deg wheelie carries them 90deg back on its own, and the wheelie clip leans them back further still - the two compounded. `VehicleRider.SetExtraPitch` now leans the rider FORWARD by a fraction of the bike's live pitch (`wheelieCounterLean`, default 0.55), which is also what a real rider does to counterbalance.
- Verification: compile clean. **IK Pass confirmed on 2 layers by parsing the built controller** (`m_IKPass: 1` x2, base locomotion layer correctly left at 0) rather than trusting the code path. **Seat rotation confirmed baked in the prefab** as quaternion x=-0.2588/w=0.9659 = exactly -30deg, on both driver and pillion, with ground-level y=0.02 preserved. Physics unchanged: drop test **PASS** (90.0deg real pitch, 95/100 airborne steps, 2.1deg roll). Both validations PASS.
- Known issues: the three values (`RiderSeatPitchBack`, `wheelieCounterLean`, and the IK weights on `VehicleRider`) are reasoned first attempts, not verified fits - all are single tunables. None of this has been seen in motion; the IK in particular has now been *made to run* but has never been *watched working*.

#### MINI-066 follow-up 2 — rider/bike lock, arm posture, and the in-game chase camera

- Request: User, after the IK started working: "for the wheelie, can you make the character move in the same angle of the bike at the same speed and then for the regular riding can you just make his arms drop slightly slightly. then can you not make the camera in the main game not be like that when riding" - clarified as "the camera should be at the back of the bike in the real game" and "i dont want them to see the flaws in the wheelieing".
- Implementation:
  - **`wheelieCounterLean` 0.72 -> 0.** The counter-lean added in the previous round was solving the wrong problem: any non-zero value rotates the rider AGAINST the bike, and at a 90-degree wheelie that read as the rider peeling off the back rather than riding it (visible in the user's screenshot). At zero the rider is carried by the seat transform alone - exactly the bike's angle, exactly its rate, no lag, which is what was asked for. Field kept rather than deleted, since the earlier "goes too much back" complaint could return if the wheelie's maximum angle is ever retuned.
  - **Arms relaxed**: handlebar IK targets lowered 6cm (`HandlebarDropForArms`). Moving the TARGET rather than weakening the IK weight is the right lever - the hands stay pinned exactly as firmly, just lower, so the elbows relax instead of the grip loosening. Verified in the built prefab: bar Y went 1.0112 -> 0.9512.
  - **In-game chase camera.** Confirmed first that the test camera had never leaked into `GrandBayProof` (it uses `ThirdPersonFollowCamera`, aimed at the player). The real problem: the rider is parented to the bike, so a camera following either of them pitches up to 90 degrees during a wheelie and rolls onto its side. New `BikeCameraAnchor` tracks the bike's **position and heading only, always level**; `BikeInteractable` retargets the game camera to it on mount and back to the character on dismount. This also frames the wheelie from behind rather than side-on, which is the angle its remaining rough edges show from - directly serving "i dont want them to see the flaws in the wheelieing".
    - Subtle correctness point: the anchor derives heading from the **flattened forward vector**, not `eulerAngles.y`. Past vertical the Euler decomposition flips and yaw jumps 180 degrees, which would whip the camera around behind the player mid-wheelie.
  - **Lean softened** (from the same round): steering smoothed over 0.35s before the lean pose is chosen (keyboard input is a hard 0/1 and was snapping the pose on every tap), lean crossfade lengthened to 0.45s, and 3.5deg of counter-roll trims the clip's built-in +/-6.6deg without re-authoring it.
  - **Wheelie hand grip**: the wheelie pose is the pack's `cheer01`, which lifts an arm clear of the bars - which is why one hand specifically drifted off. Hand IK is boosted x1.6 while wheelieing only.
- Verification: one real compile error caught and fixed (`ThirdPersonFollowCamera` lives in `UpIzUpMini.Cameras`, not the Vehicles namespace). Handlebar drop and IK Pass both confirmed **by parsing the built assets**, not assumed. Physics unchanged: drop test **PASS** (90.0deg real pitch, 95/100 airborne steps, 2.1deg roll). Both validations PASS.
- Known issues: `leanInputSmoothing`, `leanBlendSeconds`, `leanTrimDegrees`, `wheelieHandGrip` and the camera anchor's `heightOffset`/`yawSmoothing` are all reasoned first values, unverified in motion. The x1.6 hand grip is deliberately forceful and may over-stretch the arm; drop toward 1.2 if so. The in-game camera retarget has never been exercised - the test scene uses its own camera, so this path only runs in `GrandBayProof` after actually buying the bike.

### MINI-066 follow-up 3 — keyframed rider seating, and driving the editor directly

- Date: 2026-08-18
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User, after several rounds of screenshots: "i realize the animation is not in sync so can you do like key framing. like when the character sits on the bike you key frame and then during the wheelie you can put the character forward." Agreed explicitly before implementing: nudge the idle pose slightly forward, keyframe it, move forward as the wheelie rises, and reverse on the way down.
- **Took direct control of the Editor this round.** The user granted screen access after noting "you not being able to test visually is an issue". This immediately paid for itself: it caught that the mount genuinely works (TestRider parented under Seat), that the wheelie really does reach 70.7deg real pitch with the front wheel off the ground in live Play Mode (not just in the headless harness), that Play/pause/stop cycles cleanly, and a stale on-screen label still reading "E held" after the wheelie key moved to R. It also ended several rounds of me reasoning about rotations I could not see - the counter-lean sign error had already cost two full test cycles.
- **Root cause of the rider never sitting right, finally identified:** the purchased mocap clip carries its own root placement, authored against whatever bike the mocap studio used - not this one. Parenting the rider to the seat anchor at zero offset therefore drops the body wherever that studio put it, which on the TMAX lands him behind and off the seat. Every previous attempt (seat rotation, counter-lean, body-lock-to-handlebars) was trying to fix a POSITIONAL mismatch with rotations and IK stretching, which cannot work - and each one broke the seating in a new way.
- Implementation:
  - **Two authored keyframes on `VehicleRider`**, blended by how deep the wheelie is: `seatedOffset`/`seatedPitch` for normal riding, `wheelieOffset`/`wheeliePitch` for a full wheelie. Starting values: seated +0.06m forward; wheelie +0.28m forward, +0.10m up, +22deg pitch onto the bars.
  - **The reverse needs no separate code.** `BikeInteractable` feeds `SetWheelieBlend` the bike's LIVE wheelie fraction, so coming down simply runs the same interpolation backward. This is deliberately different from the earlier independent-rotation approach, which could and did drift out of sync with the bike.
  - Eased over `poseBlendSeconds` (0.25s) so the rider shifts his weight rather than snapping between poses.
  - **All six keyframe values exposed as live sliders** in the wheelie tuner (shown only while someone is mounted), plus a live 0-1 blend readout - so the seating can be dialled in by eye during an actual wheelie instead of another guess/rebuild/screenshot cycle.
  - Reverted two things from the previous round that the user's screenshots proved harmful: `bodyLockWeight` 1 -> 0 (it dragged the torso toward the bars and left him reclined off the seat - the hand IK already held the grips on its own) and `wheelieCounterLean` 0.8 -> 0 (rotating him against the bike made his body swing back like a pendulum off his pinned hands, which is exactly the "character is back" wheelie screenshot).
- Verification: compile clean. Keyframes confirmed baked into the rebuilt scene by parsing it (`seatedOffset {0,0,0.06}`, `wheelieOffset {0,0.1,0.28}`, `wheeliePitch 22`, `poseBlendSeconds 0.25`). Bike physics **untouched and re-proven** after the user asked directly whether the wheelie had been reverted: `enableWheelie 1`, `maxWheelieAngle 90`, `wheelieRiseRate 60`, `wheelieHydraulicStrength 9`, `wheelieRearTorqueBoost 900` all unchanged, and the drop test still **PASS** (90.0deg real pitch, 95/100 airborne steps, 2.1deg roll). Both validations PASS.
- Known issues (honest):
  - The six keyframe values are a starting point chosen by reasoning, not a verified fit - they exist as sliders precisely because they need to be set by eye. This has NOT yet been seen in motion.
  - Only the BODY is keyframed; the hands remain pinned by IK, so pushing the rider too far forward will bunch the arms rather than break the grip. That is the signal the forward offset has overshot.
  - The pillion still has no keyframes of its own and will inherit the same mocap-root mismatch when someone finally rides on the back.
  - Driving the Editor directly works but is slow, and the test scene's obstacle course makes repeat wheelie runs awkward (the bike hits boxes or launches off the ramp). A clear straight run would make visual iteration much faster.
- Next action: user (or Claude, with control) opens the tuner mid-wheelie and drags the six RIDER SEATING sliders until the seating looks right, then reports the values so they can be baked in as defaults.

### MINI-066 follow-up 4 — tuned seating baked in, stop/rest/pull-away sequence, bigger bike, EXE

- Date: 2026-08-19
- Owner: Claude
- Request: User, after dialling the sliders in Play Mode: "thats it so now we can transfer this to the other game and then make sure both character are the same scale so they can equally ride the bike... when the bike is at stop after driving need you to put the motoidle02 motoidle01 and then motoidle01 idle animation if the driver stays at stop and then motoidle01 motoidle02 will come back on the bike and then once this is done document and build exe... we will have to [make] skills to build systems for later or other games from this one."
- **Tuned values baked as defaults** (read off the user's own Play Mode screenshots): `seatedOffset (0.11, -0.01, 0.32)`, `wheelieOffset (0, -0.40, 0.28)`, `wheeliePitch 22`. Confirmed in the rebuilt scene by parsing it, not assumed.
- **A real process failure worth recording:** the user had these values dialled in live, and an unrelated file edit of mine triggered a Unity recompile that ended Play Mode and wiped them. Reverting the edit did not help - Unity reimports on file TIMESTAMP, not content. The values only survived because the user had screenshotted them. Fixed properly by adding a **"PRINT these values to Console"** button to the tuner: Console output survives Stop and recompiles, and prints the numbers pre-formatted as C# ready to paste in as defaults. Never tune live values again without a way to persist them.
- **Stop / rest / pull-away sequence**, from the user's own reading of the pack's preview video. The pack's `<from>_<to>` naming makes these exact:
  - coming to rest -> `AA_MOTO_MOTOIdle02_MOTOIdle01` (settle from riding onto the stopped pose)
  - still sitting after `restAfterSeconds` (2.5s) -> `AA_MOTO_MOTOIdle01_Idle` (relax; the clip does not loop so Mecanim holds its last frame, which is exactly the "still sat there" look)
  - pulling away -> `AA_MOTO_MOTOIdle01_MOTOIdle02`
  These fire on EDGES (moving<->stopped) rather than being held, because each is a transition BETWEEN poses - holding one would freeze the rider mid-move. Suppressed entirely while wheelieing so a wheelie at low speed cannot trigger the stop settle.
- **Bike scaled 1.15x** on the prefab ROOT, so mesh, both WheelColliders, the body collider and every measured marker scale together (scaling only the mesh would leave the physics wheels inside a larger-looking bike). **The rider does NOT inherit it** - he is a child of the seat, so `VehicleRider.Mount` divides out the parent's scale to keep him at the size the game already has him, restoring it on dismount.
  - The validation harness correctly FAILED on this (mesh 2.524m vs the 2.195m it expected) and was fixed by deriving the expectation as `2.195 x BikeScale` rather than hardcoding the new figure - so it still catches an *accidental* scale change instead of rubber-stamping whatever the asset happens to be.
- **Character scale, measured rather than guessed** (Blender, both FBX): Sacat/`Mainchar` **1.827m**, Franki/`Strong` **1.767m** - 6cm apart, 3.3%. Deliberately left different: that is a realistic human difference, Franki being stockier is a story trait, and the hand/foot IK adapts reach automatically so both ride the same seating. Flagged to the user rather than silently normalised.
- Files changed: `VehicleRider.cs` (baked keyframes, scale compensation), `BikeRiderAnimation.cs` (stop/rest/pull-away state machine + `restAfterSeconds`), `Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs` (3 new action ids + clip entries), `TmaxWheelieTuner.cs` (print-values button, wider forward range, SIDE sliders, taller panel), `Mini064TmaxAssetPrep.cs` (`BikeScale`), `Mini065TmaxValidation.cs` (scale-aware length check), prefab + both scenes rebuilt.
- Verification: compile clean. All 3 new states (`BikeStopSettle`, `BikeRest`, `BikePullAway`) confirmed baked into the shared Animator controller; no missing-clip warnings. Seating values confirmed in the scene. Bike root scale confirmed `1.15`. Drop test **PASS** (90.0deg real pitch, 95/100 airborne steps, 1.7deg roll). `MINI-064/065 VALIDATION PASS`, `MINI-001 STATIC VALIDATION PASS`.
- Known issues (honest):
  - **`seatedPitch` is still 0** - the user's screenshot cut off before that slider, so it was never captured. Everything else came from their real tuned values.
  - The user's tuned wheelie **Up sat exactly at the slider's minimum (-0.40)**, which means the range may have been *blocking* them from going lower. Worth extending if they want him lower still.
  - The stop sequence has **not been seen in motion** - it is wired, baked and compiles, but no one has watched the settle/rest/pull-away actually play.
  - The pillion is still built-but-unridden, and inherits the same mocap-root mismatch the driver needed keyframes to solve.
- **Reusable pieces, for the vehicle skill the user wants later:** `VehicleSeat` (role + `EntryStyle`, so bike-straddle vs car-door entry differ without special-casing), `VehicleRider` (runtime humanoid `OnAnimatorIK` goals + keyframed seating + parent-scale compensation), `BikeRiderAnimation` (bike state -> pose state machine), and the tuner pattern itself (live sliders + a print-to-Console button). Adding a second bike should mean: measure the mesh, place the anchors, tune two keyframes. That is the shape the skill should encode.
  - **Post-build fix, found by the user immediately: the bought bike could not be ridden at all.** `BikeInteractable` was only ever added in `Mini065TmaxPhysicsTest.BuildTestScene`, i.e. onto the test-scene bike, never onto the PREFAB. So the bike `VehicleSpawnController` spawns after purchase in the real game had no interactable on it - visible, driveable-looking, but with no [F] prompt and no way to mount ("i cant interact and hop the bike in the game"). Moved to `WireController` so it lands on the prefab itself and every instance - spawned, placed, or future - is rideable. A placed test bike near the safehouse was also added and then removed at the user's request ("leave it where i bought it thats fine") - purchase remains the only way to get one.
  - **Three smoothness/attachment bugs found once the user tested in the real GAME rather than the test scene** (their own conclusion: "i think we must test in the game from now on" - agreed and worth honouring, the test scene had been hiding these):
    1. **Stutter.** The bike's Rigidbody had `m_Interpolate: 0`. Physics runs at a fixed 50Hz while rendering runs at display rate, so with interpolation off the bike's visible transform only moved on physics steps and visibly juddered between them - amplified by a chase camera, and inherited by the rider parented to it. Set to `Interpolate`, plus `ContinuousDynamic` collision (a 220kg body at 60km/h covers ~0.33m per physics step, enough to tunnel through the road).
    2. **Camera judder from a script-order race.** `BikeCameraAnchor` updated in `LateUpdate` and `ThirdPersonFollowCamera` reads it in *its* `LateUpdate`; Unity gives no ordering guarantee between two LateUpdates, so the camera could follow last frame's anchor. Moved the anchor to `Update`, which is reliably before every LateUpdate and after Rigidbody interpolation has been applied.
    3. **The rider visibly came apart from the bike when cornering** ("the character leans off the bike and the legs and hands come out of the bike"). Root cause found by dumping the prefab hierarchy: `Seat` is a **SIBLING** of `VisualLeanRoot`, not a child - so the bike BODY leaned up to 40deg into a corner while the rider stayed bolt upright. Fixed by exposing `CurrentVisualLean` and rolling the rider with it (`riderLeanFollow`, default 1). Deliberately NOT fixed by reparenting `Seat` under `VisualLeanRoot`: that anchor is what every IK target and seating keyframe is measured against, and putting it under a cosmetically-rotating transform would tilt all of those measurements too. Lean also reduced 40 -> 16deg per "lessen on the bending significantly".
- Next action: user plays the EXE, checks the stop/rest/pull-away sequence and the 1.15x bike, and reports `seatedPitch` if the seating needs it.

### MINI-066 follow-up 5 — the wheelie pose becomes a partial blend (fixing "the character angles further back")

- Date: 2026-08-19
- Owner: released
- Request: "i cant seem to get a perfect sync for the wheelie as the character angles further back when wheelieing", then agreement to the partial-blend approach ("ok this yes the partial idea sounds good").
- Root cause (a structural conflict, not a tuning shortfall): the pack's wheelie pose is `cheer01`, measured in Blender at **+1.7deg torso from vertical vs -59.8deg for the riding pose**. Playing it outright therefore imposes a ~61deg back-lean that is *authored into the clip* and answers to no numeric offset. `wheeliePitch`, `wheelieAngleSync` and `wheelieCounterLean` were all fighting a constant they could not reach, which is why "perfect sync" was unobtainable rather than merely fiddly: the rider's angle had two owners, only one of which was tunable.
- Why layer weight on `FullBodyOverride` was NOT the fix (the first approach I started and abandoned): the poses are crossfaded *within* that one layer, so lowering its weight blends the held pose towards the **base locomotion layer** - a standing idle - not towards the riding pose. A half-weight wheelie would have mixed in standing legs, i.e. feet off the pegs. This is worth recording because it looks like the obvious one-line fix and is wrong.
- Implementation: a **second full-body override layer, `FullBodyBlend`, stacked above `FullBodyOverride`**. The riding pose is now held at full weight underneath *through the whole wheelie*, and the wheelie pose plays on the layer above at `wheelieClipWeight`. Weight `w` is then a genuine riding-pose -> wheelie-pose blend, so the bike's live angle plus the tuner's pitch offset keep authority over how far back the rider actually goes, while the authored pose still contributes its character.
  - `HumanoidAnimationManager`: new `FullBodyBlendLayerName`, plus `PlayBlendedPose` / `SetBlendedWeight` / `BlendedWeight` / `StopBlendedPose`. `BeginSustainedAction` also gained an optional `weight` and a `SetSustainedWeight`, so a held pose can be retuned live without restarting. `EndSustainedAction` drops the overlay too, so a dismount mid-wheelie cannot leave the pose stuck on.
  - Weight is ramped frame-by-frame in `BikeRiderAnimation.UpdateWheelieOverlay` rather than by a fade coroutine - deliberately, so the tuner slider retunes it *mid-wheelie* without the clip restarting. Same reasoning as the earlier setpoint-vs-measurement lesson: a value you cannot observe changing under load is a value you cannot tune.
  - `HumanoidAnimationLayerBuilder` mirrors every `fullBody` action onto the new layer (a state referencing the same clip, weight 0 unless asked for), so any pose can be used as a partial overlay later without another controller rebuild. Created *after* `FullBodyOverride` so it sits higher in the stack - order matters and is now asserted.
  - New tuner slider `WHEELIE pose blend (0=ride pose)`, default 0.45, and it is included in the PRINT-to-Console dump.
- Also fixed: `BikeRiderAnimation`'s doc comment claimed "Mini066RiderValidation checks the two lists agree". **No such file existed** - the cross-check was never written. Rather than delete the claim, the check now exists for real in `Mini065TmaxValidation`: every pose id the runtime asks for must have a state on `FullBodyOverride`, the wheelie pose must have one on `FullBodyBlend` (or the new slider would silently do nothing), and `FullBodyBlend` must sit above `FullBodyOverride`.
- Files changed: `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Character/HumanoidAnimationManager.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/BikeRiderAnimation.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Scripts/Vehicles/TmaxWheelieTuner.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/HumanoidAnimationLayerBuilder.cs`, `Assets/UpIzUpMini/Editor/Mini065TmaxValidation.cs`.
- Scene/prefab changes: `StarterAssetsThirdPerson.controller` gained the `FullBodyBlend` layer (index 3, above `FullBodyOverride` at 2) with `BikeWheelie` mirrored onto it; `TMAX_560.prefab`, `TMAX_Physics_Test.unity` and `GrandBayProof.unity` rebuilt by the pipeline.
- Verification commands (Editor closed first - batch mode aborts if it holds the project):
  - `-executeMethod UpIzUpMini.EditorTools.Mini064TmaxAssetPrep.BuildPrefab`
  - `-executeMethod UpIzUpMini.EditorTools.Mini065TmaxPhysicsTest.WireController`
  - `-executeMethod UpIzUpMini.EditorTools.Mini065TmaxPhysicsTest.BuildTestScene`
  - `-executeMethod UpIzUpMini.EditorTools.Mini011PhaseBSetup.BuildScene`
  - `-executeMethod UpIzUpMini.EditorTools.Mini065TmaxValidation.Validate`
  - `-executeMethod UpIzUpMini.EditorTools.Mini065TmaxDropTest.Run`
- Verification results: all exit 0. Validation **PASS**, including the new layer/state checks (confirmed non-vacuous by grepping the controller asset: `FullBodyBlend` present at index 3, `BikeWheelie` state count 2). Drop test **PASS** - REAL max pitch **90.0deg**, front collider clearance 1.962m, front visual wheel 1.954m, peak roll 1.7deg and fully recovered, pitch back to 1.8deg after release. No EXE built (standing instruction).
- **Process note worth keeping: `Mini064TmaxAssetPrep.BuildPrefab` reports "BUILD OK" while producing a prefab with ZERO MonoBehaviours.** It only builds the physics hierarchy; `Mini065TmaxPhysicsTest.WireController` is what attaches `TmaxBikeController`/`TmaxWheelVisuals`/seats. Running the prefab build alone therefore *silently strips the controller*, and the next validation fails with the misleading "expected a TmaxBikeController on the root". The four steps must run in the order listed above. This cost a debugging cycle this pass.
- Known issues:
  - The blend default (0.45) is a starting estimate, not a measured value - the point of the change is that the user can now actually dial it. Expect to bake whatever they land on.
  - `seatedPitch` is **still 0** - never captured, across five follow-ups now.
  - The stop/rest/pull-away sequence has still never been watched in motion.
  - Not verified in Play Mode by me: Play Mode entry via `-executeMethod` remains broken in this environment (Search-module init exception, documented since MINI-001). The drop test exercises real PhysX in Edit Mode, but the *animation blend itself* needs the user's eyes on a running frame - the layer/state wiring is proven, the visual result is not.
- Next action: user presses Play in `GrandBayProof.unity`, mounts the bike by the safehouse, holds R, and works the `WHEELIE pose blend` slider under T from 0 upward. 0 = pure riding pose driven only by the bike's angle (should now track the throttle exactly, both up and down); raise it until the pose reads well without reintroducing the back-lean. PRINT before stopping.


### MINI-067 / MINI-068 — the "Gucci Law" 18k chain, and the bike's home spot

- Date: 2026-08-19
- Owner: released (chain placement handed to the user for hand-tuning)
- Request: "i want to put the gold chain on the character's chest and the boss man c chest as well. i have created the model in E:\Assets gold chain 18k i want it to be sold for $6000"; then "once i have bought the bike it should be in my safe house exact where you have it even every respawn. so if i forget it anywhere i will see it there"; then "make it larger and it is suppose to be arround his neck... i want you to call it Gucci Law"; then "the back of the chain should be at the back of the neck like nap area"; finally "can i put it for myself and then you just record its position."

**MINI-067 — the chain**

- **The source model was not usable as delivered: 1,995,768 triangles and two 8192x8192 textures, ~60MB, for a necklace.** On a mobile-first target that is indefensible, so it is welded, decimated to 8,000 tris and its maps resized to 1024 in headless Blender before Unity ever sees it - 60MB to 2.0MB. `Mini067GoldChainPrep` then builds the prefab, scaling from the MEASURED renderer bounds (the model authors ~0.75m wide, roughly a whole torso) rather than a hardcoded multiplier that a re-export would silently invalidate.
- Priced at 6000 and renamed **"Gucci Law"**. The item ID stays `chain_gold` - a display rename must not strip a 6000-dollar purchase off an existing save.
- The chain was already a placeholder ring of spheres in `CharacterEquipment`, and Boss C already wore hand-built bead strands. Both now use the real model; both keep their generated version as a fallback so a missing prefab leaves a character wearing something rather than nothing.
- `CharacterEquipment` gained an `alwaysEquipped` list. Boss C wears his unconditionally because it is character, not a purchase - selling yours must not strip his.
- **The bone-axis trap, hit again.** `PositionOnBone` placed the chain at `(0, 0.14, 0.08)` in CHEST-BONE local space, and `AccessorySwing` forced the accessory to the bone's rotation outright. This rig's bone rest orientations are not world-aligned - already documented on Boss C's necklace, where trusting the neck bone's axes hung the chain down by the hip. A symmetric ring of spheres hid it; a real modelled chain does not. Placement is now built in the CHARACTER's frame, and `AccessorySwing.Initialize` takes an optional world rotation whose delta is captured once and re-applied every frame, so the accessory still follows the bone but from the correct orientation.
- **Verified by rendering and looking, not by assertion** - and the render harness itself cost most of the effort, through three false conclusions worth recording:
  1. Freshly instantiated renderers are not in the culling data, so `Camera.Render` skips them. This produced pixel-identical images at wildly different offsets and read as a placement bug. Fixed by saving to a throwaway scene and re-opening it.
  2. The camera aimed at a guessed 1.42m above the character root while the chest bone was at 1.22m - enough to push the chain to the very bottom edge of frame and look like "not rendering".
  3. The companion spawns right beside the player, so a chest-height camera one metre out sits inside HER. Other characters are now hidden before each shot.
- Renders confirmed on Boss C (baked, in-scene) and on the player. The remaining question - exact fit - is now the user's own call, see below.

**MINI-068 — the bike's home spot**

- `BikeHomePoint` is left in the scene at the safehouse parking pose, wired into `VehicleSpawnController`, and `SaveLoadSystem` calls `ReturnBikeHome()` on load.
- **This deliberately reverses the save-the-last-position behaviour added two tasks ago**, at the user's explicit request and for their stated reason. `bikePosition`/`bikeEuler` are still WRITTEN to the save, so "park it where you leave it" can be restored without a save-format change.
- Purchasing now REUSES the bike already in the world rather than instantiating a second one - two TMAXes would both answer `ReturnBikeHome` and end up stacked on the same spot. Validation asserts exactly one exists.
- Files changed: `Scripts/Character/CharacterEquipment.cs`, `Scripts/Character/AccessorySwing.cs`, `Scripts/Vehicles/VehicleSpawnController.cs`, `Scripts/SaveLoadSystem.cs`, `Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`, `Data/Shop/chain_gold.asset`; new `Editor/Mini067GoldChainPrep.cs`, `Editor/Mini067ChainRender.cs`, `Editor/Mini067ChainValidation.cs`, `Editor/Mini067ChainTuning.cs`; new asset `Art/Accessories/GoldChain18k_clean.glb` + `GoldChain18k.prefab`.
- Verification: `Mini067ChainValidation.Validate` **PASS** - cleaned/textured prefab within a 20,000-tri budget (the check exists specifically to catch a re-export that skips the Blender step), priced 6000, Boss C wearing it in the built scene, `BikeHomePoint` wired, exactly one TMAX parked on it. `Mini065TmaxValidation` still **PASS**. No EXE.
- Known issues / not done:
  - **The chain's exact fit is unresolved and is now with the user.** Getting the top arc to the nape while the bottom clears a chest that curves outward is awkward because the model is a FLAT loop (2cm deep) - a constant forward offset cannot follow the body, so it needs tilt, and I was converging slowly by render sweep. `CHAIN_TUNE` is placed on Sacat's chest bone in the saved scene for hand-placement.
  - The player's chain is attached at RUNTIME on purchase, so validation cannot cover that path - only the rendered checks do.
  - `ChainSide`/`ChainTilt` are 0 pending the user's numbers.
- **Follow-up, same session — the chain was fitted by hand and the fit is now good.** The user placed it themselves after I was converging too slowly by render sweep; I read the result back with `MINI-067/2. Read Tuned Chain` and baked the player's numbers: `ChainForward 0.044`, `ChainUp 0.383`, `ChainSide -0.032`, `ChainTilt -25.6`.
- **Two real bugs surfaced only because a human moved the object**, both worth keeping:
  1. **Bone scale is inherited, so one prefab is a different size on every character.** The same chain measured 0.308m on the player and 0.068m on Boss C. `CharacterEquipment.NormaliseAccessoryScale` now forces a target width after parenting.
  2. **That fix was itself wrong the first time**: it measured `Renderer.bounds.size.x`, which is a WORLD-axis-aligned box, so on a rotated chain the X extent is the chain's *thickness*, not its width - it "corrected" Boss C to 4x. Now measured from the mesh's own local bounds times world scale, which rotation cannot fool. Same fix applied to the tuning report.
  3. `ReadTuned` used `GameObject.Find("BossChain_18k")`, which returns **Boss J's** chain (he wears one too, and always has) - measuring it against Boss C's chest reported a 4.2m forward offset. It now searches within Boss C's own hierarchy. It also no longer bails out entirely when the player's `CHAIN_TUNE` is absent, which had made a scene rebuild silently destroy the ability to read Boss C at all.
- **Boss C sits on a completely different rig** - a Blender `metarig` (spine/spine.001/spine.002), where the player is `mixamorig9`. That is why he needs his own constants rather than sharing the player's, and why his chain's local rotation reads ~-94deg while looking correct.
- **RESOLVED - both characters' placements are baked into code, not just the scene.** The user hand-placed Boss C, I read it back with the (now fixed) `MINI-067/2. Read Tuned Chain` and baked `BossChainForward 0.195`, `BossChainUp 0.356`, `BossChainTilt -6.1`. `Mini011PhaseBSetup.BuildScene` was then re-run and regenerated his chain from those constants, which is the check that mattered: the placement now survives a scene rebuild instead of living only in the saved scene. Measured width came back 0.258m against the 0.27 target, confirming the scale normalisation holds on his rig too.
- Residual, for the user's eye rather than a code change: after the regenerate, Boss C's chain reads slightly lower/larger than the object he placed by hand. The numbers are right; small differences in the bone pose at build time can shift the result. Worth a glance next time the scene is open - if it is off, nudge and re-read rather than guessing.
- **Process (user instruction, now in memory): do NOT drive their computer to click through the Unity Editor.** Give the exact menu path and let them run it. Batch-mode `-executeMethod` is still fine and preferred when the Editor is closed. This came after a long stretch of me automating Editor menus, which was slow and repeatedly blocked by other apps stealing focus.
- Boss J also wears a chain. He always did (the old bead strands came from the same call), so this is inherited behaviour rather than a regression - left as-is, flagged for the user to decide.
- Verification note: the render-check harness (`Mini067ChainRender`) is unreliable and was superseded by direct Editor inspection - keep the three traps documented above in mind before trusting it again.


### MINI-069 - Not Ah Word vs Dog Life, phone regroup, and the bike control scheme

- Date: 2026-08-19
- Owner: released
- Request: the other main character should fight any Dog Life member on sight after the Gardey Zafeh mission, recruits too; call the crew to you from the cell phone on Q; wheelie moves to E and mount/dismount to F; Chevy stands next to the recruiter. Then, mid-task: "dont forget doglife should fight back as well", "i dont need the looking around on the bike", "you have my character and the other main character punching so you can use that animation for fights even the police for now", and "also the chase feature for each as well but when rival gang remains 1 they stop".

**The fight (`FactionBrawler`, new)**

- **One component drives BOTH gangs**, with a `Side` enum, rather than an attacker class and a separate retaliator. The behaviour is symmetric - see someone from the other side, close, swing - and writing each side separately is exactly how the two drift out of step. Dog Life fighting back is then the same code with the side flipped, not a second implementation.
- Reuses the existing punch (`SimpleMeleeCombat.ActionId`) on the user's instruction, so no new animation work.
- `ReceiveHit` routes damage through whichever health component the fighter actually has - the player's crew are `CharacterVitals`, Dog Life are `NpcCombatHealth`. That one indirection is what lets a single brawler hurt either side.
- **Gated on `ProgressionManager.DogLifeRevealed`**, which is set by paying the Boat Man for the Gardey Zafeh reading - i.e. the existing flag already means "after the Gardey Zafeh mission". No new progression state was invented for this.
- **The controlled character is skipped**: he punches manually with F. Auto-swinging on the player's behalf would take the fight out of their hands, and the ask was specifically for the character you are NOT driving.
- **Chase uses two ranges, deliberately.** An enemy must come within `engageRange` (8m) to start something, but once committed the fighter pursues to `chaseRange` (22m). With a single range a chase ends the instant the target backs off one step, which does not read as a chase at all.
- **Stand-down rule**: a fighter disengages once the opposing side has `standDownWhenEnemiesLeft` (1) or fewer still standing. Applied symmetrically, so it reads as both sides breaking off rather than one gang hunting the last man down - and it stops a decided fight grinding on forever.
- Deliberately NOT merged into `CompanionCombatAssist`: that one is about police and adds full heat on every swing. A street scrap with a rival gang should not call the police on you.

**Controls**

- Wheelie `R` -> `E`, mount/dismount `F`. The look-left/look-right fields were **deleted rather than rebound** - they were declared but read by nothing, dead weight holding Q and E hostage ("i dont need the looking around on the bike").
- Mounting is handled inside `BikeInteractable` rather than through `InteractionDetector`, so `E` stays the world's general interact key for everything else.
- **F is also the punch key**, so mounting used to throw a punch on the same frame. `BikeInteractable.ConsumedMountKeyThisFrame` lets `SimpleMeleeCombat` skip that frame.
- **Trap worth remembering: changing a C# field default does NOT change an existing prefab.** The bike kept `R` after the code said `E`, because the serialized component holds whatever value it was created with - and validation caught it. The control scheme is now written onto the prefab explicitly in `Mini065TmaxPhysicsTest.WireController`.

**Phone + Chevy**

- Cell phone call moved `C` -> `Q`, and now summons every recruited member as well as the other main character, fanned out behind the player on a short arc (alternating left/right so a full crew is not one long line). Summoned members are forced back to `Follow` - otherwise someone on guard duty would be teleported in and immediately walk back to his post, making the call look broken. Teleport rather than pathing, matching the existing companion call: there is no navmesh route to rely on.
- Chevy moved from `right * 10.5` to `right * 17`, two metres past the recruiter at `right * 15`.
- Files changed: `Scripts/Vehicles/BikeInteractable.cs`, `Scripts/Combat/SimpleMeleeCombat.cs`, `Scripts/Character/CellPhoneController.cs`, `Scripts/Character/GangMemberController.cs`, `Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`, `Editor/Mini065TmaxPhysicsTest.cs`; new `Scripts/Combat/FactionBrawler.cs`, `Editor/Mini069GangWarValidation.cs`.
- Verification: `Mini069GangWarValidation` **PASS**. Regression run all **PASS**: `Mini065TmaxValidation`, `Mini058FactionsValidation`, `Mini067ChainValidation`, `Mini046CompanionCombatValidation`. Prefab re-wired, both scenes rebuilt.
- **NOT verified - the fight itself.** Who wins, whether the chase reads well, whether the stand-down looks right, and whether recruits summoned by phone land somewhere sensible are all behaviour over time; `Update()` never ticks in this batch environment (documented since MINI-001). The validation proves every fighter has the component on the correct side with sane ranges - it does not prove the brawl is any good.
- Known issues / judgement calls to revisit:
  - Damage 28 and cooldown 0.9s are estimates, not tuned. A four-strong crew against Dog Life may be lopsided.
  - `FactionBrawler` moves fighters with a plain `CharacterController.Move` toward the target - no pathfinding, so a chase can walk into a wall. Acceptable for a street brawl in the open; would need NavMesh work if fights move indoors.
  - The stand-down count includes the controlled player character, who never auto-fights. That is intentional (he is still a standing member of the side) but worth knowing if the numbers feel off.
- Next action: user plays the EXE, pays the Boat Man for the Gardey Zafeh reading, then walks the crew into the Lalay block and watches a fight - checking the chase distance, whether both sides swing, and that everyone stops when Dog Life is down to one man.


### MINI-070 - Boss C's black SUV becomes a real Range Rover

- Date: 2026-08-19
- Owner: released
- Request: "i have downloaded a high poly range rover in my downloads i want you to put it in the game as a replacement to the bossman's black vehicle do all the necessary work."
- Source: `Hi3D_Glossy Black Modern Range Rover SUV 3D Model_allparts_20260819_104442.glb` from the user's Downloads (hitem3d), 62MB.
- **Not usable as delivered, and for the third time in this project the same way: 1,984,875 triangles with two 8192x8192 textures.** Same answer as the gold chain (MINI-067) - weld, decimate and resize maps in headless Blender BEFORE Unity imports it. 62MB down to 5.6MB.
  - Budget set at **20k triangles and 2048 maps**, deliberately more generous than the chain's 8k/1024: a car occupies far more screen area than a necklace, and this one is parked where the player will walk right past it.
- `Mini070RangeRoverPrep` builds the prefab, and **measures rather than assumes**:
  - The model arrives unit-normalised (longest axis exactly 1.0), so scale is derived from the imported bounds against a real 4.95m Range Rover length. A hardcoded multiplier would have been meaningless and would silently break on any re-export.
  - **Which axis is "length" is derived, not assumed** - glTF's Z-up to Y-up conversion decides whether length lands on X or Z, and guessing wrong scales the car by its width. The prefab is normalised so its forward is always the car's nose.
  - Origin sits at the tyres and centred on the body, so placing it is just "put it where it should stand" - no per-placement height fudge.
  - One `BoxCollider`, matching the TMAX's approach. A 20k-tri mesh collider on parked set dressing is not worth the physics cost.
- Placement: `BuildBlackSuvProps` now instantiates the prefab, keeping the primitive stand-in only as a fallback if the prefab is missing. The SUV's rotation changed from `LookRotation(right)` to `LookRotation(dir)` - the old placeholder faced `right` while building its body 4.2m along local X, i.e. it was modelled sideways relative to its own forward. That quirk was not worth inheriting once a car with a real nose went in.
- Verified by **rendering it in the built scene and looking**: correct proportions beside the characters, sitting on its wheels, clear of the Land Office building, parked along the road by Boss C. `Mini070RoverRender` frames from the subject's own bounds rather than a guessed camera height - the MINI-067 chain check is on record for producing confident-looking empty photos exactly that way.
- Files changed: `Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`; new `Editor/Mini070RangeRoverPrep.cs`, `Editor/Mini070RoverValidation.cs`, `Editor/Mini070RoverRender.cs`; new asset `Art/Vehicles/RangeRover_clean.glb` + `RangeRover.prefab`.
- Verification: `Mini070RoverValidation` **PASS** - prefab within the 30k-triangle budget, textured, box-collided, no MeshCollider, and in-scene the car measures within a believable range on its longest horizontal axis and in height, with no leftover `Body`/`Cabin`/`Wheel` primitives hiding inside the real model. Regression run all **PASS**: `Mini069GangWarValidation`, `Mini067ChainValidation`, `Mini058FactionsValidation`, `Mini065TmaxValidation`.
- Known issues / scope:
  - It is **set dressing, not a driveable vehicle** - no physics, no controller, nothing to get into. The request was to replace the black vehicle, and that is what this does; making it driveable is its own task (and would want the TMAX's WheelCollider treatment).
  - The triangle budget is a judgement call, not a measured frame-rate result. Nothing in this project has been profiled on a phone yet, so 20k is a defensible guess rather than a proven ceiling.
  - The car is parked on grass rather than on the road surface, matching where the old placeholder stood.
- Next action: user plays the EXE and walks past Boss C to see the car in the gameplay camera.


### MINI-071 / MINI-072 - a driveable Range Rover, and bike camera/lean fixes

- Date: 2026-08-19
- Owner: released
- Request: "lets start working on the vehicle system with the range rover and test inside of the game. give it the price of the real latest range", then "for the TMAX 560, you can rename it to TNAX 560", then "for the bike i dont want to be able to look around the bike while riding just the third person regualar, if you try to pan is in a stop possition and the character lean/ bend still too very excessive so make it subtle like a 15 degree angle".
- Scope was agreed up front rather than assumed: the user chose **"minimal: just get in and drive"** over building the planned shared vehicle architecture, and **"walk to door, sit in"** for car entry.

**Pricing and naming**

- Range Rover priced at the real 2026 base MSRP, **$113,300** - an endgame purchase, roughly 17x the TMAX.
- Sold as **"Range Rova"**, and the bike renamed **"TNAX 560"**. Near-miss names follow the user's own standing direction on that shop list ("Fictional near-miss brand names ... so no real trademark is used"), and they confirmed it mid-task by asking for the TNAX rename themselves.
- **Display names only - item ids are unchanged** (`tmax_560`). Renaming an id would strip the vehicle off any existing save, the same reason `chain_gold` kept its id when it became "Gucci Law".
- `Mini065TmaxValidation` asserted the dealer stocked *exactly* 1 item, written when the user wanted the bike alone. Relaxed to "no more than 2", which preserves the actual intent (no flavour-only filler vehicles) instead of a count that any real addition breaks.

**The car (`CarController` + `CarInteractable`, new)**

- **Written fresh rather than generalised out of `TmaxBikeController`.** The bike carries wheelie pose driving, visual lean, upright assist and rider IK sync - none of which mean anything to a car - and dragging a car through that machinery would put the wheelie/rider tuning from MINI-064 to 066 at risk. Extracting a shared layer is worth doing once two vehicles exist and the real commonality is visible; that is a refactor toward a known shape rather than a guess, and it is what the user's "minimal" choice bought.
- Car entry is deliberately not the bike's: you must be near the **driver's door** specifically, and `VehicleSeat.DoorSeated` (added speculatively back in MINI-066) finally has a use. No door-opening animation - the project has no clip and inventing one was out of scope. The seated pose borrows the bike's upright sitting clip, which reads acceptably through glass.
- `Mini071RoverVehiclePrep` places the physics rig from the model's MEASURED bounds: wheelbase came out 3.07m against a real 2.997m, track 1.68m against ~1.70m. Suspension springs are sized from the car's own mass - a spring tuned for a 220kg bike would let a 2.5-tonne SUV sink straight through its travel.
- **The body collider deliberately starts ABOVE the wheels.** A box spanning the full height rests on the ground, the WheelColliders never take load, and the car sits there inert.

**Verification caught two real faults - both worth keeping**

1. **First drive test FAILED honestly**: the car settled level, then travelled 0.00m under full throttle. Cause: Edit Mode never calls `Awake`/`FixedUpdate`, so `Physics.Simulate` was stepping PhysX past a controller that had never initialised. Driven by reflection now, the same way the TMAX drop test does it. Without the test this would have been discovered as a wasted play session.
2. **Lowering the centre of mass changed nothing** - the retest returned bit-for-bit identical numbers (39.6deg lean, 58.43m travelled). Same trap as the bike's wheelie key earlier today: **a serialized prefab keeps whatever value it was created with, so changing a C# default does nothing to it.** Rebuilding the vehicle prefab dropped corner lean from 39.6deg to 33.9deg. Bit-identical results are a much better smell for this than "the number looks wrong".

**MINI-072 - bike camera and lean**

- **Camera orbit is now LOCKED while riding or driving** (`ThirdPersonFollowCamera.OrbitLocked`). Mouse orbit is ignored and the camera eases in behind the vehicle. Note the user's report was not just a preference: the orbit yaw is absolute, so panning away left the camera pointing off into the world while the bike drove out of shot - following the target's heading is the fix, not clamping the pan.
- **Rider lean capped at 15 degrees**, the user's own figure. Implemented as a CLAMP rather than a smaller follow factor, so the rider still tracks the bike one-for-one through ordinary cornering and only stops short of the extremes that read as him falling off.
- Files changed: `Scripts/Camera/ThirdPersonFollowCamera.cs`, `Scripts/Vehicles/BikeInteractable.cs`, `Scripts/Vehicles/VehicleSpawnController.cs`, `Editor/Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`, `Editor/Mini065TmaxValidation.cs`, `Data/Shop/tmax_560.asset`; new `Scripts/Vehicles/CarController.cs`, `Scripts/Vehicles/CarInteractable.cs`, `Editor/Mini071RoverVehiclePrep.cs`, `Editor/Mini071RoverDriveTest.cs`; new asset `Art/Vehicles/RangeRover_Vehicle.prefab`.
- Verification: `Mini071RoverDriveTest` **PASS** - settles level (tilt 0.0deg), 58.4m in 5s reaching 83km/h, steers 116deg with worst corner lean 33.9deg, stops dead under braking. All measured from the Rigidbody, never the input. `Mini065TmaxDropTest` **PASS** (the bike is unregressed). `Mini069`, `Mini070`, `Mini065` validations **PASS**.
- Known issues / deliberate limits:
  - **The visible wheels do not spin or steer.** The source is one merged mesh (`geometry_0`) so they cannot be isolated. Splitting them out is real modelling work, out of scope for "minimal".
  - Corner lean of 33.9deg is at full lock from ~80km/h. It recovers and does not roll, but it is on the dramatic side for an SUV.
  - The car has no save/respawn home the way the bike does (MINI-068) - park it somewhere odd and it stays there.
  - The seated pose is the bike's sitting clip. Fine through tinted glass, not correct.
- Next action: user plays the EXE, walks to the Rover parked beside the bike at the farm safehouse, presses F at the driver's door, and drives - then checks the bike's camera no longer pans and the rider's lean is subtle.


### MINI-073/074 - large mixed batch + radio + a mission tail. Mostly DONE - see "NOT DONE" below for what remains.

- Date: 2026-08-19
- Owner: released
- **IMPORTANT FOR WHOEVER PICKS THIS UP (the user said Codex continues tomorrow): this was one very large, multi-part request handled in a single pass under a tightening token budget. Everything below marked DONE was compiled, scene-rebuilt, and validated. Everything under NOT DONE is genuinely not started - do not assume partial progress exists on those unless you find code for it.**

**The full original request, verbatim, for reference (it arrived in several follow-up messages, not one message):**
1. Make the dialogue/interaction feedback box smaller and fade away faster, without needing to press E - just walking away should fade it. (Later clarified: "the dialogue box should fade as walk motion is done" - i.e. tied to distance walked, not a flat timer.)
2. The mission side panel doesn't show full info - fix that.
3. More unlocks, in a better sequence matching the game's plot.
4. A second rider on the back of the bike (pillion).
5. Recruited gang members should be able to ride in the vehicle (Range Rover).
6. Missions for Normy, for the Boatman, for "your gang" (Not Ah Word), for the Rastaman, for all shopkeepers.
7. Rename Chevy -> Zoomy. Gang members should be "Deluxe and Draco" (replacing existing names). Later: "change skeng to Rio".
8. Rasta mentor gets the nickname "Rasta" and a liberation shirt instead of his current look.
9. Two-story house with a garage for a vehicle, in Lalay - positioned so it doesn't clash with existing buildings, "for sale", keeping the same "rest" features the Montine farm safehouse has.
10. "start from easy and go to hard" (difficulty/sequencing direction for the mission content).
11. "check the tokens to see if you can finish if not do up to a certain point and save, document and build exe" - explicit permission to triage.
12. Mid-task follow-ups: "if you have to make the bike bigger to accommodate for the pillion rider that is still fine"; "use reference images for that to help" (pillion spacing - answered via a web search on real pillion ergonomics, not an image tool); "you should know the vehicle size like the range rover for the garage size and dont forget the spawning of the vehicles when bought"; "you can also call gang on phone to come on your range as well"; "make sure the phramacy items be able to store in your inventory and able to use after even food as well let it impact the character in some way meaningfully and reasonably"; "you can have a main button for crops and suboptions same for pharmacy and food and then we will use that for the weapons later".

---

## DONE (compiled, scene rebuilt, validated)

**1. Interaction feedback box (`InteractionDetector.cs`)**
- Box shrunk 900x130 -> 620x92, font 26 -> 20.
- Hold time cut from 3-9s to 1.4-4.5s.
- **Fades by WALKED DISTANCE, not a flat timer**, per the user's explicit follow-up correction. `_feedbackHoldEndPos` is captured the instant the reading-hold ends (via E-dismiss, walking out of range, or natural timeout - three call sites, all updated), and `FeedbackAlpha()` computes `1 - distanceWalkedSince / feedbackFadeWalkDistance` (2.2m to fully fade). A small time-based ease (`feedbackFadeSeconds`, blended in via `Mathf.Min`) is kept ONLY as a backstop so a player who never moves still eventually loses the box - motion is the primary driver, time is the fallback.
- The box's TEXT is no longer nulled instantly on walk-away/E-dismiss (an earlier version of this same fix did that and broke the fade entirely - the box would just vanish since alpha calc had nothing to render). Worth knowing if this regresses.

**2. Mission objective card (`MissionHUD.cs`)**
- Was a FIXED 430x130 box with `Text.verticalOverflow` at its UGUI default (`Truncate`) - longer objectives (title + wrapped instruction + distance line) were silently cut off with no visual sign anything was missing. That was the "doesnt give the full info" bug.
- Fixed by measuring the text's own preferred height every frame (`Text.cachedTextGeneratorForLayout.GetPreferredHeight`, the same technique a `ContentSizeFitter` uses internally) and growing `objectivePanelRect.sizeDelta.y` to fit. New serialized fields: `objectivePanelRect`, `objectivePanelMinHeight` (90), `objectivePanelPadding` (24), wired to the panel's own RectTransform in `Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs` (`mhSo.FindProperty("objectivePanelRect").objectReferenceValue = opRect;`). Caught the wiring being missing partway through this same pass and fixed it before shipping, rather than leaving it inert - worth knowing that pattern (write a field, forget to wire it in the Editor-build code) as a recurring risk in this codebase's SerializedObject-heavy setup files.

**3. Renames**
- Gang roster (`Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`, `BuildNotAhWordRoster`): Chevy -> Zoomy, Reds -> Deluxe, Ju -> Draco, Skeng -> Rio. GameObject names changed too (`NotAhWord_Zoomy` etc.), which broke two stale references in `Mini058FactionsValidation.cs` (`NotAhWord_Chevy`, `NotAhWord_Reds`) - **found and fixed as part of this pass**, validation re-run and PASSES. One cosmetic leftover: that validation's own PASS log message text still says "Chevy" in a hardcoded string (line ~99) - functionally harmless, just an outdated word in a debug log, not fixed (ran out of priority for it).
- Rasta Mentor -> "Rasta" (his `npcName` field and all 5 dialogue `speaker` fields). His prompt label is unaffected (uses the generic `[ E ] Talk` fallback for `NpcRole.Villager` - I deliberately did NOT special-case his prompt text to avoid touching the shared Villager default, which other NPCs like the Dog Life pool also use).
- Rasta's shirt recoloured green ("liberation shirt") via a new `ApplyLiberationShirt(GameObject)` helper in `Mini011PhaseBSetup.cs`, same keyword-match-and-clone-material technique as the existing `ApplyGangTint`/`ApplyPoliceUniform`.
- **TNAX/Range Rova naming and pricing from earlier this session are also part of the same shop-list edits** (documented in the MINI-071/072 entry above this one) - not re-documented here, just noting they're adjacent.

**4. Bike pillion (`BikeInteractable.cs`)**
- The OTHER main character (not the one driving) automatically boards the pillion seat when the driver mounts, if within `pillionBoardRadius` (8m) and not locked away on the Guadeloupe run. `BoardPillion()`/`DismountPillion()`, called from the existing mount/dismount paths.
- Deliberately reuses `VehicleRider.Mount()`/`Dismount()` as-is rather than re-implementing pose handling - `Mount()` already plays whatever `RidePoseActionId` the seat carries, and the pillion seat was ALREADY wired (back in MINI-066) to `"RidePillion"` with its own grab/foot IK targets (`PillionGrabLeft/Right`, `PillionFootLeft/Right`) - that wiring existed but was never actually exercised until now ("pillion built but unridden" per the MINI-066 handoff entry).
- **Verified the existing seat SPACING is reasonable** (not just assumed): wrote a throwaway Editor script to read `Seat`/`PillionSeat` marker local positions directly off the prefab (0.33m local Z separation x 1.15 bike scale = ~0.38m world), cross-checked against a web search on real pillion ergonomics ("passenger should sit ... as close to the rider as possible"). 0.38m is in a reasonable range - **the bike was NOT resized**, even though the user explicitly said resizing was fine if needed. This has NOT been confirmed by an actual Play Mode look - only by the numbers. If it looks cramped in person, the user already pre-approved scaling the bike up; that would mean revisiting `Mini064TmaxAssetPrep.BikeScale` or the seat marker Z-offsets.
- **NOT verified**: whether the pillion rider's pose actually looks right through a wheelie, whether he interferes with the driver's own animation, whether mounting order (driver mounts, THEN pillion auto-boards, always in that order) ever races oddly. This whole pillion path has never been seen running - MINI-001's standing limitation applies (Play Mode does not tick in this batch environment), so this is compiled-and-wired-correct, not seen-in-motion-correct.

**5. Car passengers (`CarInteractable.cs`, `Mini071RoverVehiclePrep.cs`)**
- Three passenger seat markers added to the Range Rover prefab (`Seat_FrontPassenger`, `Seat_RearLeft`, `Seat_RearRight`), mirrored off the driver seat's own offsets.
- `BoardNearbyRecruits()` (called on driver Enter) and the new `FillEmptySeats()` (public, safely re-callable) seat any recruited-and-Following member within `boardRadius` (9m) into an empty seat. **Refactored mid-task**: the first version cleared the passenger list unconditionally on every call, which would have orphaned already-seated members (stuck riding forever, `IsRiding` never cleared) the moment it was called a second time - caught before it shipped, not left as a latent bug, but worth knowing the pattern if you see similar boarding code elsewhere.
